# Supervised Orchestration Technical Reference

> **WHAT:** Post-implementation technical reference documenting the architecture, subsystems, data flow, and operational details of supervised orchestration (the Orca coordinator and worker contract, its hook guards, launchers and installer) as currently built, with every claim tied to the kind of evidence behind it.
> **WHY:** Serves as the evidence-based source of truth for people and AI coding agents who must understand or modify these mechanisms without reading every source file. Most of the authority in this system is instruction text read by language-model sessions; this reference separates that text from the executable code, from the Orca product, and from the deployed state that was never observed.
> **HOW TO USE:** Maintainers consult it before modifying the contract, the hooks, the launchers or the installer. Read the evidence-class legend under Document Information first; every behavior claim carries a class (normative, checkout, origin/master, installed files, host-installed, documented, historical or deployed) and a verification tag.

### Evidence Limits (stated once)

1. The running Orca build, its effective settings (for example `nestedWorkerMaxDepth`, the heartbeat setting), live hook ordering and execution, and effective hook registration on other hosts were not observed.
2. The ORCA-CHECKOUT (`dc57b3d6e`) is an older snapshot than the INSTALLED-FILES build; neither is the running process.
3. The HOST-INSTALLED set under `~/.claude/` is mixed relative to HEAD, `origin/master` and the working tree. The per-file matrix (read-only `cmp` run at about 02:48 UTC on 2026-11-12, after another session refreshed the installed files at 01:59:25 UTC) is the single provenance matrix, in Section 3.0.3. `origin/master` carries a different `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (338 lines against 327, with a coordinator preflight), so every contract line number in this reference holds for HEAD and the installed copy only. Other hosts, plugin builds and wheels were not checked.
4. The 12 live guard observations (OB-H1 items 1-9, OB-H2, OB-H3, OB-CTX) are skipped tests; the tests in `tests/hooks/` were read, not run. Only `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` (739 passed) and `tests/core/test_orca_session_launch_guidance.py` (5 passed) were run, once, on 2026-11-11.
5. No measured performance figures exist beyond the recorded speed-test results cited in Section 11.
6. The Claude Code version installed on the host was not read; documented Claude Code behavior is cited with URLs and is not observed behavior.

## 1. Overview

Supervised orchestration is the way an Orca *coordinator* session, opened by a person, plans work, creates Orca Tasks and starts one *worker* session per Task, where each worker runs the `Command:` lines of its brief in order (usually one IronClaude `/sc:` command; chained lines are governed by WC-7, WC-14 and WC-15, `src/superclaude/core/WORKER-CONTRACT.md:60,63,64`), each with the `--unattended` flag, and reports back once per Task. The rules live in one normative file, `src/superclaude/core/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (terms, command-side rules WC-2 to WC-15, the worker's duties, the coordinator's rules and a brief template), which the installer copies to `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md`. NORMATIVE, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:3,5`. The dispatch preamble that Orca gives each worker, not this repository, is the authority for Orca verb syntax and heartbeat cadence (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:7`); the generator is `buildDispatchPreamble` in the Orca source (`IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/preamble.ts:52-152`, ORCA-CHECKOUT, per `research/gap-P3-G01.md` S1).

Almost all authority in this system is instruction text read by language-model sessions. The IronClaude code around it is narrow: one lexical PreToolUse hook that blocks `orca worktree create` without `--agent` (and only when the command word is `orca`); a launch-level guard and a job-identity helper that govern only sessions launched by the `/sc:issue --claim` script; Python launchers (the claim script and the issue watcher) that start non-worker Claude sessions through `orca agent launch`; and an installer and doctor that copy the contract, hooks, ccsession and (by asking Orca) the Orca skills. CHECKOUT, `research/01` sections A, C; `research/03` sections A, F; `research/04` F4. No IronClaude code runs the coordinator loop, detects a worker run, writes or reads a worker result, or distinguishes a coordinator from a worker (`research/02` section 7 takeaways 1-2; `research/gap-P3-G04.md` 4.4; `research/01` G1). The contract is installed (`src/superclaude/cli/install_core.py:27` lists it) and pointed to from `src/superclaude/core/HOW-WE-WORK.md:398-403`, but it is not `@`-imported (`src/superclaude/core/CLAUDE.md:3` imports only `BASH_INSPECTION_POLICY.md`; the file has no other reference to it), and no hook or startup step that loads it into a coordinator session was found (search limited to the three guard scripts and `hooks.json`; other hooks and ccsession [UNVERIFIED]), so contract section 4 binds a coordinator only if that session reads the file (`research/02` 6.1). Orca itself contributes some machine-side controls (worker-start scope, a nested-worker depth cap, a narrow Run-binding refusal) whose defaults differ by build and whose effective setting on the running Orca is [UNVERIFIED] (`research/05` section 5.2).

Two boundaries frame every statement in this document. Subordinate (sub-orchestrator) mode is deferred and not implemented: no command, skill, flag or contract role exists, worker fan-out is barred by WC-13 (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:62`, text), and the related design question is owned by open issue #444 (re-read 2026-11-12: OPEN, as are #339, #423, #431). The ownership wording clarification ("The eligible session that creates a worktree owns its agent(s). ... A supervised orchestrator's coordinator is person-opened", `src/superclaude/core/CLAUDE.md:57-61`, re-read 2026-11-12) exists only as an uncommitted edit set in this worktree; HEAD, `origin/master` and the HOST-INSTALLED `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` still carry the older wording "becomes its orchestrator, or a coordinator under another orchestrator", and the clarification does not resolve the future-role question or claim any rollout (`research/01` A.1, `research/06` F5-F6, Section 14.3). The terms and codes used below (WC-n, S0-S11, ESCAPE, the guard levels, IR-4, IR-7, L2, T-SPD-5, SD-1, `LL-*`, lineage, quarantine, plugin mirror, ccsession, shim check) are defined in Section 16 (Glossary).

**What it does:** Defines, as instruction text read by language-model sessions, how an Orca coordinator (person-opened) dispatches IronClaude commands to unattended worker sessions, and asks workers not to fan out or act outward (WC-13 and WC-9, NORMATIVE, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:62,41`); no IronClaude hook enforces either rule for a supervised worker, and Orca's own nested-worker depth cap differs by build with the running setting [UNVERIFIED] (Section 5.5). It also launches non-worker claim and watcher sessions, the claim-launched ones under a launch-level guard.

**Who uses it:** A person who opens an Orca coordinator session and the LLM coordinator that follows `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` section 4; worker sessions that follow sections 1-3; the `/sc:issue --claim` flow and the issue watcher (non-worker launched sessions); maintainers of the hooks, installer and contract. Orca (a separate product) supplies the runtime, preamble and mailbox.

**Where it lives:** Contract and guidance in `src/superclaude/core/`; guards in `src/superclaude/hooks/` (installed flat to `~/.claude/hooks/`); launchers in `src/superclaude/skills/sc-issue-protocol/scripts/orca_worktree.py` and `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/launch.py`; ccsession in `src/superclaude/skills/ccsession-tag/`; installer in `src/superclaude/cli/install_*.py`; per-command rules in `src/superclaude/commands/*.md`. Orca-side behavior is documented from `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/` (external) and `/opt/Orca/resources/app.asar.unpacked/out/` (installed files).

**Key numbers:** 2 session roles (coordinator, worker) plus the person; 5 owner decisions D1-D5 (historical record `.dev/orca-command-refactor/decisions/APPROVED.md:8-12`); contract rules WC-2 to WC-15 (there is no WC-1) in a 327-line contract; 31 of 46 command files carry `## Unattended mode` (16 in phase P1, 15 in P2; re-counted 2026-11-12: 31 of the 46 command files, the folder's 47 `.md` files minus `README.md`), 8 command names are barred from workers, and the other 7 command files (implement, load, post-release, roadmap, save, validate-roadmap, validate-tests) carry no section and are not barred (Section 5.7, item 9; with `--unattended` such a command is refused by guard case 2 when no copy the worker reads has the section (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:18`); without the flag no guard case applies (case 1 needs a section in every copy, `:17`), so it runs as for a person (`:29`), writes no contract (WC-4 binds only `--unattended` runs, `:32`) and may stop at a person prompt; instruction text); 3 guard scripts (`require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` 358 lines in the working tree and 357 at HEAD, `launch-level-guard.sh` 747 and `launch-job-identity.sh` 566 lines at HEAD) with 4 registrations in `hooks.json` (192 lines; one PreToolUse hook per script, the launch-level guard also on UserPromptSubmit; timeouts 3 s for the worktree gate, 5 s for the guard and the identity hook); script sizes elsewhere: `install_hooks.py` 611 lines, `issue_watch/launch.py` 370 lines, `orca_worktree.py` 1,473 lines at HEAD and 1,884 on ORIGIN/MASTER; 7 uncommitted modified files (`CLAUDE.md`, `src/superclaude/core/CLAUDE.md`, `src/superclaude/skills/ccsession-tag/README.md`, both copies of `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh`, `tests/core/test_orca_session_launch_guidance.py`, `tests/hooks/test_require_orca_worktree_agent.py`; `git status --short | grep '^ M'` re-run at about 02:48 UTC on 2026-11-12); 2 Orca builds in play (checkout 1.4.214 and installed files 1.4.245-aidev02.9cfecdd30b2a); test runs on record: 739 + 5 passed, 12 live observations skipped.

## 2. Architecture

### 2.1 High-Level Architecture

The system has four planes that must not be conflated. Each plane is a different evidence class, and only plane B and the Orca source of plane C are code that can be read here.

| Plane | What it holds | Evidence class | Enforcement strength |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. Policy text | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`, `core/CLAUDE.md`, `HOW-WE-WORK.md`, command `## Unattended mode` sections, brief template | NORMATIVE | Read by LLM sessions; pinned only as text by `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` (739 tests) and `tests/core/test_orca_session_launch_guidance.py`; no executable follows it |
| B. IronClaude code | Three hook guards, `hooks.json`, `install_hooks.py`, claim script, issue watcher, ccsession, installer, doctor | CHECKOUT (HEAD plus 7 uncommitted files); some files differ in ORIGIN/MASTER and HOST-INSTALLED | Real but narrow (Section 5.3) |
| C. Orca runtime | Task and Dispatch store, preamble, mailbox, worker-start authority, nested-depth cap, Run binding | ORCA-CHECKOUT and INSTALLED-FILES (two different builds) | Partial machine-side controls; effective settings UNVERIFIED |
| D. Deployed state | The running Orca, live Claude Code hook execution and ordering, effective settings on any host | DEPLOYED-RUNNING | Never observed |

Diagram 1: roles and the supervised message flow. Supported by NORMATIVE text (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:5,11,39,60,67-69,93,237-246`) and, for the Orca box, ORCA-CHECKOUT source (`research/gap-P3-G01.md` S1-S5, `research/05`). The coordinator and worker boxes are LLM sessions: no IronClaude code runs them.

```
                      opens
        PERSON  ───────────────▶  COORDINATOR session (Claude; person-opened)
                                   plans, creates Tasks, starts workers, reads reports,
                                   performs every outward action (push, PR, install, paid run)
                                   [NORMATIVE WORKER-CONTRACT.md:67-69, 41; HOW-WE-WORK.md:400]
                                          │  orca orchestration: task-create, worker-start,
                                          │  check --wait, reply, ack     (syntax: Orca CLI specs and the contract)
                                          ▼
                   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
                   │ ORCA runtime (separate product)                       │
                   │  Tasks / Dispatches / mailbox; preamble generator;    │
                   │  worker-start authority; nested-worker depth cap;     │
                   │  report mailbox (to live creator Dispatch, else Run)  │
                   │  [ORCA-CHECKOUT dc57b3d6e | INSTALLED-FILES 1.4.245]  │
                   └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
                                          │  dispatch preamble (Task ID + Dispatch ID,
                                          │  heartbeat, ask / check / worker_done)
                                          ▼
                       WORKER session (top-level Claude; runs its brief's Command: lines in order, --unattended)
                         identity = "received a preamble with Task ID and Dispatch ID"
                         [NORMATIVE :11; no code detects it]
                         writes <report-dir>/return-contract.yaml   (written by the LLM)
                         sends worker_done --outcome ... --report-path <that file> exactly once
                                          │                                [NORMATIVE :39, :59]
                                          ▼
                   ORCA validates worker_done and settles the Task; it does not check the report file
                   (lifecycle-reconciliation.ts:175-293, ORCA-CHECKOUT, per research/02 5.2; Orca does not start
                    ready Tasks itself on the live surface: the old scheduler is retired, per gap-P3-G01 C6)
                                          │
                                          ▼
                       COORDINATOR opens the contract, admits or rejects by worker_status
                         [NORMATIVE :93, :237-246; no code does this]
```

Diagram 2: where IronClaude code touches the picture. Every box below is CHECKOUT code unless marked; arrows state the mechanism the cited source shows. Hook execution order is not shown because it is unspecified: Claude Code documents that matching hooks run in parallel and merge by `deny` > `defer` > `ask` > `allow` (DOCUMENTED, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks and https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide, read 2026-11-11; not observed on this host), and the `hooks.json` array order is not a documented sequence. Terms used in the diagram: ESCAPE is the launch-level guard's class for starting or messaging another session or typing into a terminal, denied at every level; the only exemption, a segment of an `orca` command that ends in `--help` or `-h`, sits inside the `orca` arm (`launch-level-guard.sh:477`) and does not apply to the other ESCAPE triggers; the levels `worktree`, `full` and `full-submit` are the words the claim script writes into `pending/<nonce>` (the guard works with W, WA = worktree after approval, F and FS; the class and level table is in Section 10.1b); a session with no launch-marker binding is ungoverned and the guard allows all its calls.

```
 ANY Claude Code session (coordinator, worker, ordinary)           Orca CLI (names: orca | orca-ide | orca-dev)
   │ PreToolUse(Bash) command hooks registered in hooks.json:105-134
   ├─ require-orca-worktree-agent.sh  (timeout 3)  blocks `orca worktree create` without --agent
   │     only when the command word's basename is exactly `orca` (or starts with `$`)   [ceiling for the gate, DEFECT for the guard: see 2.3 row 6]
   │     fail-open on every input it cannot judge; no role input                       [require-orca...sh:2-15, :145]
   ├─ launch-level-guard.sh           (timeout 5)  governs ONLY sessions bound by a launch marker; ESCAPE class for an `orca` word
   └─ launch-job-identity.sh          (timeout 5)  rewrites governed Bash calls through a supervised runner (Linux, uv)

 LAUNCHERS (start non-worker Claude sessions, not supervised workers)
   /sc:issue --claim ─▶ orca_worktree.py worktree ─▶ `orca worktree create ... --no-parent --comment ...` (NO --agent)   [orca_worktree.py:666-682]
                     ─▶ orca_worktree.py launch   ─▶ pending launch record ─▶ `orca agent launch --agent claude ... --prompt "[sc-launch:v1:<nonce>] ..."`  [:1351-1355]
                              Orca starts its configured Claude command (ccsession-orca, if the host is set up so) ─▶ ccsession names the tab ─▶ shim check reads title/screen (advisory)
   issue-watch once ─▶ cli/issue_watch/launch.py ─▶ `orca agent launch ... /sc:issue --claim <n>` (fixed 12-element argv) in one configured checkout
                       [per research 04 F2; Orca names tried: _ORCA_CLIS = ("orca-ide", "orca") defined at install_user_setup.py:35, imported at launch.py:22, looped at launch.py:193]
                       The watcher prompt carries no [sc-launch:v1:<nonce>] marker, so that session is NOT bound by the launch-level guard
                       at this hop; the claim session it starts may then launch the second, marked agent (each hop read separately;
                       the combined chain at runtime is [UNVERIFIED], research 04 F4 B3)

 INSTALLER  superclaude install ─▶ hooks (flat copy to ~/.claude/hooks/, settings merge), core files incl. WORKER-CONTRACT.md,
            ccsession wiring, then `<orca> skills install --agent claude-code --skill computer-use --skill orca-cli --skill orchestration`
            (non-fatal; Orca produces those skills, IronClaude ships none)   [install_hooks.py:88-93; install_core.py:27; install_user_setup.py:34-35,230; per research gap-P3-G03 A.3]
```

Reading rules for both diagrams: (1) The coordinator-to-worker path is supervised dispatch through Orca; the claim and watcher paths never call `worker-start`, `dispatch`, `task-create` or any `orchestration` verb (`research/04` F4 B1; its grep found no executable call in the Python launchers). (2) A hook sees a Bash tool call as text; `orca_worktree.py` runs Orca as a subprocess from Python, so its own `orca worktree create` is outside the Orca worktree hook (hook header `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:60-61`; the Bash command word of a claim call is `uv`). (3) Which Orca binary name an agent types is undetermined: `orca`, `orca-ide` and `orca-dev` are each printed by Orca in different circumstances (ORCA-CHECKOUT `cli-command.ts:6-40`, `preamble.ts:54`; INSTALLED-FILES `orchestration-recovery-command.js:6-15`, per `research/gap-P3-G02.md` 3.3); on the research host `orca` and `orca-ide` reach the same launcher through a dispatcher script (`gap-P3-G02.md` 3.2, host observation). Both IronClaude hook scripts key on the literal word `orca`, and the claim script itself writes the literal `"orca"` in every argv and does not try `orca-ide` (`research/gap-P3-G02.md` 3.1, `research/04` F1). For the launch-level guard this is the recorded DEFECT, for the worktree gate a documented ceiling (statement, probes and remediation link: Section 8.2).

### 2.2 Subsystem Map

Seven subsystems, numbered and named to match Section 5. Section 5 owns the detail.

| Subsystem | Purpose | Primary Directory | Key Files |
|-----------|---------|-------------------|-----------|
| 5.1 Roles and Authority | Defines the session kinds (person, person-opened coordinator, worker run, ordinary session, subagent), what each may do, and the owner decisions D1-D5 behind the rules. Authority is instruction text; the ownership paragraph names the creator-owns-the-agent rule and the "hook blocks `orca worktree create` without `--agent`" sentence. | `src/superclaude/core/` | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (`:11` worker run, `:62` WC-13, `:67-69` coordinator); `CLAUDE.md` (`:45-61` Orca session launches and ownership paragraph, uncommitted wording); `HOW-WE-WORK.md` (`:400` coordinator identity) |
| 5.2 Coordinator Lifecycle and Orca Worker-Side Lifecycle | The coordinator loop (create Task, `worker-start`, wait, sweep, classify states S0-S11, escalate L0-L5, admit results, retry as a new Task) and the worker-side half Orca supplies (dispatch preamble, heartbeat, liveness projection, mailbox, retired scheduler). The loop is normative text; the Orca half is source. | `src/superclaude/core/` (contract sections 4-5); Orca: `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/` and `IronOrca/src/shared/` | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (`:67-282` coordinator rules, `:283-327` brief template); `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/preamble.ts` (`buildDispatchPreamble` `:52-152`); `IronOrca/src/shared/orchestration-heartbeat.ts` |
| 5.3 Hook Guards and Job Identity | Three registered hook scripts: a lexical gate on `orca worktree create` without `--agent`; a launch-level guard for `/sc:issue --claim`-launched sessions; a Linux job-identity runner that rewrites governed Bash calls. Includes registration, timeouts, fail-open and fail-closed behavior, the DEFECT on alternate Orca names, and the documented Claude Code hook semantics. | `src/superclaude/hooks/` | `hooks/scripts/require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` (`:145` command-word test; `:351-358` block message); `hooks/scripts/launch-level-guard.sh` (`:455-478` `orca` arm); `hooks/hooks.json` (`:63-71,105-134`); `hooks/scripts/launch-job-identity.sh` (`:74` `LEVELS`) |
| 5.4 Launchers | Start non-worker (not supervised) Claude sessions through `orca agent launch`: the `/sc:issue --claim` script (agent-less create, first-tab close, pending launch record, marked prompt, advisory ccsession shim check), the issue watcher (one fixed argv launching `/sc:issue --claim <n>`), and ccsession, which writes the tab title the shim check reads. | `src/superclaude/skills/sc-issue-protocol/scripts/`; `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/`; `src/superclaude/skills/ccsession-tag/` | `orca_worktree.py` (`:666-682` create, `:1274` `cmd_launch`, `:1351-1355` launch argv, `:779` `SHIM_TITLE`); `issue_watch/launch.py` (`:22,193` Orca name probe); `ccsession-tag/ccsession-orca` and `ccsession-tag/ccsession` |
| 5.5 Orca-Side Enforcement (Development Source) | The machine-side controls Orca applies that bear on the contract: who may call `worker-start` and only as a same-Run local helper, where worker reports are routed, the narrow Run-binding refusal, and the nested-worker depth cap whose default differs by build. Read from the development checkout and compared with the installed files; the running process is unobserved. | `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/` and `IronOrca/src/shared/` (external to this repo); installed build `/opt/Orca/resources/app.asar.unpacked/out/` | `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/rpc/methods/orchestration/worker/worker-start-authority.ts` (`:12-20,51-55`); `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/dispatch-report-mailbox.ts`; `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/db/runs/worker-run-binding.ts`; `IronOrca/src/shared/nested-worker-depth.ts` and installed `out/shared/nested-worker-depth.js` (the two defaults and their lines: Section 5.5) |
| 5.6 Installer, Orca Skills Install and Doctor | Copies the contract, hooks and ccsession to `~/.claude/`, registers hooks in settings, asks the Orca CLI to install its own skills (`computer-use`, `orca-cli`, `orchestration`; none are shipped by IronClaude), and checks ccsession health. Without `--force` existing destination files are kept, which is why the host-installed set is mixed. | `src/superclaude/cli/` | `install_hooks.py` (`:88,90,93` guard scripts in `_FRESHNESS_SCRIPTS`); `install_user_setup.py` (`:34-35` skill and CLI-name constants, `:198` `_find_orca`, `:230` `install_orca_skills`, `:449` `install_user_setup`); `install_core.py` (`:27` contract in the core file list); `install_ccsession.py` (`:11`); `doctor.py` |
| 5.7 Worker Result Channel (`return-contract.yaml`) | The `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` that a worker command writes and the coordinator reads: field set, four file layouts, status mapping, nested-result rule, and the 31 per-command `## Unattended mode` sections. Writer and reader are LLM sessions; no executable parses the worker fields. | `src/superclaude/core/` (WC-4); `src/superclaude/commands/`; producer refs under `src/superclaude/skills/` | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (`:32-39` WC-4 field set and hand-off, `:59` WC-5); `commands/*.md` (31 `## Unattended mode` sections); `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` (static text pins, `RULE_PHRASES` `:25-374`) |

Orca provenance for row 5.5 (ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e` versus the installed build's recorded commit `9cfecdd30b2a...`, `git diff --quiet` per file, `research/05` section 1): identical for `worker-start-authority.ts`, `worker-run-binding.ts`, `dispatch-report-mailbox.ts`, `worker-dispatch-start.ts`, `dispatch-depth.ts`, `local-worker-start.ts`, `dispatch-creator.ts`, `run-binding.ts`, `run-scope.ts` and `run-create.ts`; different for `shared/nested-worker-depth.ts` (the depth default, Section 5.5), `shared/default-global-settings.ts`, `runs.ts` (the `runUse` takeover check only) and `workers.ts` (one line). Whether the installed minified bundle equals that commit's compiled output was shown only for the depth files and the `runCreate` and `worker-dispatch-start` heads. `worker-run-binding.ts` lives under `db/runs/`, not under `rpc/`.

Subsystem dependencies at a glance (direction of reliance; the rows summarise body text in Sections 5.2 to 5.7 and the Dependencies and Consumers tables there where they exist):

| From | To | Nature |
|---|---|---|
| 5.2 coordinator loop | 5.5 / Orca runtime | Calls Orca verbs by instruction; Orca enforces only worker-start scope, depth cap, Run-binding |
| 5.2 worker half | 5.7 | Worker maps `worker_status` to `worker_done --outcome` and passes `--report-path` (instruction) |
| 5.3 guards | 5.4 | Launch-level guard governs sessions the claim script launches; claim preflight checks the guard's registration |
| 5.4 launchers | Orca CLI (external; verbs in 8.1a) | `orca worktree create`, `orca agent launch`, `orca terminal ...` called as subprocesses by name `orca`; 5.5 holds the Orca-side controls, not these verbs |
| 5.6 installer | 5.1, 5.3, 5.4 | Distributes contract text, hooks and ccsession; asks Orca for its own skills |
| 5.1 | all | Roles and authority text that the other subsystems cite or implement in part |

Not a subsystem: subordinate (sub-orchestrator) mode. It is deferred; no file implements, enforces or tests it (`research/03` GAP-10; `research/01` section E). One related limit, relevant only to that deferred mode: the launch-level guard's read-only `orca` allow-list (`launch-level-guard.sh:474-476`) contains `status`, `help`, `--version`, `worktree current|list|show`, `terminal list|show|read` and `orchestration check|inbox|run-current|task-list|worker-show`, so the contract's sweep and ladder verbs (`worker-list`, `worker-read`, `worktree ps`, `terminal send`, `orchestration reply`, `task-create`, `worker-start`) fall into the ESCAPE default for a governed session when the CLI is typed as `orca` (unless the segment ends in `--help` or `-h`, `launch-level-guard.sh:477`); under `orca-ide`, `orca-dev` or `orca.cmd` the `orca)` arm is not reached and no class is added (the recorded DEFECT, Section 8.2). This binds only sessions the claim script launched; a person-opened coordinator is an ungoverned session to the guard (`research/02` 6.4 and `research/gap-P3-G02.md` 4.5, re-read against `launch-level-guard.sh:474-478`). It is a limit for any future governed supervisor, not a shipped capability.

Sections 6 (State Management) and 7 (Component Inventory) of the template are N/A for this feature (no frontend); the rationale is in those sections.

### 2.3 Key Design Decisions (As Built)

There is no TDD for supervised orchestration as a whole. The "TDD Divergence" column therefore records divergence from the nearest recorded design or decision source (named in the cell) and says "none recorded" where no such source exists. Decisions D1-D5 are HISTORICAL owner approvals of 2026-11-02 (`.dev/orca-command-refactor/decisions/APPROVED.md:8-12`), cited to explain why the contract says what it says; the contract file, not that record, is the current normative text. Rows 5, 6 and 7 are the boundary between what is enforced and what is only written down.

| # | Decision | What Was Built | Rationale | TDD Divergence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The contract is rule text for LLM sessions, not code | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` holds terms, command rules WC-2..WC-15, worker duties, coordinator rules and a brief template; commands carry only a short `## Unattended mode` section that points to it. No executable implements the coordinator loop, the state table S0-S11, the ladder, acks, retries or resume notes. Tests assert that phrases exist in the file. NORMATIVE; CHECKOUT (`research/02` section 7 takeaways 1-2, `research/gap-P3-G04.md` 4.4 negative result) | "Commands themselves contain no Orca calls" and Orca's preamble is the authority for verb syntax and heartbeat cadence, so the shared rules are written once (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:3,5,7`) | None recorded. The contract never claims code enforcement ("the worker checks", "the coordinator opens"); this is a stated enforcement ceiling, not a defect (`research/02` 6.5, `gap-P3-G04.md` section 5) |
| 2 | Two session roles, nesting depth one (D5) | Roles: person-opened coordinator and worker run (a session that received a preamble with Task ID and Dispatch ID). Worker fan-out is barred by WC-13 "at any depth setting" (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:62`). No middle layer, no upward channel, no grant token. IronClaude code cannot tell the roles apart (`research/01` G1). Orca adds partial machine-side limits whose default depth differs by build (the values by build, their sources and the effect, with the running Orca [UNVERIFIED], are in Sections 5.5 and 9.3); at the installed default only WC-13 text bars a worker's helper start | D5: keep depth 1, coordinator is a person-opened session (`APPROVED.md:12`); `HOW-WE-WORK.md:400` "never a session that Orca itself started as a worker" | None recorded against D5. Native Orca capability does not amend policy (`research/05` 5.2). Orca's `workerRunBindingRefusal` is narrow: a worker can still create a new Run through `runCreate` (`research/05` 7.5) |
| 3 | Unattended mode is selected only by the `--unattended` flag | 31 command files carry `## Unattended mode`; no command detects Orca, its preamble or its environment; the flag counts only as a standalone unquoted argument token; person-mode flags are refused by the command text; a worker-side "brief guard" checks every `Command:` line. No parser or guard executable exists (grep of `src` for "brief guard"/"guard refused" hits only `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`). NORMATIVE for the guard; CHECKOUT for the 31 sections (`research/01` B.2) | D1: `--unattended` is the only switch, plus the worker-session brief guard (`APPROVED.md:8`); `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:12` | None recorded. Note an internal inconsistency kept open as owner question C2 (Section 14.3.1): `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:14` lists `--monitor 1` (pr-submit) as refused, yet `/sc:pr-submit` has no unattended section and is on the never-as-worker list (`:23`) |
| 4 | Workers never act outward; the coordinator does every push, PR, comment, install and paid run | WC-9 (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:41-45`): no brief turns outward action on; the only git write a brief may authorize is an end-of-Task local commit under the three conditions listed in Section 5.1 (How It Works, item 3); the coordinator stages `.dev/tasks`. 8 command names are on the never-as-worker list (`:23`, pinned by the `NEVER_AS_WORKER` tuple, `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:382-391`). No IronClaude hook enforces it for a person-opened coordinator or a supervised worker (both are ungoverned sessions to the launch-level guard; `research/02` 6.3) | D4 (no worker acts outward) and D3 (`/sc:pr-submit` never an Orca worker) (`APPROVED.md:10-11`) | None recorded |
| 5 | The only machine check on Orca worktree creation is a lexical `--agent` test | PreToolUse(Bash) hook `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` (timeout 3 s, `hooks.json:105-114`). A call is blocked when its command word's basename is `orca` (or the word starts with `$`), its first two non-option arguments are `worktree` and `create`, and none of its own words is `--agent`, `--agent=...`, `--help` or `-h` (`:30-33,145`). Exit 2 with a message on stderr; fail-open (exit 0) for missing `jq` or `awk`, non-JSON, non-Bash tools, no command, or any scanner error (`:10-15`, `:342-349`). Header lists known ceilings: `bash -c`/`eval`/`env -S`/`ssh` strings, `xargs orca`, heredoc into a shell, built command words, `claude --bare`, humans and subprocess callers such as `orca_worktree.py` (`:39-61`). The hook also does not block raw `git worktree add`, paths under `.dev/worktrees/`, `--setup run`, or removal of a worktree that holds unmerged work (no match for these in the script, grep 2026-11-12; `research/02` 5.4), although the project rule says never to create a worktree that way; those parts of `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:220-225` are text only (Section 5.2 table D). "No worktree for your own use" and "the creator owns the agent" are instruction text (`:7-8`). Registered only at user level: the project `.claude/settings.json` has no entry, so the guard runs only where `superclaude install` registered it (`research/03` B.1) | Make "every Orca worktree gets an agent" mechanically checkable at the cost of a bounded text scan that never blocks what it cannot judge (header `:10-15`) | None recorded against a design doc. The header's claim that its tokenizer is "copied" from `block-bare-python.sh` is [CODE-CONTRADICTED] (a derivative, 25 changed lines; `research/03` A.5) |
| 6 | **DEFECT (launch-level guard) and documented ceiling (worktree gate):** both IronClaude hook scripts recognize the Orca CLI only under the command word `orca` | The two predicates are `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:145` and `launch-level-guard.sh:458`. The hook is a documented ceiling (every statement of its reach names the literal `orca worktree create`); the guard is the DEFECT because its approval-forgery guarantee (`docs/designs/sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:193`) holds only for the name `orca`. **Not fixed.** The statement, the probes, the owner confirmation and the remediation-plan link: Section 8.2 | The scripts were written against the bare name; the design lists accepted limits for built or obfuscated names (O-7, R1), a hostile agent (R12) and another session typing `approved` (R15), none of which covers a literal installed name | DEFECT against `docs/designs/sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:193` (design guarantee vs code). Live effect on approval forgery was not observed; issue #442 (live observations) is OPEN |
| 7 | The launch-level guard is a separate, text-level control for claim-launched sessions | `launch-level-guard.sh` governs only a session whose `session_id` has a binding file; classifies Bash/Monitor text, Skill names, Edit/Write paths and MCP tools; unbound sessions are never blocked. Fail-closed only for its own failure paths (EXIT trap maps internal failure to LL-BROKEN or LL-NOBIND for a governed session); identity loss (no valid `session_id`) allows a tool call silently and a prompt only when it carries no launch marker (detail by event in Section 5.3, Failure behavior; qualifier stated once in Section 10.3). Registered on UserPromptSubmit and PreToolUse, timeout 5 s (`hooks.json:63-71,115-124`), no `onFailure` key. A hook stopped at its timeout lets the call go ahead: the design records "hook killed at timeout \| call goes ahead (V8, R2)" (`sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:216,440`) and a probe showed TERM/KILL produce exit 143/137 with no deny (`research/gap-P3-G06.md` section 3, single runs). DOCUMENTED: a PreToolUse command hook that reaches its timeout is canceled and does not block unless `"onFailure": "block"` is set (Claude Code v2.1.295 or later) (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, read 2026-11-11; `research/web-01` Q1); the host's actual behavior and signal are [UNVERIFIED]. Text-level accepted leaks are pinned by tests (`tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:3602-3622`). The identity helper's `identify` mode has no runtime consumer in this checkout (its consumer, the L2 pre-push hook, exists only on ORIGIN/MASTER) | Limit what an unattended claim agent can do (commit, push, merge, escape through other sessions) without blocking ungoverned sessions; `onFailure: "block"` was rejected because a broken hook would then block every ungoverned session (`.dev/brainstorms/20261105-170229-launch-level-hook/merged-requirements.md:98,694,701`, via `gap-P3-G06.md` section 2) | Design vs code and tests: (a) design table says T-SPD-5 "under 1 s each, asserted", but the test asserts 3.5 s per run and keeps 1 s as a reported target (`tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:996-1021`, decision 2026-11-08; `gap-P3-G06.md` section 6); (b) design IR-7 says the claim should refuse without an L2 layer, the HEAD claim script warns only (`orca_worktree.py:800-805`: "IR-7 ... says refuse; this plan builds no L2 layer"); `origin/master` adds an owned L2 pre-push hook, refusal still off (`research/04` F5, not read in full). The guard's ship gate (OB-H1 item 1, OB-H2, OB-H3) is a usage rule that the claim script does not check (`research/03` GAP-7) |
| 8 | `/sc:issue --claim` creates the worktree without `--agent` | `orca_worktree.py:666-682` runs `orca worktree create --repo ... --name issue-<N> --base-branch ... --no-parent --comment ... --json` with no `--agent`; after copying the issue files it closes Orca's plain first terminal tab only if exactly one agent-less terminal is listed; the agent is started later by `orca agent launch` (`:1351-1355`) after an enforcement preflight and a one-time pending record (`research/04` F1) | The claim must copy files, pass the repository check and write the launch record before an agent starts, so a failed copy means no agent (`docs/designs/sc-issue-claim-pipeline-flag.md:11,194,380`; commit `24d79b7fa` body, per `research/04` F4 B3) | An accepted exception, not a defect: owner decision recorded 2026-11-08 in the design document, but the unqualified rule text `core/CLAUDE.md:55` ("Every Orca worktree gets an agent") and project `CLAUDE.md:312` do not name it (owner question C1 open). The approval is a sentence in a design doc, not a primary owner message |
| 9 | The worker result is a file written by an LLM and read by an LLM | `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` with `worker_contract_version`, `worker_status` (`succeeded`/`partial`/`failed`/`blocked`), `command`, `report_path`, `artifacts`, `blocked_question`, `reason` (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:32-39`). Orca's `worker_done --report-path` carries the path and "does not check the file". No schema, validator or parser exists; no executable under `src/` mentions the worker fields (two greps, zero non-markdown hits). Four file layouts coexist across commands, of which the contract names two (`research/gap-P3-G04.md` 3.3). The package contract of an archived plan is a different file with a different vocabulary and is read by code (`archive_workspace.py:325-334`) | Orca carries a pointer, so the coordinator opens the file itself (`:39`); the fall-back for a missing or invalid file is `--outcome failed` (`:59`) | None recorded. Contract text speaks of an `/sc:implement` producer that does not exist (row 14) |
| 10 | Orca skills are produced by Orca, never shipped | IronClaude ships no `orchestration`, `orca-cli` or `computer-use` skill. `install_orca_skills` finds Orca by probing `orca-ide` then `orca` for a topic named `orca-cli` (`install_user_setup.py:35,198`), and asks `<orca> skills install --agent claude-code --skill computer-use --skill orca-cli --skill orchestration`, never forwarding `--force`; every return path is "ok" so the step cannot fail the install, except that `_find_orca` reads `topics` outside its `try` and a malformed reply can raise (owner question C3). Doctor checks ccsession but not Orca skills, an Orca CLI, the three guard scripts or `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (`research/gap-P3-G03.md` A.2-A.3, C.2-C.3) | Orca generates the skills for its own version; the step shares a 30 s network budget with plugins (`install_user_setup.py:36-40`) | The code comment calling the Orca step a network download is [CODE-CONTRADICTED] (Orca's help says no network is needed); low impact (`gap-P3-G03.md` A.5) |
| 11 | The installer keeps existing files unless forced | `_copy_scripts` skips an existing destination unless `force`; `_merge_settings` does not refresh an outdated registration without `force`; core files are skipped likewise (`install_hooks.py:279-281,417-419`; `install_core.py:70-72,94-105`, per `research/03` B.3 and `research/06` F5). Consequence on the research host: the installed set is mixed and only `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` equals the working tree (matrix in Section 3.0.3; `research/06` F6 for the research-time result) | Preserve user customizations across re-installs; `superclaude update` and `install --force` refresh (both overwrite the shipped files they copy with no backup, apart from the exceptions listed in Section 5.6 item 1) | None recorded. No single revision describes the installed behavior; the final document must state which revision each statement documents |
| 12 | The ownership wording was narrowed in an uncommitted edit set | Working-tree text: "The eligible session that creates a worktree owns its agent(s). A supervised orchestrator's coordinator is person-opened; ownership does not authorize a supervised worker to create worktrees or dispatch workers." (`core/CLAUDE.md:57-61`); same idea in the hook message (`require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:354-355`), project `CLAUDE.md:312` (different wording) and the ccsession README. HEAD and ORIGIN/MASTER still say "becomes its orchestrator, or a coordinator under another orchestrator"; HOST-INSTALLED `CLAUDE.md` and hook carry the old text. A managed package records the explicit request and its execution on 2026-11-10 (`.dev/tasks/done/TASK-WF-planTheExplicitl-20261110-024937/`, staged, uncommitted; its own advisory review did not finish). Acceptance, commit and rollout are not recorded. "Eligible session" and "orchestrator" are not defined anywhere (owner questions C8, C9) | A narrow wording clarification that keeps the person-opened coordinator rule and the worker fan-out ban; it is explicitly "not implementation of a subordinate mode" (package `.dev/tasks/done/TASK-WF-planTheExplicitl-20261110-024937/source.md`, per `research/01` A.1) | Departs from the shipped PR #449 phrase (HISTORICAL report sections 2.2, 4 A01, 7.6 present the old wording as current and stay as history). Does not close A01 or the future-role question |

The historical report decided O6 "defer" with no implementation authorized (`RESEARCH-REPORT-sub-orchestrator-mode.md:2108,2294`, HISTORICAL). Issues #339, #423, #431 and #444 are OPEN (re-read 2026-11-12). Orca's federated own-Run preamble text exists in Orca source and is not IronClaude sub-orchestration (`research/05` 5.3) | Owner scope: deferred pending the design question owned by #444 | None. Never described as shipped, planned for release or resolved |
| 14 | The `/sc:implement` worker-contract producer is deferred to phase P4 | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:33,243,250` describe an implement contract; `commands/implement.md` and the `sc-implement-protocol` skill contain no unattended or worker-contract text, and `commands/workflow.md:73` says the executor accepts the flag "only in a later phase" (`research/gap-P3-G04.md` 5.1) | Scope decision in `.dev/tasks/done/TASK-WF-orcaWorkerContra-20261103-012232/source.md` (section 3 row `implement` `:169`; section 6 `:216,224,234`; approved decisions `:385-391`), pinned as `deferred:<phase>` at `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:1930-1934` | Present-tense contract wording describes a producer that does not exist; a future-phase item with a cited scope decision, not a DEFECT (owner question C7 asks only whether the decision is still current) |
| 15 | Every retry is a new Task; `worker-start --retry-of` is not used (WC-6) | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:71-79`: a retry is a new Task with a new report directory, only after the earlier attempt is settled; the three-failed-starts comparison is made by the coordinator itself (`:75`). No IronClaude code performs the comparison. Orca's breaker sets `circuit_broken` when a Dispatch's inherited-plus-new failure count reaches three (`IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/db/dispatch-context/dispatch-circuit-breaker.ts:1`, `DISPATCH_CIRCUIT_BREAK_FAILURES = 3`), the refusal to retry such a Dispatch sits inside the `if (params.retryOf)` branch of `worker-dispatch-start.ts` (`:95-104`), and on `worker-start` the count is inherited only along a `retryOf` chain (`:126-127`; detail in Section 5.2 item 1); because WC-6 forbids `--retry-of`, the contract's own retry flow reaches neither the state nor the refusal through `worker-start` (ORCA-CHECKOUT; installed bundle `main/chunks/orcad-migration-source-retention-B-HAVPZ-.js:2591` agrees on the branch, `git diff --quiet` of the source file printed no difference; `research/02` section 2 and 6.3). Orca's own guide recommends `--retry-of` for failed or stopped workers (`IronOrca/src/cli/bundled-skill-guides.ts:4455`), so the contract diverges from Orca's documented retry path on purpose | The brief is immutable after Task creation and report directories could otherwise mix (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:73`, per `research/02` section 2) | Intentional divergence from Orca's documented retry path, a policy choice rather than an Orca limitation. The timing argument for not using `send --to dispatch:<id>` for a retry brief ("received its task on the command line", `:78`) is [UNVERIFIED]; the Dispatch ID reaches the caller only after the preamble is delivered as a typed agent turn (`gap-P3-G01.md` C8, R6) |

Open owner questions that bear on this section (reproduced as questions, not decisions; the full list is Section 14.3.1): C1 (name the claim-flow exception in the rule text), C2 (the pr-submit `--monitor 1` clause), C3 (`_find_orca` raising), C7 (P4 scope decision still current), C8 and C9 (define "eligible session"; replace or define "orchestrator").

### 2.4 Technology Stack (Feature-Specific)

Only technologies specific to this feature are listed; the platform stack (Python package, `uv`, pytest plugin, click, rich) is in project `CLAUDE.md` and is not repeated. "Not pinned" means the repository declares no version for it.

| Technology | Version | Purpose in This Feature |
|---|---|---|
| Orca (desktop app and CLI, external product) | Two builds in play, neither proven to be the running one: development checkout `main` `dc57b3d6e`, version 1.4.214 (ORCA-CHECKOUT); installed files `1.4.245-aidev02.9cfecdd30b2a`, commit `9cfecdd30b2a90c06e5178b1245cb31a97d11bac`, from `/opt/Orca/resources/app.asar.unpacked/out/package.json` (INSTALLED-FILES). Running build: [UNVERIFIED] | Supplies the orchestration runtime (Tasks, Dispatches, mailbox, preamble, worker-start authority, nested-depth cap) and the CLI that launchers and the installer call by subprocess under the names `orca`, `orca-ide`, `orca-dev` |
| Claude Code hooks (external) | Installed Claude Code version not read [UNVERIFIED]; documented behavior cited per statement (for example `onFailure` needs v2.1.295 or later, DOCUMENTED https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks) | Command hooks on `PreToolUse` and `UserPromptSubmit` carry the three guards; documented: matching hooks run in parallel and merge `deny` > `defer` > `ask` > `allow`; exit 2 blocks, including in `bypassPermissions`; hooks run inside subagents (payload carries `agent_id`/`agent_type`; whether a subagent's `session_id` is the parent's is undocumented, ship gate U1); `--bare`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE=1`, `--safe-mode`, `disableAllHooks` and `allowManagedHooksOnly` are documented routes that skip or limit settings hooks (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide; `research/web-01`, `research/web-02`). None observed on this host |
| Bash with `jq` and `awk` | Not pinned; research host observation: `jq` 1.7, `mawk` (`research/03` B.4, one machine) | Hook scripts: `jq` extracts `tool_input.command`; one stateful awk scanner tokenizes it (`require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:345-347`). Missing `jq` or `awk` makes the worktree gate fail open; the launch-level guard treats a governed session with a failing `jq` as LL-BROKEN (deny) |
| `uv` and embedded Python (`python -I`) | Not pinned | `launch-job-identity.sh` runs its program as `uv run --no-project python -I -c` (`:55-566`, per `research/03` E); the claim preflight requires `uv`, `jq` and `cat sed tr mkdir mv ln rm` on PATH (`orca_worktree.py:841`, `GUARD_COMMANDS`, per `research/04` F1) |
| Linux process interfaces (`/proc`, `pidfd_open`, `prctl` child subreaper, `fcntl`) | Linux only | The identity helper supervises governed Bash jobs and classifies a process as governed, ordinary or unknown; it is "not a sandbox" and a deliberate `setsid` escape is not contained (`launch-job-identity.sh:1-9`, per `research/03` E.1); the claim preflight checks these interfaces (`orca_worktree.py:847-877`, per `research/04`) |
| Python standard library only (claim script, installer) | Project `requires-python >=3.10` (project `CLAUDE.md`) | `orca_worktree.py` is "standard-library-only"; `install_hooks.py` is pure stdlib (module docstring, per `research/03` B.3); no third-party library is specific to this feature |
| bash wrappers (ccsession, ccsession-orca) | Not pinned | `ccsession-orca` is a bash wrapper Orca is configured to use as its Claude command; it execs `ccsession ... --shim`, which names the tab (`gap-P3-G05.md` F1). Orca's Claude command setting on any host is [UNVERIFIED] |
| YAML (`return-contract.yaml`) | Contract version string `"1"` (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:33`) | Format of the worker result file; no parser or schema validator exists in `src/` for the worker fields |
| pytest | Project dependency `>=7.0.0` (project `CLAUDE.md`) | Static text pins (`tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py`), hook script tests that run the real scripts with a temporary `HOME` (`tests/hooks/`); live observations are skipped by design |

## 3. Directory Structure

Four separate locations matter and they are not the same tree: the IronClaude checkout (source of truth), the host's installed copies (what `superclaude install` and Orca wrote), Orca's own sources, and runtime state directories that scripts create. Each tree below lists only paths that a cited source names; there are no file counts (template rule, key numbers live in Section 1). Tags in comments: [C] checkout, [I] installed host copy, [O] ORCA-CHECKOUT, [OI] Orca INSTALLED-FILES.

### 3.0 Checkout tree (CHECKOUT, HEAD `5bdb01681`, branch `ironbelly/design-doc-shim-title-check`)

```
IronClaude/ (worktree root)
├── CLAUDE.md                                   # project rules (NORMATIVE); ownership sentence is an uncommitted edit
├── src/superclaude/                            # source of truth for everything that is installed
│   ├── core/
│   │   ├── WORKER-CONTRACT.md                  # NORMATIVE: terms, WC rules, coordinator rules (sec. 4), brief template (sec. 5)
│   │   ├── CLAUDE.md                           # NORMATIVE: "Orca session launches", ownership wording; imports only the bash policy (@ line 3)
│   │   └── HOW-WE-WORK.md                      # routing guide; points at the contract; not auto-loaded
│   ├── commands/
│   │   ├── <name>.md                           # converted commands carry "## Unattended mode" (report dir, Contract bullet)
│   │   └── implement.md                        # no unattended text: no worker-contract producer today
│   ├── hooks/
│   │   ├── hooks.json                          # registrations: guards and ccsession hooks, installed paths under ~/.claude
│   │   └── scripts/
│   │       ├── require-orca-worktree-agent.sh  # PreToolUse(Bash): "orca worktree create" needs --agent
│   │       ├── launch-level-guard.sh           # L1 guard: UserPromptSubmit + PreToolUse
│   │       └── launch-job-identity.sh          # PreToolUse(Bash) identity adapter and job runner
│   ├── cli/
│   │   ├── main.py                             # install / update / doctor entry points
│   │   ├── install_core.py, install_hooks.py, install_user_setup.py, install_ccsession.py, doctor.py
│   │   ├── issue_watch/{launch.py, cycle.py, cli.py}   # watcher: non-worker launch path B (no worker start)
│   │   └── reflect/contract.py                 # reads reflect's OWN return-contract.yaml; ignores worker keys
│   └── skills/
│       ├── sc-issue-protocol/
│       │   ├── SKILL.md, refs/claim.md, templates/launch-prompt.md
│       │   └── scripts/orca_worktree.py        # claim path A: worktree create, agent launch, shim check
│       ├── ccsession-tag/
│       │   ├── ccsession, ccsession-orca, README.md
│       │   └── hooks/{session-start.sh, session-name.sh}
│       ├── sc-workflow-protocol/refs/return-contract.md    # workflow package contract + worker contract text
│       └── sc-implement-protocol/scripts/archive_workspace.py   # reads the PACKAGE contract only
├── tests/
│   ├── skills/{test_worker_contract.py, test_issue_orca_worktree.py}   # static text pins; fake orca
│   ├── hooks/{test_launch_level_guard.py, test_require_orca_worktree_agent.py, test_launch_job_identity.py, test_launch_level_observations.py}
│   └── issue_watch/, unit/{test_ccsession_tag.py, test_install_ccsession.py}, core/, cli/{test_install_user_setup.py}
├── docs/{designs/, getting-started/installation.md, developer-guide/}
├── plugins/superclaude/{hooks/, commands/, core/}   # mirror, NOT equivalent: core/ has no WORKER-CONTRACT.md or CLAUDE.md
├── .claude/                                    # gitignored sync-dev copy (NORMATIVE, project CLAUDE.md names only settings.json as tracked); at HEAD git also tracks cache/sc-recommend-lookup.yaml (CHECKOUT, .gitignore:127-132)
└── .dev/{tasks/{to-do,done}/, orca-command-refactor/specs/, issues/<N>/}
```

The plugin build carries `## Unattended mode` in only two commands, `recommend.md` and `workflow.md` (Grep of `plugins/superclaude`). `core/CLAUDE.md:3` is the only `@` import (`@BASH_INSPECTION_POLICY.md`), so the worker contract is never auto-loaded. ### 3.0.1 Installed host copies and runtime state (HOST-INSTALLED; the research host only)

```
$HOME
├── .claude/
│   ├── CLAUDE.md, WORKER-CONTRACT.md, HOW-WE-WORK.md     # [I] copied from src/superclaude/core by install_core
│   ├── hooks/{launch-level-guard.sh, launch-job-identity.sh, require-orca-worktree-agent.sh, launch-level-pre-push.sh}   # [I] flat copies of hooks/scripts/; the pre-push copy equals origin/master, HEAD has no such script
│   ├── settings.json                                     # [I] user-level hook registrations (re-read with jq 2026-11-12: guard on both events, gate, identity hook; see Section 9.1)
│   ├── skills/
│   │   ├── ccsession-tag/, sc-issue-protocol/scripts/orca_worktree.py      # [I] copied by the generic skill installer
│   │   ├── orchestration, orca-cli                       # symlinks into ../../.agents/skills/ (Orca-written, not shipped by IronClaude)
│   │   └── computer-use/                                 # real directory (Orca-written)
│   ├── state/launch-level/{pending/<nonce>, sessions/<session_id>[.ok], jobs/<kernel session id>.json}
│   ├── projects/<key>/topics/<name>.txt                  # ccsession name lists
│   └── ccsession.env
├── .agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md                        # Orca writes the stubs here, then links them into ~/.claude/skills
├── .local/bin/{ccsession, ccsession-orca, orca-ide}      # first two link into the ccsession-tag skill; orca-ide links to /opt/Orca
├── .local/state/ccsession/orca-tabs/<pane key>           # tab record: session id, launch folder, terminal handle
├── .cache/ccsession/orca-titles/<terminal handle>        # title mark: the exact title ccsession set
└── .config/orca/linux-orca-cli-shim/                     # Orca's CLI shim directory
```

- Core files: `install_core.py:14-29` lists the twelve known core names, including `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `HOW-WE-WORK.md`; `install_core.py:70-72` skips an existing destination unless `force`. [CODE-VERIFIED, CHECKOUT]
- Hook scripts: `install_hooks.py:86-94` names the three guard scripts; `:264-273` pairs each with a flat destination name, `:279-281` skips an existing file unless `force`, `:284-285` copies with `copy2` then `chmod 0o755`; `hooks.json:110,120,130` register them as `~/.claude/hooks/<name>.sh`. A plain `superclaude install` therefore keeps an older installed script silently. [CODE-VERIFIED, CHECKOUT]
- Orca-written files: IronClaude ships no `orchestration`, `orca-cli` or `computer-use` skill; `install_user_setup.py:34` names them and `:243-245` builds `[orca, "skills", "install", "--agent", "claude-code", "--skill", ...]`. Orca owns the bytes and the link layout (`research/gap-P3-G03.md` A.5, F.1: shared folder `~/.agents/skills` first, then a link into each agent folder; ORCA-CHECKOUT, not re-read here). [CODE-VERIFIED for the request, CHECKOUT; Orca's write behavior ORCA-CHECKOUT via research]
- Runtime state: the pending record path is `$HOME/.claude/state/launch-level/pending/<nonce>` (`orca_worktree.py:51-53` docstring, `PENDING_PARTS` at `:773`); the guard reads `$LL_STORE/sessions/$LL_SID` (`launch-level-guard.sh:257`); the identity runner keeps job records in a `jobs` subdirectory of its store (`launch-job-identity.sh:222`). The ccsession state locations (`orca-tabs`, `orca-titles`, name lists) are from `research/gap-P3-G05.md` F2.2, F2.4, F8 (`ccsession:518-525`, `:564-572`, `:387`), not re-read. [CODE-VERIFIED for the first three; the rest research-sourced]
- Existence re-check 2026-11-12: all host paths in the tree above exist except those not checked (`state/launch-level/jobs/`, `projects/<key>/topics/`, the per-handle files). Host symlink targets and modification times are research observations (`research/gap-P3-G03.md` F.3, 2026-11-11), a moment-in-time fact about one host. The installed guard, identity hook, claim script and ccsession README are therefore the ORIGIN/MASTER versions, so on this host the claim script is the 1884-line version with the L2 additions, not this checkout's 1473-line file (`wc -l` of the checkout file printed 1473; the installed size is `research/04-launchers.md` F5). The installed `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` equals HEAD, so Flow B below describes the installed contract; it does not include the coordinator preflight that exists on origin/master. `HOW-WE-WORK.md` matched the older origin/master of 2026-11-11 (`research/06` F6, eighth file) and now matches no ref. Two more installed files are not in the matrix: `skills/sc-issue-protocol/SKILL.md` and `skills/sc-issue-protocol/refs/claim.md` each differ from HEAD, `origin/master` and the working tree (`cmp`, 2026-11-12, ctime 01:59:25 UTC), so this reference's citations of those two files are CHECKOUT (HEAD) text and not a statement about the installed skill. [CODE-VERIFIED]

### 3.1 File Naming Conventions

Only conventions that a cited source shows are listed. No other naming rule is evidenced and none is invented.

| Pattern | Convention | Example | Evidence |
|---------|-----------|---------|----------|
| Task package id | `TASK-<TYPE>-<slug>-<YYYYMMDD>-<HHMMSS>`; the folder is named by the id and holds `<id>.md`; the folder sits in `.dev/tasks/to-do/` and moves to `done/` on archive | `.dev/tasks/done/TASK-WF-planTheExplicitl-20261110-024937/` (types seen: `WF`, `FIX`, `RESEARCH`, `TECHREF`) | Glob of that folder plus existence checks of the `TASK-FIX-` and `TASK-RESEARCH-` folders, 2026-11-12; archive move: `gap-P3-G04.md` section 2 step 7 (`src/superclaude/skills/sc-workflow-protocol/refs/return-contract.md:33,51`, not re-read) |
| Package contents | `progress.md`, `return-contract.yaml`, `source.md`, `artifacts/final-*.log`, `artifacts/retries/T<n>/prompt-<n>-<k>.md`, `artifacts/retries/T<n>/q0-head` | the `TASK-WF-planTheExplicitl-...` folder above | Glob result, 2026-11-12 |
| Worker report file | fixed name `return-contract.yaml` inside the one report directory the brief names | `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:32` (WC-4) |
| Package contract versus worker contract | two files, same file name, different folders and vocabularies: `<pkg>/return-contract.yaml` (key `status`, read by code) and `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` (key `worker_status`, read by no code) | `archive_workspace.py` reads the first only | `research/gap-P3-G04.md` 4.2, 4.4, takeaway 4 |
| Worker handoff files | `<report-dir>/handoff/<n>-<command>.md`, written after each `Command:` line | WC-15 | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:64` |
| Brief lines | fixed prefixes `Guard:`, `Chain:`, `On a blocked gate:`, `Local commit:`, `Report directory:`, `Worktree:`, `Outward actions:`, `P0 only:`, `Command:` | the text block in section 5 of the contract; the `Local commit:` slot is `not authorized` or `authorized` plus the user's instruction quoted word for word (grant conditions: Section 5.1 item 3) | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:288-296`, slot rule `:302`; `P0 only:` is removed on ORIGIN/MASTER (contract diff, 2026-11-12) |
| Contract-internal labels | rules `WC-<n>`; liveness states `S0` to `S11`; ladder steps `L0` to `L5`; ack steps `A1` to `A4`; run-notes labels `pending_ack` and `unacked-at-settlement` | `WC-6`, `S9` | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:67-165` (states `:116-129`, acks `:131-138`, ladder `:146-155`, re-read 2026-11-12; tabulated in Section 5.2 tables B and C); the two run-notes labels appear only in the contract (`research/02-lifecycle.md` 4.2) |
| Issue claim directory | `.dev/issues/<N>/`; the claim copies it file by file into the worktree and keeps `worktree.json` and `launch-prompt.md` there | `<wt>/.dev/issues/<N>/launch-prompt.md` | `orca_worktree.py:20-22,39-40,51-56` |
| Claim worktree identity | Orca worktree name `issue-<N>` with comment `sc:issue <owner/repo>#<N>` | `issue-452` | `orca_worktree.py:674,679` |

| Hook scripts | source `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/<name>.sh`; installed flat as `~/.claude/hooks/<name>.sh` | `launch-level-guard.sh` | `hooks.json:110,120,130`; `install_hooks.py:264-273` |

## 4. Data Flow

### 4.1 Primary Data Flow

Two end-to-end flows exist and they must not be merged. **Flow A (claim flow)** starts a non-worker Claude session in a new Orca worktree and checks that it sits behind ccsession; it never starts a supervised worker. **Flow B (worker flow)** is the supervised path: a person-opened coordinator starts workers, workers report through `worker_done` and a `return-contract.yaml`, and the coordinator admits the result. Only hops that a cited source supports are drawn. Every hop carries one tag:

| Tag | Meaning |
|-----|---------|
| [CODE] | executable code in the CHECKOUT (HEAD plus uncommitted edits) |
| [TEXT] | NORMATIVE instruction text that an LLM session follows; no code runs it |
| [ORCA-SRC] | Orca source, ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`; INSTALLED-FILES agreement is stated where checked |
| [HOST] | Claude Code or host configuration; not observable here, so [UNVERIFIED] for the running system |

#### 4.1.1 Flow A: claim flow (issue claim, worktree, agent launch, ccsession title, shim check)

```
FIRST HOP
person: /sc:issue --claim <N>  [TEXT]      (path B: a scheduler the owner chooses [UNVERIFIED; none enabled per the runbook, off by default] -> issue-watch once -> the same prompt, no marker  [CODE])
   |
   v
claim session (Claude, Bash tool) runs scripts/orca_worktree.py:
   preflight  -> orca status --json                                                         [CODE]
   worktree   -> orca worktree list, create (no --agent), copy .dev/issues/<N>/, close plain first tab   [CODE]
   launch     -> gates -> write pending/<nonce> -> orca agent launch --prompt "[sc-launch:v1:<nonce>] Read ..."   [CODE]
   |
   v
Orca: opens a PTY tab in the worktree, sets ORCA_PANE_KEY and ORCA_TERMINAL_HANDLE,
      starts its configured Claude command with the prompt as the last argument      [ORCA-SRC; configured command: HOST, UNVERIFIED]
   |
   +--------------------------------+------------------------------------+
   v                                v                                    v
ccsession-orca -> ccsession     Claude Code runs hooks [HOST dispatch]   claim session keeps reading
  --auto-name makes the label     UserPromptSubmit: launch-level-guard     behind_ccsession(handle), 5 tries  [CODE]
  OSC title "<label> . Claude Code"   consumes pending/<nonce> and          orca terminal read --screen
  exec claude --name <label> ...      binds sessions/<sid>  [CODE]          orca terminal show (title)
                                  SessionStart: session-name.sh ->
                                    orca terminal rename (same title) [CODE]
   |
   v
LAST HOP
JSON {status: launched, warning: W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION when the check fails} read by the skill; "launch" entry
written to <D>/worktree.json; the agent keeps running; sessions/<sid> stays bound until a person deletes it
```

The three branches after the Orca spawn run concurrently; their relative timing (title write against the five reads) was not measured (`research/gap-P3-G05.md` R3). [UNVERIFIED]

The `sessions/<sid>` binding is released only by a person deleting `sessions/<sid>` and `.ok` (`research/03-guards.md` C.6 item 5) |

Neither launcher is a supervised-worker launcher: `orca_worktree.py` and `launch.py` contain no `orchestration`, `worker-start`, `dispatch` or `task-create` call. [CODE-VERIFIED] (negative result, `research/04-launchers.md` F1, F2, F4 B1). The worker-side bans on launching (WC-8, WC-13, never-as-worker) are instruction text with no code refusal on the launcher path (same file, B2).

#### 4.1.2 Flow B: worker flow (dispatch preamble, heartbeat, `worker_done`, `return-contract.yaml`, coordinator wait and sweep)

Role split in this flow: the coordinator is "an Orca coordinator session the person opened" (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:69`); a worker is a session that received an Orca preamble carrying a Task ID and a Dispatch ID (`:11`). No IronClaude code implements the coordinator loop or any worker duty. The executable parts are Orca's (ORCA-CHECKOUT; installed-file agreement is stated per fact in 4.2) and the Python that touches nearby contracts (4.3). Subordinate or sub-orchestrator coordination is not a hop in this flow.

```
FIRST HOP
person opens a coordinator session; it reads ~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md (nothing loads it automatically)   [TEXT, HOST]
   |
   v
coordinator: task-create --spec "<brief>" (one brief per Task, unique report dir)                       [TEXT; verb: ORCA-SRC]
   |
   v
coordinator: worker-start (never --retry-of)                                                            [TEXT]
   Orca: workerStart handler -> resolveWorkerStartRun (coordinator path) -> createStartingWorkerDispatch   [ORCA-SRC]
   |
   v
Orca types the dispatch preamble + TASK into the worker's terminal (sendAgentTurn)                       [ORCA-SRC]
   |
   v
worker (top-level Claude session)                                                                       [TEXT]
   brief guard on each Command: line -> run each command through its Skill (WC-7)
   skill LLM writes <report-dir>/return-contract.yaml (+ handoff files)
   heartbeat per preamble (only when the setting is on)   ----> Orca records last_heartbeat_at           [ORCA-SRC]
   ask / check per preamble                               ----> run mailbox or creator mailbox          [ORCA-SRC]
   worker_done --outcome succeeded|failed --report-path <contract> (exactly once) -> Orca settles Task    [TEXT; settlement ORCA-SRC]
   |
   v
coordinator: check --wait --types <9 types> --timeout-ms 300000 ; handle batch ; run-notes ; --ack        [TEXT]
             sweep (<= every 5 min): worker-list, worktree ps --agent-wait, stat contract paths          [TEXT]
   |
   v
LAST HOP
coordinator opens return-contract.yaml, admits succeeded / partial / failed / blocked, then creates
dependents with --deps or retries as a NEW Task; commits and publishes only on the person's instruction  [TEXT]
```

| # | Hop | Tag | Evidence | Notes |
|---|-----|-----|----------|-------|
| B1 | First hop: the person opens the coordinator. The contract defines the coordinator as person-opened; nothing in the checkout loads the contract into that session (`core/CLAUDE.md:3` imports only the bash policy; `HOW-WE-WORK.md` is "not loaded automatically") | [TEXT] and [HOST] | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:67-69`; `research/02-lifecycle.md` 6.1 | Hooks searched: only the three guard scripts and `hooks.json`; other injection paths [UNVERIFIED] (`gaps-and-questions.md` section B) |
| B2 | Task and brief: `task-create --spec` with the brief template; seven required brief contents; the Task's brief cannot change after creation (`task-update` changes only `--status` and `--result`) | [TEXT]; verb flags [ORCA-SRC] | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:73,81-93,283-297`; `IronOrca/src/cli/specs/orchestration.ts:166-174` (via `research/02-lifecycle.md` section 2 table) | The installed contract lists the `P0 only:` brief line (`:295`); ORIGIN/MASTER removes it and adds a coordinator preflight before the first `task-create` for a command (`### Before the first task-create for a command`, commit `146be6fc3`). Neither is code |

It names no IronClaude file and no `return-contract.yaml` |

#### 4.1.3 What the two flows enforce, and the recorded DEFECT

| Concern | Flow A (claim) | Flow B (worker) |
|---------|----------------|-----------------|
| Executable steps in the checkout | Yes: `orca_worktree.py`, `launch.py`, `ccsession`, the guard and identity hooks | No coordinator or worker step; only hooks that fire on Bash calls, Python that reads nearby contracts (reflect wrapper, workflow archive) and static text tests |
| Executable steps in Orca | Spawn of the tab and environment variables (ORCA-CHECKOUT, via research) | `worker-start`, preamble, mailbox routing, settlement, `check`/`ack` (ORCA-CHECKOUT; installed files agree on the points compared) |
| Instruction text | Skill waves and the ccsession README setting | The whole coordinator and worker contract (sections 3 to 5 of `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`) |
| Hook enforcement | Guard binds the launched session by nonce; worktree gate requires `--agent` on a Bash-typed `orca worktree create` | The worktree gate and, only inside a nonce-bound session, the guard's ESCAPE class. A supervised worker or a person-opened coordinator is not bound by the guard (`launch-level-guard.sh:6-7`) |
| Static test pins | Fake-`orca` tests pin argv, order and result mapping (`test_issue_orca_worktree.py`); no test joins the nonce handoff or the label to the title pattern | `test_worker_contract.py` pins phrases of the contract; nothing parses a produced contract |

**DEFECT pointer (owner-confirmed 2026-11-11): the launch-level guard classifies the Orca CLI only under the command word `orca`.** The statement, the probe table, the owner confirmation, the remediation-plan link and the plan's state are in Section 8.2 (state: 14.2 TD1); they are not repeated here. In data-flow terms: hop A8 binds the session by nonce and is not affected, but the ESCAPE and approval-forgery guarantee of the "Hook payload to decision" row (4.3) holds for the name `orca` only, so a nonce-bound session that types `orca-ide orchestration worker-start ...` is not stopped by the guard. The worktree gate has the same lexical limit (`require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:145`, `:349`), but its documented reach is the literal `orca worktree create`, so for the gate this is a recorded ceiling, not a defect. Which name a launched or supervised session actually types was not observed [UNVERIFIED].
