### 5.1 Roles and Authority

**Purpose:** Defines the two session roles of supervised orchestration (a person-opened coordinator and a dispatched worker), what each may do, and which human rulings are encoded in the rules. Almost all of it is instruction text read by language-model sessions; the only IronClaude-side machine check on these rules is described in 5.3.

**Key Files:**

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/superclaude/core/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` | Sections 1-3 (`:9-65`): terms, command-side rules WC-2/4/8/9/10/11, worker duties WC-3/5/7/12/13/14/15. Section 4 opens at `:67-69` with the coordinator definition. Not in `git status` (equals HEAD). NORMATIVE. |
| `src/superclaude/core/CLAUDE.md` | `:45-61` "Orca session launches" and the ownership paragraph. Working-tree wording is uncommitted. NORMATIVE. |
| `src/superclaude/core/HOW-WE-WORK.md` | `:400`: "The coordinator is a session the person opened ... never a session that Orca itself started as a worker." NORMATIVE, committed. |
| `CLAUDE.md` (project root) | `:312` states the same ownership rule in its own words; uncommitted edit; not pinned by a test. |
| `src/superclaude/skills/ccsession-tag/README.md` | `:239-245` second copy of the ownership paragraph (uncommitted). |
| `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` | Static text pins: `NEVER_AS_WORKER` (`:382`, 8 entries), `RULE_PHRASES` (`:25-374`). 739 tests passed in the research run (`research/06` F4). |
| `tests/core/test_orca_session_launch_guidance.py` | Pins the ownership paragraph in the two `COPIES` (`:8`); asserts `"coordinator is person-opened"` (`:33`) and the absence of `"coordinator under another orchestrator"` (`:36`). 5 passed. |
| `.dev/orca-command-refactor/decisions/APPROVED.md` | HISTORICAL record of owner decisions D1-D5 (`:8-12`), approved 2026-11-02. Not distributed. |

**How It Works:**

1. **Roles.** The contract defines a *worker run* as "a session that received an Orca preamble carrying a Task ID and a Dispatch ID. Only such a session may send lifecycle messages" (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:11`); every other session is ordinary. The coordinator is "an Orca coordinator session the person opened" that "plans, creates Tasks, starts workers, reads their reports and performs every outward action" (`:69`). Subagents have no identity in the contract: a command's own Agent-tool fan-out "stays as it is" and may not send lifecycle messages (`:60`). NORMATIVE.
2. **No code identifies a role.** No command, hook or script in the checkout reads a preamble, a Task ID or a Dispatch ID (grep over `src/superclaude/commands` and `hooks/scripts`: no hit, `research/01` B.1). "Person-opened" is a procedural fact, not a runtime property. [CODE-VERIFIED by absence]
3. **Command-side limits.** Under `--unattended` a command asks no person (WC-2, `:31`), writes one report contract (WC-4, `:32-39`), never calls `worker-start`, `dispatch` or `task-create` (WC-8, `:40`), takes no outward action and no brief can turn one on (WC-9, `:41-45`, decision D4; the one git write a brief may authorize is the worker's end-of-Task local commit, described next), and keeps heartbeats alive (WC-10, `:46`). The mode is selected only by a standalone `--unattended` token (decision D1, `:12-13`); 31 command files carry an `## Unattended mode` section (16 in P1 plus 15 in P2; set pinned by `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` `test_p1_command_set_is_complete` `:1457` and `test_p2_command_set_is_complete` `:1569`).
   - **Local-commit grant (the only git write a brief may authorize; `:42-45`).** Three conditions must hold: (1) the brief quotes, word for word, the user's instruction naming a local commit for that Task; (2) the worker runs alone in its own child worktree created by Orca (WC-11); (3) the command has exited with `worker_status: succeeded`. Procedure (`:43`): the worker reads coordinator follow-ups, refuses on a detached HEAD, the default branch or any path outside its report directory, `report_path`, `artifacts` and `.dev/tasks`, stages with `git add -- <paths>` plus `.dev/tasks`, commits with `git commit --only -- <paths>`, never uses `--no-verify`, never pushes, and gives the commit ID in its `worker_done` body. A refused or failed commit gives `--outcome failed` with a body whose first sentence reads `worker_status: succeeded; commit not made` and the reason (WC-5, `:59`). A worker that finds `index.lock` in its worktree reports `blocked` with the path (`:45`). Outside the grant a worker never runs `git add` (including `git add .dev/tasks`), never commits, never moves HEAD and never touches a remote; the coordinator stages `.dev/tasks` (`:44`). The brief slot `Local commit:` is `not authorized` (the template default) or `authorized` followed by the quoted instruction (`:291`, `:302`). Per-task commits inside `/sc:implement` wait for issue #326 (K3) (`:42`). NORMATIVE; no hook enforces any of it (re-read 2026-11-12).
   - **Parallel-safe writes (WC-11, `:47`).** Two workers in one worktree must never write the same file. Default output paths carry a timestamp or the slug the coordinator passes, the coordinator gives every Task its own report directory, and single-owner shared files (for example memory keys overwritten whole) are not written in unattended mode. Workers share the feature worktree only when they write nothing but their own report directory, which goes under `.dev/`. A worker gets its own child worktree (brief slot `Worktree:`, `worker-start --worktree new-child --base-branch <feature branch>`, `:91`, `:293`) when (1) its brief grants local commits, (2) it runs `/sc:implement` and any other writer (another worker, the coordinator, the person, a taken-over terminal or a hook) may write outside `.dev/` in the same worktree during the run, (3) it is a parallel experiment to compare or discard, or (4) its command writes the user index. The coordinator's liveness `stat` reads only the worker's own contract path and report directory. NORMATIVE; pinned only as the phrase "Two workers in one worktree must never write the same file" (`RULE_PHRASES` entry `WC-11`, `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:129`); a grep of `*.sh`, `*.py`, `*.json` and `*.js` under `src/superclaude` for `parallel experiment`, `child worktree` and `WC-11` found no file, so no code applies the placement rule (CHECKOUT, [CODE-VERIFIED by absence], 2026-11-12).
4. **Worker-session limits.** Questions go to the coordinator via the preamble's `ask`, at most once per Task (WC-3, `:53-58`); `worker_done` is sent exactly once from the top-level session (WC-5, `:59`); the worker runs each chained `Command:` by invoking its Skill (WC-7, `:60`); and "never calls `worker-start`, `dispatch` or `task-create`, Orca's low-level dispatch, or `orca-cli` `worktree create --agent` or `agent launch`, at any depth setting" (WC-13, `:62`). The brief guard (`:16-22`) is a worker-session duty with four `guard refused:` cases; no executable implements it, tests pin its wording only (`research/01` B.2, G7). Rule IDs present are WC-2 to WC-15; there is no WC-1.
5. **Never-as-worker list** (`:23`): `/sc:agent`, `/sc:git`, `/sc:help`, `/sc:issue`, `/sc:pm`, `/sc:spawn`, `/sc:swarm-wizard`, plus `/sc:pr-submit` while decision D3 keeps it off workers. Eight entries, pinned by `NEVER_AS_WORKER` (`tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:382`); advisory, nothing stops a coordinator dispatching one.
6. **Ownership wording (three states).** Working tree (uncommitted): "The eligible session that creates a worktree owns its agent(s). A supervised orchestrator's coordinator is person-opened; ownership does not authorize a supervised worker to create worktrees or dispatch workers" (`core/CLAUDE.md:57-61`). HEAD and the installed `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` on this host: the older sentence "becomes its orchestrator, or a coordinator under another orchestrator". Seven files are modified (` M`; list in Section 1, "Key numbers"). A managed package (`.dev/tasks/done/TASK-WF-planTheExplicitl-20261110-024937/`) records the explicit request and its execution on 2026-11-10; its own advisory review did not finish. No source records acceptance, commit or rollout. [CODE-VERIFIED state]
7. **Human rulings.** D1 (flag only plus brief guard), D2 (only the person gives a Ruling), D3 (pr-submit never a worker), D4 (no outward action by a worker), D5 (nesting depth one, coordinator person-opened, adds WC-13) appear at `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:12,57,23,41,62,69`. HISTORICAL source: `APPROVED.md:8-12`. The owner ruling that a worker that only reads a command file does not satisfy WC-7 is recorded at `APPROVED.md:83` (row P1-C5) and pinned as text at `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:353-359`.
8. **Ruling rule end to end.** `/sc:implement` stops `E-HALT-QA` and `E-NO-RULING` are never asked (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:57`); `src/superclaude/skills/sc-implement-protocol/refs/qa.md:249` lists "executor-invented approval" as `E-NO-RULING`. The ledger eligibility rule (`src/superclaude/skills/sc-implement-protocol/refs/retry.md:498`) checks only the ordering of a `T<id>: Ruling:` line against `blocked` and `halt` records. Nothing checks who wrote the line. [CODE-VERIFIED ceiling]
9. **Human-ruling and approval gates.** The coordinator's actions are conditioned on the person's instruction at these contract lines (NORMATIVE; no code enforces any of them):

| Contract line | Gate |
|---|---|
| `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:57` | A Ruling halt (`E-HALT-QA`, `E-NO-RULING`) is never asked; the person gives the Ruling in their own session or writes the Ruling line themselves after the worker has settled (D2); a Ruling written or drafted by the coordinator or by any worker is "executor-invented approval". |
| `:108` | A `decision_gate` goes to the person; a `question` is answered with `reply --id` or forwarded to the person; `merge_ready` merges only on the person's instruction. |
| `:221` | `--setup run`, `make sync-dev` or any other repository target, script or hook only when the user's instruction names that step, confirmed with the person at the moment of action unless that instruction is in the coordinator's live conversation. |
| `:229` | Every git and GitHub outward action, and every install, VM or paid run, only when the user's instruction names that action (same confirmation rule). |
| `:233` | Before any push that would publish worker commits the person has not seen, the coordinator shows them and pushes only on the person's go; a commit grant covers one Task and ends at that Task's `worker_done`. |
| `:246` | A ready Task the coordinator did not create is not started; it is reported to the person with its spec text. |


**Public Interface:** none in code. The interface is text: the rule IDs above, the `Command:` and guard lines of the brief (see 5.2), and the `worker_done` outcome mapping (see 5.7).

**Dependencies:**

| Depends On | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Orca dispatch preamble | External text | Defines a worker run (`:7`, `:11`); issued per dispatch by the running Orca build (5.2). |
| `~/.claude/skills/sc-implement-protocol/refs/qa.md`, `refs/retry.md` | NORMATIVE | Halt table and Ruling eligibility the contract cites. |
| `superclaude install` | Installer | Copies the contract to `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (5.6). |

**Consumers:**

| Used By | How |
|---|---|
| Coordinator session | Reads `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` voluntarily; no hook or startup step loads it (`research/02` G12, UNVERIFIED beyond three hook scripts and `hooks.json`). |
| Worker session and the 31 converted commands | Apply WC-2 to WC-15 and their `## Unattended mode` sections. |
| `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` | Pins phrases of the contract text. |

**Conventions & Patterns:**

- Authority is expressed as brief text and contract rules; mechanical enforcement is the exception (5.3, 5.5).
- A statement of the hook's reach always names the literal `orca worktree create` (`research/gap-P3-G02` section 3.5).
- Cite the ownership wording as "working-tree wording, uncommitted at 2026-11-12"; never describe the older wording as current.

**Extension points:** To add a rule: edit the contract (`src/superclaude/core/WORKER-CONTRACT.md`), add its phrases to `RULE_PHRASES` in `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py`, run `make sync-dev` and `make verify-sync`, and reinstall (5.6). To add a converted command: add an entry to `UNATTENDED_EXPECT` (`:438`; `UNATTENDED_COMMANDS` is derived at `:746`) and a phase tuple. To add a role: N/A, deferred. A subordinate role is not designed or shipped; issue #444 owns the question and this reference does not resolve it.

### 5.2 Coordinator Lifecycle and Orca Worker-Side Lifecycle

**Purpose:** Describes the full life of a supervised Task from both ends: the coordinator's rules (create, wait, sweep, classify, escalate, admit, retry, merge) that exist only as contract text, and the worker-side half that Orca generates (dispatch preamble, heartbeat, `ask`, `check`, `worker_done`, settlement, liveness evidence). The two halves meet at Orca messages; the coordinator rules are normative, the worker-side behavior is read from Orca source.

**Key Files:**

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/superclaude/core/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` | Section 4 (`:67-282`): WC-6 retries (`:71-79`), brief contents (`:81-93`), liveness supervision (`:95-200`), worktrees (`:218-225`), git ownership (`:227-235`), result admission (`:237-246`), `/sc:implement` freeze (`:248-269`), fan-in (`:271-273`). Section 5 (`:283-327`) is the brief template. NORMATIVE. |
| `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/preamble.ts` | `buildDispatchPreamble` (`:52-152`): the text a worker is given. ORCA-CHECKOUT; same text in the INSTALLED-FILES chunk `main/chunks/orcad-migration-source-retention-B-HAVPZ-.js` (per gap-P3-G01 S8). |
| `IronOrca/src/shared/orchestration-heartbeat.ts` | Heartbeat setting names and defaults (`:10-12`). |
| `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/db/attempt-outcome-projection.ts` | 60 s attempt-observation window (`:11`); has no production writer except `worker_report` (see below). |
| `IronOrca/src/shared/orchestration-fleet-worker-projection.ts` | `projectLiveness` (`:53-100`, per gap-P3-G01 S5): the fleet liveness verdict a coordinator reads. |
| `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/coordinator.ts` and `coordinator-task-dispatch.ts` | The retired automatic Task scheduler (dormant). |
| `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` | Static pins of section 4 phrases. Does not test coordinator behavior. |

**How It Works:**

```
 person opens coordinator session (NORMATIVE, WORKER-CONTRACT.md:69)
        |
 task-create --spec (brief + unique report dir)   [coordinator LLM]
        |
 worker-start  ---->  Orca writes dispatch preamble into worker session
        |                      |
        |            worker: heartbeat / ask / check / status ...
        |                      |
 check --wait --types <9 types> --timeout-ms 300000  <----  Orca mailbox
        |   sweep <= every 5 min: worker-list, worktree ps --agent-wait, stat contract
        |   S0-S11 state table --> ladder L0-L5 (WC-6 governs any restart)
        v
 worker_done --outcome --report-path   -->  Orca settles Task (valid report only)
        |
 coordinator opens return-contract.yaml, admits succeeded/partial/failed/blocked
        |
 dependents created only after inputs are admitted (--deps); merge, stage, publish on instruction
```

*Coordinator rules (NORMATIVE; no code runs the loop).*

1. **Retry is a new Task (WC-6).** The coordinator never uses `worker-start --retry-of`; every retry is a new Task from `task-create --spec` with its own report directory (`:73`). Orca's breaker works on a Dispatch's failure count: the state `circuit_broken` is set when the inherited count plus the new failure reaches 3 (`DISPATCH_CIRCUIT_BREAK_FAILURES`, `dispatch-circuit-breaker.ts:1`; `dispatch-completion.ts:166-167`). On `worker-start` a fresh start's Dispatch begins at count 0 and the count is inherited only along a `retryOf` chain (`worker-dispatch-start.ts:94,126-127`, written to `failure_count` by `dispatch-row-writer.ts:135`), and the refusal to retry a `circuit_broken` Dispatch sits inside the `if (params.retryOf)` branch (`:95-104`); so a coordinator that follows WC-6 (a fresh Task and a fresh start each time) reaches neither the state nor the refusal through `worker-start`. The per-Task accumulation (`MAX(failure_count)` over the Task's Dispatches) exists only in the low-level `createDispatchContext` path (`dispatch-context-store.ts:51-55`), whose non-test callers are the `dispatch` RPC (`dispatch-methods.ts:152`) and the retired scheduler (`coordinator-task-dispatch.ts:108`). The three-failed-starts comparison is the coordinator's own (`:75`) and no code performs it. [CODE-VERIFIED, ORCA-CHECKOUT]
2. **The wait (`:99-108`).** `orca orchestration check --wait --types status,dispatch,worker_done,merge_ready,escalation,handoff,decision_gate,question,heartbeat --timeout-ms 300000` (`:101`). `--types` only decides when a waiter wakes; a returned Delivery is the whole FIFO batch, replayed until `--ack` and shared by every wait on one Run inbox (`:103`). The coordinator handles every message in the batch and writes one run-notes line per message ID before `--ack`; if the notes write fails, the batch is not acked (`:104`). After three empty waits in a row it enumerates workers with `worker-list` (`:107`). Per-type duties: table A.
3. **The sweep (`:110-114`).** After every wake or timeout, at most once every 5 minutes: one `worker-list`, one `worktree ps --agent-wait`, one `stat` per worker contract path (WC-11), updating each worker's heartbeat record (arrival time, progress token (defined under Table B), declared window). Costly probes (transcript tail, screen read) run only for workers a trigger fired for. Deadline W is `max(10 min, 2 x observed median heartbeat interval)`, or the worker's `expect_silence_min`, capped at 45 minutes; a semantic change is a substantive change of the progress token, the contract file or the transcript content (a message arrival or a repeated identical read is not one); starting values: startup grace 10 minutes, a read-only probe after 10 minutes with no semantic change, a named state or an L5 report by 15 minutes after the last semantic change, an unreachable report after 10 minutes of continuous `unverifiable`. Every value is "a starting value, not a measured one" (`:97`); the coordinator keeps the values it uses in its run notes, a brief may override them, a worker may not. States: table B.
4. **Responses (`:131-192`).** Ladder, ack timeline, restart classes, resume note and the never-on-silence list: table C. Worktree, git and fan-in duties: table D.
5. **Admission (`:237-246`).** `succeeded`: dependents may start; `partial`: the coordinator records usability first; `failed` or `blocked`: no dependents; `guard refused:` with no contract: at most 2 brief fixes. An `/sc:implement` Task is the exception to the WC-6 retry (`:243`) and follows the stop and freeze procedure of decision D2 (`:248-269`): table E.

*Coordinator loop tables.* All five tables are NORMATIVE contract text (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md` at HEAD, 327 lines, lines re-read 2026-11-12); no code runs the loop, detects a state or sends a nudge. The Orca column of table B is `research/gap-P3-G01.md` S9 (ORCA-CHECKOUT, installed files identical per S8; it names what Orca can show, not what a coordinator does).

Table A: per-message-type duty (`:105-108`; no code implements this dispatch table).

| Type | Duty |
|---|---|
| `heartbeat` | Update the worker's heartbeat record; never a stall proof. |
| `status` | With an ack line: ack rules (table C). Naming a report path or saying the work is complete: sets S9 when the brief has no later `Command:` line, otherwise logged. Any other: logged. |
| `worker_done` | Open the contract. A second one for the same dispatch is logged and ignored. The coordinator never sends or synthesizes `worker_done` for a worker. |
| `question` | Answer with `reply --id`, or forward to the person; starts the ack clock. |
| `escalation` | L1, then L5. Orca itself also sends one when a worker's terminal exits, unless the exit was deliberate (item 11 of the worker-side list below). |
| `decision_gate` | Goes to the person. |
| `handoff` | Any new work is left to the coordinator's decision (WC-13). |
| `merge_ready` | Merge only on the person's instruction. |
| `dispatch` | Logged as unexpected. |
| unknown type | The sender goes to S11 and is shown to the person; never silently acked. |
| `worker_done`, `question`, `escalation`, `handoff`, `merge_ready` with a Task ID or Dispatch ID that differs from the sender's current ones | Quarantined: logged and shown to the person, never applied. |

Table B: liveness states S0-S11 (`:116-129`).

| State | Trigger (abridged) | Coordinator response | Orca-side evidence (gap-P3-G01 S9) and class |
|---|---|---|---|
| S0 starting | No progress token or transcript 10 minutes after start | L1, then L2 "begin your brief", then L5 by 15 minutes | When the preamble was accepted but the turn start was not seen, `worker-start` moves the worker from `starting` to `start_unknown` with stage `turn_start_unobserved` (`worker-dispatch-outcome.ts:107-132`, called at `worker-start-readiness-settlement.ts:107-121`) and returns a receipt with `state: outcome_unknown`, `stage: turn_start_unobserved` and `turnStart: unobserved`; its next commands are `worker-show`, `terminal read --screen` (left out for a structured or chat worker) and `worker-abandon` (`:122-146`); the grace is the contract's. CODE-VERIFIED states (ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`), grace contract-only |
| S1 progressing | Token changed within W and a semantic change within 10 minutes | L0 | Contract-only; Orca adds `projection.stage.activity` only when liveness is `live` (`orchestration-fleet-worker-projection.ts:236`) |
| S2 long step | A declared window, or transcript activity under 5 minutes old | L1 at 10 minutes even inside the window (the window defers only nudges and typed text); past it, L1, L3, then L5 by 15 minutes | Contract-only (token, window), as S1 |
| S3 idle at prompt | No token change for W, last transcript entry 5 minutes old or older with no `worker_done` in the final turn, empty prompt, no pending ask | L1, L2, L3; L4 only with a stall proof; a third idle episode goes to L5 | No Orca verdict; raw evidence is `worker-read --source transcript\|terminal`, and `nextAction: none` when the agent is `live` with no pending input (`orchestration-fleet-worker-projection.ts:168-174`). Contract-only |
| S4 blocked on a person prompt | `agentWait` non-null | Capture the screen; L5 at 5 minutes plus Orca's own delay, re-reported every 15 minutes; never answer the prompt | `observation.agentWait` of `worker-show`: non-null names a worker parked on a human-only prompt (source hook, prompt text or title), `null` = looked and found none, absent = never looked (`orchestration-worker-specs.ts:58`; `worker-observation.ts:140-148`). CODE-VERIFIED |
| S5 blocked in ask | An open `question` | Answer it, then the ack timeline | A pending `question_threads` row sets `pendingInput` and the `input` attention category (`worker-terminal-attention-query.ts:46-49`; `orchestration-fleet-attention.ts:49-51`); the worker's `ask` blocks on `dispatch:<id>` (`ask-methods.ts:195`). CODE-VERIFIED |
| S6 context exhausted | Token unchanged for W while heartbeats arrive, or the S3 trigger; screen text such as `Autocompact is thrashing` confirmed by a second read 5 minutes later | Interrupt with "write your handoff file now, then end your turn" (the WC-14 exception); if the turn ends without `worker_done`, L4 within the budget, otherwise L5 and ask the person for a recorded stop | Contract-only; no Orca verdict or field |
| S7 unreachable | Liveness `unverifiable` | L5 after 10 minutes; never L4 | `projection.liveness.verdict == unverifiable` with one of ten reasons (item 9 below); Orca's guide: `unverifiable` is always absence (`bundled-skill-guides.ts:4504-4509`), so "unreachable" means no fresh positive evidence, not an uncontactable process. CODE-VERIFIED reason set; how often a stalled worker shows which reason is [UNVERIFIED] |
| S8 exited | Liveness `exited` | Settle under WC-6, then L4 or L5 | `verdict == exited` with source `execution_host`, `resource_release` or `worker_stop`: durable proof (`orchestration-fleet-worker-projection.ts:59-71`); `nextAction.kind == recover` when unsettled with nothing pending (`:155-166`). Not produced by a silent worker. On a terminal exit Orca itself may already have failed the Dispatch and escalated (item 11 of the worker-side list below). CODE-VERIFIED |
| S9 finished but unreported | Contract present, no later `Command:` line, no `worker_done` within one sweep | L1 (read the contract), L2 "send `worker_done` per WC-5 with `--report-path <path>`", then L5 by 10 minutes after the nudge | Orca has no detector for "finished but unreported". `finished_unverified` needs process-turn, liveness or outcome facts that no production code writes (the only production writer records `worker_report`, `worker-report-settlement.ts:46`), so it is not produced. `outcome_unknown` is different: it is the derived default for an attempt with no accepted report and no live fact (`attempt-outcome-projection.ts:84-107,136-137`; read in production at `worker-terminal-attention-query.ts:95-103`; default at `worker-list-projection.ts:38`), so it says nothing about "finished but unreported", and the fleet layer replaces it with `succeeded`, `failed` or `in_progress` only when the worker or dispatch row decides one, and returns it unchanged otherwise (`src/shared/orchestration-fleet-outcome-resolution.ts:35-57`, re-read 2026-11-12; the unchanged return is `:57`). The contract's `stat` (WC-11, `:47`) is the only detector. Contract-only; ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`; `finished_unverified` unproduced [CODE-VERIFIED by grep of non-test `src` for `recordAttemptObservation`] |
| S10 settled | `worker_done` received | None | Accepted report: Dispatch `completed`/`failed`, `worker_report` fact, Task `completed`/`failed` (`worker-report-settlement.ts:93-94,205-267,282`); a repeat report is judged against the settled state: with the same outcome (Dispatch and Task already in the status that outcome sets; a `circuit_broken` Dispatch counts as `failed`) it returns `duplicate: true` (`:93-94,101-109`), with the other outcome it is rejected `inactive_dispatch` (`:115-135`), and when the Dispatch was `failed` or `circuit_broken` with `last_failure` `agent_prompt_stalled` and the Task is `failed` it is no duplicate in either outcome but is accepted as a correction (`:95-99,172-193,235-243`; `succeeded` sets Dispatch, Task and worker row to `completed`/`succeeded`, `failed` replaces `last_failure` with the report's result). ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`, `worker-report-settlement.ts` re-read 2026-11-12. CODE-VERIFIED |
| S11 unknown | The deciding evidence is stale or unknown | L1, then as the resulting state; no typed input while S11 | Maps to `unverifiable` and to an absent `agentWait` ("never looked"). CODE-VERIFIED |

*Progress token (read by the S0, S1, S3 and S6 triggers, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:118,119,121,124`, and recorded by the sweep's heartbeat record; S2 uses a declared window or transcript activity instead, `:120`; defined here once).* NORMATIVE, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:65`: the token is the current `Command:` line index plus a count that rises only when on-disk progress rises (WC-15), carried in a heartbeat's subject or phase text or in a `status` body line, together with `expect_silence_min` before a step expected to outlast the cadence; a heartbeat whose token has not changed shows that the process exists, not that the work progresses. It is a duty the contract places on the worker, not an Orca field: the heartbeat command in Orca's preamble is `--subject "alive"` with `--phase "<short: investigating|implementing|reviewing|waiting>"` and names no counter or `Command:` index (`IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/preamble.ts:77`, ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`, re-read 2026-11-12), so a worker that copies the preamble's heartbeat command unchanged carries no token, and whether a live worker sends one was never observed [UNVERIFIED]. With heartbeats off the preamble has no heartbeat command (`preamble.ts:63-66`), so no heartbeat exists to carry a token for that dispatch; S1 ("Token changed within W") and the heartbeat alternative of S6 ("Token unchanged for W while heartbeats arrive") then cannot fire, S3 ("No token change for W") holds trivially and so rests on its other conditions (`:119,121,124`), and with no observed heartbeat interval deadline W rests on its 10-minute floor (`max(10 min, ...)`, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:112`; derived from the formula, not run).

Table C: responses and records (`:131-192`).

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| L0 observe | Watch only; always. |
| L1 probe (`:151`) | Read-only: `worker-show`, `worker-read --source transcript`, a screen read, the contract `stat`; every sweep, at most 2 costly probes per triggered worker per sweep. |
| L2 nudge (`:152`) | Typed with `--wait-submit <seconds>` (the contract names the flag without its value, `:152,:161`; Orca requires a positive integer of at most 3600 and rejects a valueless flag, `IronOrca/src/cli/flags.ts:50-57,101-113` and `handlers/terminal-send.ts:19-28`, ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`; text at `:164`); the S6 variant is an interrupt plus a handoff request; at an identified prompt after L1 confirms S3, S9 or S6; 1 per incident, 2 per lineage, and a nudge with no turn start still counts. |
| L3 ask (`:153`) | Typed "Reply with `status`: current step, what you are waiting for" after L2 failed once and the state is still ambiguous; 1 per incident. |
| L4 restart with resume note (`:154`, `:157`) | Reconcile, stop the worker, settle it, set the old Task `failed --result cancelled`, create a new Task. Only on an Orca positive stall proof (`exited`, the process exit, or a transcript whose final agent turn sent no `worker_done`) or a person-directed cancellation with confirmed quiescence (WC-6), within 1 per rolling 60 minutes and 2 per lineage; the WC-6 three-failed-starts rule also applies, whichever is stricter. |
| L5 report to the person (`:155`) | The state, its evidence and what was tried; any person-only state or any cap reached; S4 is re-reported every 15 minutes. |
| Ack timeline (`:131-138`) | A1 at 0: record the answer as `pending_ack`. A2 at 5 minutes with no ack and no heartbeat showing the ask returned: L1 probe. A3 at 5 minutes, only when the probe positively shows an idle empty prompt: type ONE copy (text at `:143`) with `orca terminal send --text ... --enter --wait-submit <s>` (`<s>` is the seconds value: `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:135`; see the L2 row for Orca's limits), at most one per message ID; a worker with no terminal skips A3. A4 at 15 minutes: L5 report with the message ID, answer, receipt fields and probe results. Never interrupt; never type into a busy, S11 or S4 session; without a positive idle identification skip to A4. A duplicate ack is a no-op; an ack does not prove the answer was right; at settlement a pending ack closes as `unacked-at-settlement`. |
| Restart cause classes (`:159`) | `idle`, `context-exhausted`, `exited`, `unreachable-then-exited`; the same class twice means no plain restart; for `context-exhausted` the next Task reduces the load (splits the `Command:` lines or narrows the inputs) or the coordinator goes to L5. |
| Resume note (`:167-180`) | Placed in the new Task's brief after the guard line and before the `Command:` lines; the new Task gets its own report directory and the old one is read-only input. Fields: `Resume` (replaced Task, cause class, evidence source and time), `Lineage` and attempt number, Task ID and Dispatch ID of the replaced attempt, completed `Command:` lines (n of m) and contract paths, last progress token, handoff file path, answered message IDs already applied, uncertain effects (actions started but not confirmed, such as a push or message send), load rule (pieces of at most 80 lines), `Start at: Command: line <k>`. For `/sc:implement`, its own `E-RESUME-NEEDED` re-run takes priority. |
| Never on silence (`:182-192`) | On silence, a passed deadline, `unverifiable` liveness or screen text alone the coordinator never: stops a worker; creates a retry Task; releases a worker or a worktree; marks a Task failed; answers a permission or person-only prompt; writes or drafts a Ruling; sends a lifecycle message for the worker. Silence authorizes only L1, L2 and L3. |
| Open points the contract lists (`:194-200`) | What `check --wait` wakes on without `--types` (the coordinator always lists every type); the cause of answers a worker did not see (a narrow `--types` list may miss a `status` reply); whether Orca has an attention category for a permission prompt or compact thrashing; what Claude Code does with text typed while a tool runs; whether two consumers on one mailbox both get a batch (owner question C11). |

Table D: worktree, git and fan-in duties (`:218-235`, `:271-273`).

| Duty | Rule |
|---|---|
| Worktree creation (`:220`) | Every worktree is created by Orca at its default location; never raw `git worktree add`, never under `.dev/worktrees/`; one feature worktree per feature, on its feature branch, where the coordinator runs. Enforcement: none for these parts; the gate blocks only `orca worktree create` without `--agent` (5.3 ceilings). |
| Child worktree and setup (`:221`) | Child created with `--setup skip` (Orca's `worker-start` otherwise defaults a new worktree to `--setup run`, which runs the repository's own setup hooks). `--setup run`, `make sync-dev` or any other repository target or hook only when the user's instruction in the coordinator's own conversation names that step, confirmed with the person at the moment of action unless that instruction is in the live conversation. A child is dispatched only after its setup succeeded. |
| Placement (`:222-223`) | A placement map (which Task writes in which worktree, sole writer or not) in the run notes and each `worker-start --comment`; a Task in a child gets an absolute report directory inside that child, read by absolute path before merge-back; a resume runs in its existing child by `--worktree id:<worktree id>`. |
| Merge-back and removal (`:224-225`) | After a child's result is admitted: merge the child branch into the feature branch, `orca worktree rm`, delete the branch; a discarded experiment is removed unmerged; a merge conflict is stopped and reported, never resolved automatically. Never removes a worktree or branch holding unmerged commits, an open PR, uncommitted changes, an unfinished `/sc:implement` ledger or an unread report without the user's instruction; never runs one managed package in two worktrees. |
| Git and GitHub ownership (`:229-235`) | The coordinator performs every git and GitHub outward action, install, VM and paid run, only when the user's instruction names it (confirmation as above); every such action targets `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`, never the upstream and never through Orca's issue or PR linking while Orca records the project as the upstream; progress posts are such actions; PRs only through `/sc:pr-submit --monitor 3` in an ordinary top-level session (decision D3); before any push that would publish worker commits the person has not seen, show them and push only on the person's go (a commit grant covers one Task; its conditions are in Section 5.1, How It Works, item 3); commit or merge in a worktree only when no worker runs there and no `/sc:implement` plan there is frozen, compare `git status --porcelain` with the settled contracts, stage explicit paths plus `.dev/tasks`, commit with `git commit --only -- <paths>`, treat "nothing to commit" as success, stop on any hook rewrite, hook failure or conflict, never `--no-verify`; the plan or brief records who owns each PR. |
| Fan-in (`:273`) | For N input Tasks feeding one merge Task: wait until all N have settled; admit an input only if `succeeded`, or `partial` judged usable; each `blocked` or `failed` input is answered or retried as a new Task before the merge, or dropped with the reason recorded; N is at most the merge command's maximum of 10 and a re-run replaces its input; create the merge Task only with at least 2 admitted inputs and list every dropped input and reason in its brief; with fewer than 2 there is no merge and the coordinator reports the single result or the failure. |

Table E: `/sc:implement` stop and freeze (decision D2; `:243`, `:248-269`). NORMATIVE contract text re-read 2026-11-12. No coordinator-side code or script performs the freeze, the hand-back or the seven checks; the only implemented parts are the executor-side ledger and ladder in `sc-implement-protocol` (`research/02` 5.3).

| Item | Rule |
|---|---|
| Decision and exception (`:57`, `:243`) | Only the person gives a Ruling (D2); a Ruling halt (`E-HALT-QA`, `E-NO-RULING`) is never asked. A `failed` or `blocked` `/sc:implement` Task is not retried under WC-6, because a WC-6 retry of a Ruling-halted plan would start an operator review by automation, which `/sc:implement` forbids (`src/superclaude/skills/sc-implement-protocol/refs/qa.md`, "Closed list of interrupts"). |
| Freeze, definition (`:250`) | A plan is frozen when its latest implement contract is `blocked`, or `failed` with any STOP code other than `E-RESUME-NEEDED`, or when the ledger's first non-complete task has `halt` or `blocked` as its last record with no eligible Ruling after it. |
| Freeze, consequences (`:251-253`) | The coordinator shows the person the contract and the full halt text and dispatches no Task for that plan; no Task that writes outside its own report directory goes into the halted worktree; no git command that changes HEAD, the index or the working tree runs there; the halted worktree is not merged or removed. |
| `E-RESUME-NEEDED` (`:255`) | The one case the coordinator may re-run without the person: the same command in the same worktree, only when the ledger shows no freeze. |
| Lifting (`:256-260`) | Only the person's explicit hand-back recorded in the coordinator's run notes and the ledger in the matching state. "Ruling written" requires the seven-condition check below; "session ended" (the person ran `/sc:implement` in their own session) requires the halted task `complete` and the ledger's last line to parse, then the drift baseline is re-recorded. After a stop with no halted task (for example `E-ARCHIVE-BLOCKED`) only the person's recorded instruction lifts it; after the hand-back the person makes no edit in that worktree until the resume Task settles. |
| Check (1) (`:262`) | The worker has settled (WC-6). |
| Check (2) (`:263`) | After the later of the halted task's latest `halt` and `blocked` record, the only new lines are one Ruling line per halted task named in the contract; each has the task's id, ends in `\n` with no `\r`, and matches the Ruling line regex (`src/superclaude/skills/sc-implement-protocol/refs/ledger.md:62-71`). |
| Check (3) (`:264`) | No field is blank, whitespace-only, punctuation-only or an angle-bracket placeholder, and the line has exactly two em-dash separators (the contract writes each as a space, the em dash character and a space). |
| Check (4) (`:265`) | The line is eligible under either clause of the Ruling eligibility rule (`src/superclaude/skills/sc-implement-protocol/refs/retry.md:498`). |
| Check (5) (`:266`) | No `halt` after the task's latest `start` has `reason=dispute`, and the latest `halt` reason is not `no-evidence`, `no-snapshot` or `head-moved`. |
| Check (6) (`:267`) | `HEAD` equals `<retries>/T<id>/q0-head` (`refs/retry.md:41`) and the paths in the `files=` field of the latest `blocked` line are unchanged against the tree its review graded; a missing `q0-head` or graded tree fails. |
| Check (7) (`:268`) | The resume brief has no `--force`. On any failed check the coordinator tells the person which condition failed and sends them to run `/sc:implement` in their own session; it never edits the ledger or the worktree. |
| Placement (`:269`) | The resume Task starts in the halted worktree by selector (`worker-start --worktree path:<absolute worktree path>`), never in a new or child worktree; the person, when running `/sc:implement` themselves, runs it from that worktree with the same absolute plan path. |
| Ceilings | Ruling authorship is not machine-checked (the ledger check verifies ordering only; L17, owner question C10). `/sc:implement` has no worker-contract producer yet (L29, P4). |

*Worker-side half (ORCA-CHECKOUT; INSTALLED-FILES identical except where noted).*

6. **Preamble.** The worker is told, per dispatch, to send `worker_done --task-id --dispatch-id --outcome succeeded` (or `failed`) exactly once (`preamble.ts:106`), to heartbeat only when the setting is on (`:77`; with `heartbeatIntervalMinutes` null the preamble has no heartbeat command at all), to `ask --timeout-ms 600000` and resume by message ID on timeout (`:118`), and to run `check --terminal <h> --json` then `--ack <delivery_id>` at checkpoints (`:128,133`). The CLI word is `devMode ? 'orca-dev' : (cliCommand ?? 'orca')` (`:54`). The preamble has no `Dispatch ID is:` line, no `check --wait` command (its heartbeat comment names `check --wait` once, `preamble.ts:69`), and no mention of IronClaude, `return-contract.yaml` or WC rules (gap-P3-G01 S1).
7. **Heartbeat setting.** Default on, interval 5 minutes, range 1-60 (`orchestration-heartbeat.ts:10-12`), baked into the preamble at dispatch time; the installed defaults are identical (per G01 S8). The running setting is [UNVERIFIED] DEPLOYED-RUNNING.
8. **Heartbeat recording.** Orca keeps only `dispatch_contexts.last_heartbeat_at` for the current Dispatch (visible as `worker-show --json` `dispatch.lastHeartbeatAt`); a late heartbeat from a settled Dispatch is suppressed. The 2x-interval stale warning exists only in the retired scheduler. Heartbeat age as a stall signal is IronClaude policy applied to a raw timestamp, not an Orca verdict (G01 S4, C12).
9. **Liveness evidence a coordinator can read.** `worker-show` returns two words: `projection.liveness.verdict` (fleet agent liveness: `live`, `exited`, or `unverifiable` with a reason) and `observation.status` (PTY liveness); text mode prints only the first (G01 S5). `unverifiable` is "always absence" (Orca guide `bundled-skill-guides.ts:4504-4509`) and carries one of ten reasons: `missing_status`, `stale_status` (no agent-hook activity for 30 minutes, `agent-status-freshness.ts:4`), `restored_unconfirmed`, `future_status`, `unsupervised_settled`, `host_unavailable` (also for a local chat-session worker), and four federated reasons. `exited` is durable proof of stop or release. [CODE-VERIFIED, ORCA-CHECKOUT]
10. **The 60 s window is not fed.** `DEFAULT_FRESH_AFTER_MS = 60_000` and a `finished_unverified` outcome exist (`attempt-outcome-projection.ts:11`), but the only production facet writer records `worker_report`; the `liveness`, `process_turn` and `outcome` facets are written only in tests, in the checkout and in the installed chunk (G01 S3, S8). Nothing should be documented as relying on `finished_unverified`. [CODE-VERIFIED by whole-`src` search]
11. **Settlement.** A valid `worker_done` moves the Dispatch and Task to `completed` or `failed` and, only for a `succeeded` outcome, promotes ready dependents `pending` to `ready` (`worker-report-settlement.ts:279-281`); it starts nothing. A report can be rejected (`unknown_task`, `unknown_dispatch`, `task_dispatch_mismatch`, `inactive_dispatch`, `stale_dispatch`); a repeat `worker_done` for one Dispatch is a duplicate only when its outcome equals the settled one; the other outcome is rejected `inactive_dispatch`, and a report after an `agent_prompt_stalled` failure is accepted as a correction (the three cases are in the S10 row of Table B, Section 5.2; `worker-report-settlement.ts:93-135`, ORCA-CHECKOUT; G01 S7). **Settlement without a report: a worker's terminal exit.** Orca also settles a Dispatch itself when the worker's terminal exits, without waiting for the coordinator (ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`, read not run). For an active Dispatch on the exited surface, `failActiveDispatchOnExit` (`terminal-resize-listeners.ts:53-73`; called from `pty-exit-lifecycle.ts:300-303` unless an abnormal SSH surface is preserved, and from `terminal-pane-mail-routing.ts:217` when an operator closes a chat worker) calls `failDispatch`: the Dispatch becomes `circuit_broken` when `failure_count + 1` reaches 3 and `failed` otherwise (`dispatch-completion.ts:167`, `DISPATCH_CIRCUIT_BREAK_FAILURES = 3` at `dispatch-circuit-breaker.ts:1`), the worker row becomes `failed` with stage `process_exited` (`:198`), and a Task that is `dispatched` with no other pending or dispatched Dispatch goes back to `ready`, or to `failed` when the Dispatch is `circuit_broken` (`:206-221`). Unless the exit was deliberate (`isDeliberateTerminalExit`, `terminal-resize-listeners.ts:66`), Orca also inserts a high-priority `escalation` message (`worker-failure-escalation.ts:56-57`) to the mailbox `dispatchReportMailbox` gives (`terminal-resize-listeners.ts:147`: the creator Dispatch or `run:<id>`), with the subject "Agent exited unexpectedly (...)" and a body saying the task "is ready to be dispatched again" (status `failed`) or "will not be retried automatically" (`circuit_broken`) (`:148-156`). A worker whose stop is already in flight in this runtime is not failed again (`:131-137`), and a Codex session that loses its app server is settled the same way with the subject "Agent session lost" and stage `agent_session_lost` (`:75-109`). The contract's S8 and L4 are written as if the coordinator settles the old Task; whether the running Orca has already failed the Dispatch and reopened the Task by then is [UNVERIFIED] (the installed bundle was not compared for this path).
12. **Retired scheduler.** `class Coordinator` still exists but is fenced three ways: CLI handlers throw `orchestration_migration_required` (`dispatch-handlers.ts:73-83`), the RPC contract retires `orchestration.run` and `orchestration.runStop` (`orchestration-rpc-contract.ts:45`), and `orchestrationMigrationFence` rejects them before any handler (`orchestration-contract-fence.ts:20`). Reachability was traced in the checkout; the installed bundle was checked textually only. Not a DEFECT: nothing in IronClaude claims it runs.

**Public Interface:**

```text
# Coordinator verbs named by the contract (abridged; the contract names the dispatch preamble as the syntax authority for worker-side verbs, WORKER-CONTRACT.md:7;
# the flags below are as the contract writes them, and the CLI spec requires more: worker-start needs (--agent <agent> | --terminal <handle>), worker-read needs --dispatch <id>;
# CLI spec: IronOrca/src/cli/specs/orchestration-worker-specs.ts, ORCA-CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12)
orca orchestration task-create --spec <text> [--deps <json_array>]
orca orchestration worker-start --task <id> [--worktree new-child --base-branch <ref> --setup skip] [--comment <text>]
orca orchestration check --wait --types <list> --timeout-ms 300000 | --ack <delivery_id>
orca orchestration reply --id <message_id> --body <text>
orca orchestration worker-list | worker-show --dispatch <id> --json | worker-read --source transcript|terminal
orca worktree ps --agent-wait          # installed files only, see contradictions
# Worker verbs in the dispatch preamble (preamble.ts:77-133, ORCA-CHECKOUT): send --type worker_done|heartbeat|escalation, ask, check
#   (heartbeat :77, worker_done :106, ask :118, escalation :122, check :128 and :133)
# Not in the preamble: the `status` message type (valid per the CLI spec, src/cli/specs/orchestration.ts:74; the ack lines are instructed by WC-12, WORKER-CONTRACT.md:61)
#   and `reply` (the preamble carries it only in its SUB-DISPATCH section, preamble.ts:231, present only when canDispatchSubWorkers is true; the coordinator's `reply --id` use is contract text)
```

**Dependencies:**

| Depends On | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Running Orca build | External runtime | Issues the preamble, mailbox and settlement; its version and settings were not observed. |
| `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` read by the coordinator | Text | Not auto-loaded; `install_core.py:27` installs it; `HOW-WE-WORK.md:396-406` points to it but omits WC-6, liveness and the freeze rules. |
| `sc-implement-protocol` refs (`qa.md`, `retry.md`, `ledger.md`) | Skill text | Halt table, Ruling rule, `q0-head`, ledger regex the freeze check cites. |

**Consumers:**

| Used By | How |
|---|---|
| Person-opened coordinator session | Follows section 4 as instructions. |
| Worker session | Follows the preamble plus WC-3/5/10/12/14/15 (5.1). |
| `/sc:implement` | Executor-side ledger and ladder; the coordinator-side freeze has no script. |

**Conventions & Patterns:**

- The preamble is the authority for verb syntax and cadence (`:7`); the contract never copies them.
- Silence authorizes only read-only probes, a nudge and a question, never a stop, retry or release (`:182-192`).
- Orca guidance is mirrored with one deliberate override: Orca's guide recommends `--retry-of`, WC-6 forbids it (`:73`).

**Evidence and contradictions:**

|---|---|---|
| `worktree ps --agent-wait` (`:110`) | ORCA-CHECKOUT `core.ts:154` lacks the flag; INSTALLED-FILES `cli/specs/core.js:157-158` accepts it | State divergence between two Orca builds 1048 commits apart, not a DEFECT. Wording: "accepted by the installed build files read on this host; absent from the development checkout `dc57b3d6e`; the running build was not checked". |
| Contract label "two consumers on one mailbox" UNVERIFIED (`:200`) | Both Orca help texts say listeners get the same Delivery (`orchestration.ts:120`, `cli/specs/orchestration.js:118`) | Label is stale-conservative (owner question C11); running build unobserved. |
| `:78` "task on the command line" | Terminal delivery is a typed agent turn (`deliver-worker-dispatch-preamble.ts:102-109`, G01 S10) | Timing claim stays [UNVERIFIED]. |
| Section 5 template keeps P0 wording (`:295,303-307`; pin coverage in this row) | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] stale | Pinned only in part by tests: the `P0 only: Report file:` line `:295` and the `--report-dir` "from P1" fragment of `:307` (`test_worker_contract.py:360-367`); `:303` and `:305` have no pin (grep of the test); 31 commands are converted. |

**Extension points:** To add an S-state or ladder rung: edit `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` section 4, extend `RULE_PHRASES` in `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` (rows S0-S5, S7, S8 and the W formula are currently unpinned, `research/02` G15), run `make sync-dev` and reinstall. To change the heartbeat cadence: an Orca setting, not IronClaude code. A coordinator program: N/A, not built; the loop is a person-directed LLM session by design.

### 5.3 Hook Guards and Job Identity

**Purpose:** Three shell hooks give the only IronClaude-side machine checks around Orca use: a worktree gate that requires `--agent` on `orca worktree create`, a launch-level guard that limits sessions bound by the `/sc:issue --claim` launch marker, and a Linux job-identity adapter that wraps those sessions' Bash calls. None of them identifies a worker or a coordinator (5.1). The launch-level guard recognizes the Orca CLI only under the name `orca`; that is a recorded DEFECT (Section 8.2), not a fixed one.

**Key Files:**

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` | PreToolUse(Bash) worktree gate. Header `:1-61` states scope and ceilings; orca-word test `:145`; verdict `:349`; block message `:351-358`. Working tree carries an uncommitted message change (`:354-355`). |
| `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/launch-level-guard.sh` | Launch-level guard. Fail handling `:147-163`; governed-session lookup `:257-273`; `orca` arm `:458-478`; decision matrix `:722-742`; default deny `:747`. |
| `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/launch-job-identity.sh` | Bash header plus embedded Python. `LEVELS` `:74`; hook mode `:414-489`. |
| `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` | Registrations: guard on UserPromptSubmit `:63-71` and PreToolUse `:115-124`; gate `:105-114`; identity `:125-134`. |
| `src/superclaude/cli/install_hooks.py` | Lists the three scripts in `_FRESHNESS_SCRIPTS` (`:88,90,93`); copy and merge (5.6). |
| `docs/designs/sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md` | NORMATIVE design: status `:6,10`, failure table `:216`, residual risks `:440`. |
| `tests/hooks/test_require_orca_worktree_agent.py`, `test_launch_level_guard.py`, `test_launch_level_guard_registration.py`, `test_launch_level_guard_with_identity.py`, `test_launch_job_identity.py`, `test_launch_level_observations.py` | Read, not run in this research. 12 live observations are skipped tests (`test_launch_level_observations.py:79-84`). |

**How It Works:**

*Registration.* All entries are `"type": "command"` with the installed flat path `~/.claude/hooks/<name>.sh`. The Bash PreToolUse group is, in array order, `block-bare-python.sh` (timeout 3, `hooks.json:95-104`), the worktree gate (3, `:105-114`), the guard (5, `:115-124`, matcher `^(Bash|Skill|Agent|Task|Edit|Write|NotebookEdit|Monitor|Workflow|RemoteTrigger|SendMessage|Cron[A-Za-z]*|Schedule[A-Za-z]*)$|^mcp__.*(merge|push|commit|pull_request|create_or_update_file|delete_file|update_ref|create_ref|create_branch)`) and identity (5, `:125-134`). The guard also runs on every UserPromptSubmit (5, `:63-71`). No registration sets `onFailure` (pinned by `test_launch_level_guard_registration.py:63-65`). The array position is a registry property, not a documented execution order (see "Documented host behavior"). No project-level registration exists; the guards run only if the user-level `~/.claude/settings.json` registers them, which `superclaude install` does (5.6) and which this host's settings show (HOST-INSTALLED, `research/03` B.4).

*Worktree gate (`require-orca-worktree-agent.sh`).* jq extracts `tool_input.command` for `tool_name == "Bash"` (`:345-347`) and pipes it to one stateful awk scanner (`:74-340`). The scanner collects each simple command's words, skips assignments, wrappers (`env sudo nice nohup time exec timeout command coproc`) and keywords, and takes the basename of the command word; the command is tracked as an Orca command only when `b == "orca" || b ~ /^\$/` (`:145`). At each separator it prints `orca` only when the first two non-option arguments are `worktree create`, no word is `--agent`, `--agent=...`, `--help` or `-h` (`:99-103`). Block is exit 2 with a five-line message (`:351-358`); anything else is exit 0. A flag in one command never satisfies another. Fail open (exit 0): `jq` or `awk` missing (`:65-72`), empty or non-JSON input, non-Bash tool (so a Monitor call is never scanned), missing command, scanner error. Header-listed ceilings (`:39-61`) include `bash -c`/`eval`/`env -S`/`ssh` strings, `xargs orca`, `find -exec orca`, quoted `--agent`, built command words, global options before `worktree`, non-Bash tools, `claude --bare`, humans, and scripts that call Orca as a subprocess (`orca_worktree.py` runs Orca itself, so its own create is outside the hook). It also does not block raw `git worktree add`, paths under `.dev/worktrees/`, `--setup run`, or removal of a worktree that holds unmerged work (the script has no match for `git worktree`, `worktree add`, `.dev/worktrees`, `setup`, `worktree rm` or `unmerged`, grep 2026-11-12; `research/02` 5.4), although the project rule says never to create a worktree that way; those parts of `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:220-225` are text only (table D in 5.2). The hook contains no role, session or environment predicate; the block message's "workers cannot create worktrees" (`:355`) is advice, not a test. Recorded ceiling, not a new defect and not fixed: in a launch-level-governed session the identity hook replaces the whole Bash input and wraps the original command as one quoted argument of `execute`, and the live order of the gate and the identity hook is unobserved; if the gate were shown the rewritten input, `orca worktree create` would sit inside one quoted argument, the scanner would see no `orca` command word, and the gate would allow the create (HISTORICAL task artifacts `.dev/tasks/done/TASK-WF-enforceTheOwnerP-20261108-031809/artifacts/t2-launch-identity-interaction.md:6-13,25-31` and `t8-delivery.md:28`; [UNVERIFIED], no live check was run, `t8-delivery.md:32-38`). Only the original-command case is tested: `tests/hooks/test_require_orca_worktree_agent_governed.py:31-56` feeds the gate a bare create from a governed session and expects exit 2 (CHECKOUT, read, not run).

*Launch-level guard (`launch-level-guard.sh`).* Governs only a session whose top-level `session_id` has a file under `$HOME/.claude/state/launch-level/sessions/<sid>` (`:257`); everything else is ungoverned and never blocked. The record content selects the level: `worktree` (W), `full` (F), `full-submit` (FS); any other content gives W plus the LL-STATE note; `<sid>.ok` lifts W to WA (`:264-272`). A session becomes governed when its first prompt carries `[sc-launch:v1:<32 hex>]` and `pending/<nonce>` exists as a regular file; the nonce is consumed with one `mv` and published with a hard link, so a binding cannot be replaced (per research/03 C.2). Each tool call gets zero or more classes (BIG, TAMPER, MERGE, GHWRITE, PROTECTED, ESCAPE, MCP, UNKNOWN, SUBMIT, REPO, PUSH, PUSHFORM, COMMIT, TURN, IMPL, SKILL); the first blocked class in that precedence order denies (`:722-742`). Classification is textual: `bash -c "git commit -m x"` and `echo "git commit"` both count as a commit, and the accepted leaks (variables, escapes, `bash ./push.sh`) are pinned by tests (`test_launch_level_guard.py:3602-3622`). The `orca` arm (`:458-478`) reads the next two non-option words; the allow list is `status`, `help`, `--version`, `worktree current|list|show`, `terminal list|show|read` and `orchestration check|inbox|run-current|task-list|worker-show`; every other `orca` command adds ESCAPE, which is denied at every level (`:737`), unless the segment ends in `--help` or `-h` (`:477`). Consequence for orchestration, as source behavior and not as a guarantee: for a governed session the guard's classification (CHECKOUT, `:458-478`, `:737`) denies `worker-start`, `dispatch`, `terminal send`, `agent launch` and `worktree create` when the shell word is exactly `orca`, because none is on the allow list. That is not a promise that no such mutation happens. The ceilings are: a hook stopped at its timeout lets the call go ahead (L21, 10.3); identity loss on a tool call is allowed silently (10.3); the classifier reads text and has pinned accepted leaks (L24); other names for the Orca CLI are not classified, which is the recorded DEFECT (8.2, L14); documented routes skip hooks altogether (L22); and no live governed Claude Code session was observed (L04). A supervised worker or a person-opened coordinator is not bound by any level.

*Failure behavior.* The EXIT trap (`:147-163`): exit 0, or exit 2 after a deliberate deny, passes; any other exit is an internal failure, which for a governed session prints LL-BROKEN (LL-NOBIND on the prompt branch) and exits 2, and for any other session exits 0. The qualifier that must travel with the phrase "fail closed" is stated once, in Section 10.3. Identity loss (no valid `session_id`) is a declared fail-open limit, and its effect depends on the event (CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12): on PreToolUse the call is allowed silently (`:390-394`); on UserPromptSubmit a prompt with no launch marker is allowed (`:366-367`), but a prompt that carries a launch marker (strict, or leading) and has no valid `session_id` is denied with LL-NOBIND (`:366-370`), and an envelope `jq` cannot parse that contains `[sc-launch:` and no `tool_name` key is denied the same way (`:233-236`); `agent_id` is never read on PreToolUse. A hook stopped at its timeout never reaches a deny: in a probe a TERM during stdin read or during a 64 KB governed command ended with exit 143 and no output, a KILL with 137 (gap-P3-G06 section 3, single runs). The design records "call goes ahead" as residual risk R2 (`sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:216,440`).

*Job identity (`launch-job-identity.sh`).* "Linux same-user job identity, not a sandbox" (`:2`). Modes: no argument or `hook` (PreToolUse Bash adapter), `execute SESSION TOOL_ID COMMAND`, `prepare SESSION COMMAND [TOOL_USE_ID]`, `run TOKEN`, `identify [PID]`. In hook mode, for a session with a store record it validates the event and rewrites the Bash command to `/bin/bash <script> execute <sid> <tool_use_id> <original>` through `hookSpecificOutput.updatedInput`, with no `permissionDecision` (`:475-483`); any validation failure prints "launch job: governed Bash call cannot be wrapped safely" and exits 2 (`:484-486`), including a non-string `agent_id` or `agent_type` (`:448-450`). It ignores non-Bash tools, so Monitor calls are not wrapped (`:426-428`), and unbound sessions (`:429-437`). `LEVELS` is the three words (`:74`); a `degraded` record (which the guard writes for a leading marker with a missing nonce, and reads as W) makes the identity hook block every Bash call, pinned by `test_launch_level_guard_with_identity.py:186-212`; open question C12. `identify` has no runtime consumer in this checkout; its consumer, `launch-level-pre-push.sh`, exists on ORIGIN/MASTER only.

*DEFECT: the Orca CLI is recognized only under the name `orca`.* Both scripts test the last path component of a shell word against exactly `orca` (gate `:145`; guard `:458`). The statement, the probe table, the reasons it is not exotic, the disposition and the remediation-plan link are in Section 8.2 and are not repeated here. Nothing is fixed, merged or deployed; the worktree gate stays a documented ceiling.

*Documented host behavior (DOCUMENTED; not observed on this host).*

- Timeout: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks says a `PreToolUse` command hook that reaches its timeout is cancelled, its output discarded and the call is not blocked unless `onFailure: "block"` is set (needs Claude Code v2.1.295 or later). Neither guard registration sets it. This matches the design's V8. The signal used and any message shown are undocumented [UNVERIFIED].
- Exit codes: exit 2 blocks and stderr is the reason; exit 0 applies stdout JSON; another code with plain-text, empty or invalid stdout is a non-blocking error; another code with schema-valid JSON is decided by the JSON. "Never exit 1" is the repository's convention, not a documented requirement.
- Order: matching hooks run in parallel, all run to completion, decisions merge `deny` > `defer` > `ask` > `allow`; several `updatedInput` rewrites are neither merged nor ordered (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide, "Limitations"). Describe hook order as unspecified, documented as parallel. A `deny` or exit 2 blocks in every permission mode, `bypassPermissions` included.
- Subagents: hooks from settings, managed policy and plugins fire for subagent tool calls, with optional `agent_id` and `agent_type` in the payload (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents). Whether a subagent payload's `session_id` is the parent's is undocumented [UNVERIFIED]; the guard's session binding assumes it (design ship gate U1; skipped test OB-H1 item 1).
- Bypass routes (documented, not observed as bypasses): `--bare` and `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE=1` skip all hook discovery including `--settings` hooks (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless, cli-reference, env-vars); `--safe-mode` skips user, project and plugin hooks but keeps managed ones; `disableAllHooks` outside managed settings disables non-managed hooks (the claim preflight already refuses it, `orca_worktree.py:977-982`); `allowManagedHooksOnly` limits hooks to managed ones. Managed-policy hooks under `--bare` are undocumented.

**Public Interface:**

```text
require-orca-worktree-agent.sh : stdin {"tool_name":"Bash","tool_input":{"command":"..."}}  -> exit 0 | exit 2 + stderr (ORCA WORKTREE BLOCK ...)
launch-level-guard.sh          : stdin PreToolUse or UserPromptSubmit payload (session_id, tool_name, tool_input, agent_id?) -> exit 0 | exit 2 + LL-* message
launch-job-identity.sh         : hook | execute SESSION TOOL_ID COMMAND | prepare SESSION COMMAND [TOOL_USE_ID] | run TOKEN | identify [PID]
```

**Dependencies:**

| Depends On | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `jq`, `awk` (gate); `jq`, `sed`, `tr` and coreutils (guard); `uv`, Linux `/proc`, pidfd, subreaper (identity) | Tools | Gate fails open without `jq`/`awk`; identity exits 2 for a bound session without `uv`. |
| `$HOME/.claude/state/launch-level/{pending,sessions}` | File store | Written by `orca_worktree.py` (5.4); read by guard and identity. |
| `~/.claude/settings.json` registrations | Host config | Installed by `install_hooks.py` (5.6); `disableAllHooks` switches them off. |

**Consumers:**

| Used By | How |
|---|---|
| Claude Code (PreToolUse, UserPromptSubmit) | Runs the hooks on tool calls and prompts. |
| `orca_worktree.py` `enforcement_preflight` | Checks the guard and identity registrations, not the gate (5.4). |
| `launch-level-pre-push.sh` (ORIGIN/MASTER only in the repository; an installed host copy exists at `~/.claude/hooks/launch-level-pre-push.sh` and equals ORIGIN/MASTER, `cmp` 2026-11-12; active use as a git hook not observed) | Calls `launch-job-identity.sh identify`. Not read by this research. |

**Conventions & Patterns:**

- Decision contract: allow is exit 0, block is one stderr message and exit 2, never exit 1; fail open for anything a hook cannot judge, except the guard's governed-session failure paths.
- Scripts are standalone flat files, so the gate's scanner is a derivative (`:74-340`, 267 lines counting the heredoc opener and closer, 265 lines of awk; 25 differing lines) of the `block-bare-python.sh` tokenizer, not a byte copy (the header's "copied" is [CODE-CONTRADICTED], low impact).
- Statements of the gate's reach name the literal `orca worktree create`.

**Evidence and contradictions:**

| Item | Class and tag | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Name `orca` only | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] DEFECT (guard) / ceiling (gate) | Section 8.2; the plan is a draft. |
| "Fail-closed for governed sessions" (research/03 C.3) | CHECKOUT, qualified by gap-P3-G06 | Holds for the script's own failure paths; not at a timeout, not on identity loss. |

| Gate timeout 3 s is pinned, but no TIMING test exists for the gate | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] debt | The value is pinned in both `hooks.json` trees by `tests/cli/test_install_hooks.py:827-846` (whole registration dict); `block-bare-python.sh` 3 s by `:765-780`; the identity hook 5 s, last in PreToolUse, by `:895-914`; the guard 5 s per event by `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard_registration.py:44-50`. G06 section 1.1 reported the gate's value as unpinned; the ranges above show the pin; the missing timing test is G06 R3. |

**Extension points:** To add a guarded verb or name: gate scanner `:145` and `track`/`fin` (`:94-103`), guard `orca` arm (`:455-478`) and the decision matrix (`:722-742`), then `tests/hooks/test_require_orca_worktree_agent.py` (`BLOCKED`, `ALLOWED` tables) and `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py` (ESCAPE and NONE grids around `:3093-3151`); then copy each edited script byte for byte over its plugin mirror by hand (keep mode 0755): the gate over `plugins/superclaude/hooks/scripts/require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` (pinned by `tests/cli/test_install_hooks.py:849-857`) and the guard over `plugins/superclaude/hooks/scripts/launch-level-guard.sh` (pinned by `:1085-1095`, assert at `:1093`); `make sync-dev` writes `.claude/` only and `superclaude install` writes `~/.claude/`. Which of the 13 mirrors have a byte-equality test, and the rule for a new mirrored script: Section 13.2. To add a hook script: add it to `_FRESHNESS_SCRIPTS` (`install_hooks.py:42`) and to `hooks.json`; `make verify-sync` requires every `hooks/scripts/*.sh` to be listed. The alternate-name change is exactly the remediation plan; do not duplicate it elsewhere.
