### 5.4 Launchers

**Purpose:** Starts non-worker (not supervised) Claude sessions through `orca agent launch`: the interactive `/sc:issue --claim` flow (`orca_worktree.py`), the issue watcher (`launch.py`; run by whatever scheduler the owner chooses and off by default, item 7), and the ccsession wrapper chain that names the launched tab and lets the claim verify it. Neither launcher starts a supervised worker, calls `worker-start`, or contains a role check.

**Key Files:**

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/superclaude/skills/sc-issue-protocol/scripts/orca_worktree.py` | Standard-library helper, 1,473 lines at HEAD. Subcommands `preflight` (`:192`), `worktree` (`:607`), `prompt` (`:746`), `launch` (`:1274`); `build_parser` `:1432`, `main` `:1464`. The only file in the skill that calls `orca`. |
| `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/launch.py` | Watcher's Orca adapter: `build_claim_prompt` (`:84`), `launch_argv` (`:95`), `find_orca` (`:163`), `check_origin` (`:233`), `outcome_of` (`:326`), `OrcaLauncher` (`:352`). Reached from `superclaude issue-watch once` (`cli/main.py:563-565`). |
| `src/superclaude/skills/ccsession-tag/{ccsession-orca,ccsession,hooks/session-name.sh,hooks/session-start.sh}` | Writer side of the tab title and tab record (per gap-P3-G05). |
| `src/superclaude/skills/sc-issue-protocol/refs/claim.md`, `SKILL.md` | Skill text that calls the script (Waves 0, 5, 7, 8). |
| `tests/skills/test_issue_orca_worktree.py`, `tests/issue_watch/test_launch.py`, `tests/unit/test_ccsession_tag.py` | Fake-Orca tests; no test starts a real Orca. |

**How It Works:**

1. **Claim flow (`orca_worktree.py`).** The caller is a Claude session running Bash from the skill, not a Python importer. Every outcome prints one JSON object and exits 0 (exit 2 is argparse only). `preflight` runs `orca status --json` and requires `ok` and `runtime.reachable` (`:192`). `worktree` finds an existing worktree by comment marker, recorded path, basename `issue-<N>` or branch, else runs
   `orca worktree create --repo path:<main> --name issue-<N> --base-branch <base> --no-parent --comment "sc:issue <repo>#<N>" --json` (`:666-682`).
   **There is no `--agent` and no `--prompt`** (docstring and test `test_fo18_exact_commands_and_no_forbidden_flag`, `test_issue_orca_worktree.py:837`). The reason recorded in the script and `refs/claim.md:101`: the issue directory and the pending launch record must be in place before an agent starts. The created path is read from a second `worktree list`, not from the create reply; for a created worktree the script then closes Orca's plain first tab (`close_first_tab`, `:550-604`) only when exactly one agent-less terminal is listed.
2. **Accepted exception to "every worktree gets an agent".** `core/CLAUDE.md:55` and project `CLAUDE.md:312` state the rule without exception; the agent-less create is recorded by owner decision of 2026-11-08 in `docs/designs/sc-issue-claim-pipeline-flag.md:11,194,380` ("The owner approved this first-tab close in place of 'agent on create'"). The approval is a sentence in a design document, not a primary owner message. Between worktree create and launch, or when launch is refused, a worktree can exist with its first tab closed and no agent. Owner question C1 (name the exception in the rule text) is open.
3. **Launch gates (`cmd_launch`, `:1274`).** In order: usable worktree record; duplicate-launch guard (record or `<git-dir>/sc-issue-launch-<N>.json` mirror, `already_launched` with no Orca call); stale-prompt check; `enforcement_preflight` (`:925`, IR-4: `jq`, `uv`, `cat sed tr mkdir mv ln rm`, Linux process interfaces, `~/.claude/hooks/launch-level-guard.sh` present with `# launch-level-guard-version: 1` (`launch-level-guard.sh:10`), `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` unset or `$HOME/.claude`, no `disableAllHooks`, guard registered on UserPromptSubmit and on PreToolUse for every tool in `GOVERNED_TOOLS`, identity hook registered for Bash); `l2_status` (`:1018`, warn-only); then `publish_pending(word)` (`:1077`) writes `pending/<nonce>` (32 hex from `secrets.token_hex(16)`, mode 0600) holding `worktree`, `full` or `full-submit`. IR-4 verifies the claim process's environment, not the agent's, and does not check the worktree gate's registration (`GUARD_COMMAND` and `IDENTITY_COMMAND` only, `:808-809`).
4. **The launch call (`:1351-1355`).** `orca agent launch --agent claude --worktree path:<wt> --surface terminal --prompt "[sc-launch:v1:<nonce>] Read .dev/issues/<N>/launch-prompt.md and follow it." --json`. When `orca agent launch` itself fails or does not report ok, the pending record is deleted and a hand command is printed with the warning that a hand-started agent is not governed (`:1357-1376`); an IR-4 refusal (`W-ENFORCEMENT-ABSENT`, `:1308-1325`) comes earlier, before the pending record is written, and prints no hand command. **A timeout is different** (CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12): the `detail` reads "orca agent launch timed out; an agent may have started, check Orca before re-running" (`:1359-1360`); the launch call's timeout is `DEFAULT_TIMEOUT` = 120 s (`:80,124-126`); the pending record is still deleted (`:1358`), but nothing is recorded, because only `record_launch` (`:1389`, reached after a successful launch) writes the `launch` entry, so the duplicate-launch guard (`:1283-1299`) cannot stop a second launch. What happens to an agent that did start depends on when its first prompt reaches the guard (derived from source, not run): if the prompt arrives before the delete at `:1358`, the guard has already consumed the nonce with one `mv` (`launch-level-guard.sh:376-378`) and bound the session normally at its requested level, and only the launch record is missing; if it arrives after the delete, the nonce is gone, so a prompt that leads with the marker is published as `degraded` (`:380-382`), which blocks every Bash call in the identity hook (Section 5.3). The safe order is therefore the same in both cases: check Orca (`orca worktree ps`, the worktree's terminals) and close any stray agent before re-running the claim. The guard consumes the marker once (5.3).
5. **Shim check (advisory).** After launch, `behind_ccsession(handle)` (`:1142`) tries `SHIM_TRIES = 5` times (`:780`), `SC_ORCA_SHIM_INTERVAL` seconds apart (default 2.0): `orca terminal read --terminal <h> --screen --json` for `SHIM_MARKERS = ("[ccsession]", "Session name (Enter or Ctrl+C")` (`:774`), then `orca terminal show --terminal <h> --json` and the title pattern `SHIM_TITLE = sc-launch-v1-[0-9a-f]{1,12}-\d{4}-\d{6}-[0-9a-f]{4}\b` (`:779`) against `result.terminal.title`. A miss, or no terminal handle, adds `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION`; status stays `launched`, the `launch` entry is still written and nothing closes the agent. Each read has a 15 s cap; with every call hanging the worst case is 5 x (15 + 15) + 4 x 2 = 158 s, otherwise about 8 s of pauses (arithmetic from the constants).
6. **Where the title comes from (ccsession).** `ccsession-orca` sees the claim prompt as one positional word, so no question is asked and `ccsession --auto-name "<prompt>"` builds `<folder>-sc-launch-v1-<12 hex>-<MMDD>-<HHMMSS>-<4 hex>` (words cut to 12 characters, folder to 20). The label is shown as `<label> · Claude Code` by an OSC escape at launch and by `orca terminal rename` from the SessionStart hook; both carry the same text. A valid `CCSESSION_NAME`, a Claude `--name`, `CCSESSION_AUTOSTART=0`, or an unwritable name-list directory yields a different or empty label and a false `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION` (gap-P3-G05 F9, causes 1-5).
7. **Watcher (`launch.py`).** `launch_argv` returns exactly 12 elements: `[orca, "agent", "launch", "--agent", "claude", "--worktree", "path:<worktree>", "--surface", "terminal", "--prompt", "/sc:issue --claim <n>", "--json"]` (`:95-116`). The prompt must match `^/sc:issue --claim [1-9][0-9]{0,8}$` (`:33`); `ORCA_AGENT = "claude"` is fixed (`:28`); origin must be `ironbellyorg/ironclaude` (`:50`, `check_origin` `:233`). `find_orca` probes `_ORCA_CLIS = ("orca-ide", "orca")` with `skills list --json` and accepts only an exit-0 reply whose `topics` include `orca-cli` (`:163`). The watcher never creates a worktree (it uses the configured checkout), writes no state itself, and records a pending entry before the call (`cycle.py` `_launch`). Its prompt carries no `[sc-launch:...]` marker, so the watcher-started session is ungoverned; it binds a second, marked agent only if that session runs the claim (Path B, inferred from two verified hops, not run end to end). **Off by default:** the config key `enabled` defaults to false (`cycle.py:228`) and a cycle that is not a `--dry-run` stops with `not_enabled` (`cycle.py:685-686`); the runbook says no cron entry is enabled and `enabled` stays false until an owner-run live test (follow-up FU-1) has passed (`docs/designs/issue-watcher-setup.md:5`), leaves the scheduler to the owner (`:202`) and treats the cron entry as follow-up FU-2, "this work does not enable it" (`:282`). The checkout ships no timer or service unit (`find` for `*.timer` and `issue-watch*.service`, 2026-11-12) and the live-test record that FU-1 is to produce (named `issue-watcher-live-test` in the runbook, `:5`, expected under `docs/designs/`) does not exist. Whether any scheduler runs `issue-watch once`, or the config on any host sets `enabled: true`, is [UNVERIFIED].
8. **Reply shapes.** `OPERATION_ID_PATHS` (`launch.py:42`) and `OP_UNKNOWN_TEXT` are marked "PROPOSAL, UNVERIFIED" in the code; whether real Orca replies carry `receipt.operation_id` is [UNVERIFIED]. Reply shapes of `agent launch`, `worktree create` and `terminal` in the claim script are fixture-only.

**Public Interface:**

```text
uv run --no-project python <skill>/scripts/orca_worktree.py preflight
uv run --no-project python <skill>/scripts/orca_worktree.py worktree --repo <owner/repo> --number <N> --base origin/<B> --source <D>      # all four required
uv run --no-project python <skill>/scripts/orca_worktree.py prompt --template <T> --level worktree|full [--submit] --number <N> --repo <owner/repo> [--source <D>] --out <path>
uv run --no-project python <skill>/scripts/orca_worktree.py launch --number <N> --source <D> --level worktree|full [--submit]            # --submit needs --level full (main, :1467-1468)
superclaude issue-watch once [--dry-run] [--config PATH]
superclaude issue-watch check-config [--config PATH]     # read-only: validates config, worktree, approvers file (cli.py:185-198); default PATH ~/.config/superclaude/issue-watch.json (cli.py:31)
superclaude issue-watch init-state [--config PATH]       # creates the state directory, store and lock; refuses if a store exists (cli.py:201-204)
superclaude issue-watch status [--json] [--config PATH]  # read-only health report; prints retry commands, never runs them (cli.py:164-168)
superclaude issue-watch reset ISSUE --forfeit-guarantee [--config PATH]   # forfeits the at-most-once launch guarantee of one issue (cli.py:282-313)
superclaude issue-watch reset --rate-limit [--config PATH]                # clears next_permitted_at instead (cli.py:289-293)
launch_argv(orca: str, worktree: Path | str, prompt: str) -> list[str]     # launch.py:95, 12 elements, ValueError on a prompt not from build_claim_prompt
find_orca(...) -> str | None   check_origin(...)   outcome_of(result: CommandResult) -> LaunchOutcome
```

(Subcommand flags above re-read from `build_parser`, `:1440-1460` (CHECKOUT, HEAD); the `issue-watch` lines re-read from `src/superclaude/cli/issue_watch/cli.py` on 2026-11-12 (CHECKOUT; configuration keys, limits and the scheduler status in Sections 9.1, 11.1b and item 7 above); the same grammar is printed at `refs/claim.md:99,118` and in the module docstring. There is no `--issue` flag: `worktree --issue 1 --repo a/b --base origin/x` exited 2 on 2026-11-12 with "the following arguments are required: --number, --source".)

**Dependencies:**

| Depends On | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Orca CLI (`orca`; the watcher also probes `orca-ide`) | External | The claim script uses the literal `orca` in every argv; it does not try `orca-ide`. |
| `launch-level-guard.sh`, `launch-job-identity.sh` and their registrations | Hooks (5.3) | IR-4 refuses a governed launch when absent (`W-ENFORCEMENT-ABSENT`). |
| Orca's Claude command set to `ccsession-orca` | Host setting | Manual step; neither installer nor doctor sets or checks it (5.6). Value on any host [UNVERIFIED]. |
| `~/.claude/state/launch-level/pending/` | File store | Handoff of the nonce to the guard. |

**Consumers:**

| Used By | How |
|---|---|
| `/sc:issue --claim` skill (Waves 0, 5, 7, 8) | Calls the script through Bash. |
| `superclaude issue-watch once` | Calls `OrcaLauncher.launch`; `status_report` prints a retry command (does not run it). |
| Launch-level guard | Binds the launched agent when the pending record is consumed. |

**Conventions & Patterns:**

- All subprocesses go through `run()`: no shell, captured output, timeouts (`DEFAULT_TIMEOUT` 120, preflight 30, terminal reads 15; `SC_ORCA_TIMEOUT` replaces every timeout).
- One JSON object per outcome, exit 0; warnings are codes; the eight `worktree` codes are ranked by `WARNING_ORDER` (worktree results only) and the `launch` codes each need a `REMEDY` entry (10.1c).
- Key environment variables (full 23-variable table: gap-P3-G05 F8, in Section 9.2):

| Variable | Default | Effect here |
|---|---|---|
| `ORCA_PANE_KEY`, `ORCA_TERMINAL_HANDLE` | unset outside an Orca terminal | Set by Orca at PTY spawn; ccsession needs both for tab title and record. |
| `CCSESSION_AUTOSTART` | `1` | `0` disables question and auto-name. |
| `CCSESSION_NAME` | unset | Valid name replaces the auto label and defeats `SHIM_TITLE`. |
| `CCSESSION_ASK_TIMEOUT` | `30` s | `0` or any non-digit (for example `30s`) means no limit. |
| `SC_ORCA_TIMEOUT` | unset | Float above 0 replaces every call timeout. |
| `SC_ORCA_SHIM_INTERVAL` | `2.0` s | Pause between the five tries. |
| `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` | unset | Claim preflight rejects any value other than `$HOME/.claude`; ccsession ignores it. |

**Evidence and contradictions:**

| Item | Class and tag | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Version: HEAD 1,473 lines versus ORIGIN/MASTER 1,884 lines (453 insertions, 42 deletions) | `git diff --numstat HEAD origin/master` (research/04 F5) | The installed `~/.claude` copy equals ORIGIN/MASTER. ORIGIN/MASTER adds an owned L2 pre-push hook with refusal off (`IR7_REFUSE = False`). This subsection describes HEAD; L2 statements are stale against ORIGIN/MASTER and the installed copy. The newer version was read only in its header and grep hits. |
| Stale second screen marker | CHECKOUT [CODE-CONTRADICTED] | `SHIM_MARKERS[1]` (`:774`) is the old question wording; the current text is `Session name (Enter = auto-named, Ctrl+C = cancel; ...)` (`ccsession-orca:200`). No effect on claim launches today (a prompt-carrying launch is never asked). Open question C17. |
| No test joins `--auto-name` output to `SHIM_TITLE` | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] debt | A change to `auto_name` could turn every claim into a warning. Open question C19. |
| Bare `orca` everywhere | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] | The claim script and ccsession (`ccsession:534,537,549-555`) use the bare name; the watcher resolves `orca-ide` first. See the DEFECT in Section 8.2. |
| Claim preflight and ccsession disagree on `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] | Open question C18. |
| `SHIM_TITLE` matches because of one live title copied into a test | HISTORICAL observation, 2026-11-08 | Title survival in `terminal show` is not otherwise pinned. In the Orca checkout a manual rename outranks the process title: `terminal show` builds the title as the manual title when one exists, else the process title (`pty-terminal-summary.ts:54-55`); the manual title is the saved tab's `customTitle`, else the rename's own title while no saved tab holds it (`:99-107`); a rename stamps the title and issues a custom `renameTab`, and its comment says a manual name "outranks later agent titles" (`main-layout-renames.ts:76-106`). Only the fallback process title is chosen by `getLatestPtyTitle` (`:54`, body not read). ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`, read not run; this replaces the earlier newest-wins reading of G05 R4. The code that stores the custom tab title and the legacy renderer-owned rename path were not read, so which path the shim check's `terminal show` takes on a given host is [UNVERIFIED]. |
| Orca-side: how the Claude command and prompt are joined; `ORCA_PANE_KEY` across restart; 60 s readiness failure semantics | ORCA-CHECKOUT read; host values [UNVERIFIED] | G05 R1, R2, R7. |
| Never-as-worker for `/sc:issue` is prompt text only | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] | No code in either launcher refuses a worker; `uv run ... orca_worktree.py` has command word `uv`, so the gate does not match it (header `:60-61`). |

**Extension points:** To add a launch step: `cmd_launch` gate order (`:1274-1409`) and a new warning code with a `REMEDY` entry (`:784-799`) appended to the `launch` notes (`WARNING_ORDER` is for `worktree` results only; `launch_result` does `REMEDY[code]`, so a launch code missing from `REMEDY` raises `KeyError`, `:1224-1228`), with tests in `test_issue_orca_worktree.py` (groups LA, IR). To change the watcher's prompt: only `build_claim_prompt` and `PROMPT_PATTERN` (`launch.py:33,84`); `test_security_invariants.py:204` pins the literal 12-element argv. To add an Orca binary name: `_ORCA_CLIS` in `install_user_setup.py:35` (imported by `launch.py`; parity pinned by `tests/issue_watch/test_find_orca_parity.py`); the claim script's literal `orca` is a separate edit. A supervised-worker launcher: N/A, not built.

### 5.5 Orca-Side Enforcement (Development Source)

**Purpose:** Records what Orca itself enforces about helper starts and report routing, read from Orca source. This is not IronClaude code. It matters because WC-13 (5.1) says a worker never starts workers "at any depth setting", while Orca's own controls differ by build: the development checkout refuses a worker's helper start by default and the installed build files do not. Source was read only; no Orca process or CLI was started.

**Key Files** (all ORCA-CHECKOUT, `/home/coder/workspaces/IronOrca`, `main`, commit `dc57b3d6e81c9825446a520bf0aee8600a2f5586`, clean tree, version 1.4.214):

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/main/runtime/rpc/methods/orchestration/worker/worker-start-authority.ts` | `resolveWorkerStartRun` (`:12`) and `assertHelperStartScope` (`:51`): who may call `worker-start` and what a worker may start. |
| `src/main/runtime/orchestration/dispatch-report-mailbox.ts` | `isDispatchReportType` (`:5`), `activeCreatorDispatch` (`:16`), `dispatchReportMailbox` (`:31`): where a helper's reports go. |
| `src/main/runtime/orchestration/db/runs/worker-run-binding.ts` | `findCallerWorkerDispatches` (`:11`), `workerRunBindingRefusal` (`:45`), `unboundWorkerMessage` (`:76`). |
| `src/shared/nested-worker-depth.ts`, `src/shared/default-global-settings.ts` | Depth cap default (`nested-worker-depth.ts:4`, `default-global-settings.ts:153`). |
| `src/main/runtime/orchestration/db/dispatch-depth.ts` | `resolveChildDispatchDepth` (`:165`, throws `nested_worker_depth_exceeded` when `childDepth > maxDepth`, per research/05 5.2). |
| `/opt/Orca/resources/app.asar.unpacked/out/shared/nested-worker-depth.js` | INSTALLED-FILES counterpart; build record in `out/package.json`. |

**How It Works:**

1. **Worker-start authority.** The Run's bound coordinator may call `worker-start` for its Run (`worker-start-authority.ts:21-30`; a different `--run` gives `consumer_fenced`). A caller that coordinates no Run must prove exactly one active, non-self Dispatch of its own; it then starts only a local helper in that Dispatch's Run (`:31-47`). Any other caller gets `consumer_fenced` ("worker-start needs the coordinator bound to the Run, or a worker on an active Dispatch in it"). `assertHelperStartScope` rejects `--on` and `--terminal` (`invalid_argument`) and any Task not last run by a helper of this creator Dispatch (`task_not_found`, "start new helper work with --spec") (`:56-69`). Only caller: the `orchestration.workerStart` handler (`worker/workers.ts:42,56`). [CODE-VERIFIED]
2. **Race and depth.** The creator is re-proven inside the creating transaction (`worker-dispatch-start.ts:137-147`, per research/05), and the child depth is `resolveChildDispatchDepth(params.creator, params.maxDepth)` (`:167`). The check is `childDepth > maxDepth` refuses. A coordinator-started worker has depth 1; a helper start from it has child depth 2.
3. **Depth default differs by build.**

| Source | Default `nestedWorkerMaxDepth` | Effect on a worker's helper start (2 > cap?) |
|---|---|---|
| ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e` | 1 (`nested-worker-depth.ts:4`; `default-global-settings.ts:153`) | 2 > 1: refused by the cap. |
| INSTALLED-FILES `1.4.245-aidev02.9cfecdd30b2a` | 2 (`nested-worker-depth.js:10`; `default-global-settings.js:161-162`; legacy 1 migrated to 2 once; file comment: upstream v1.4.251+ defaults to 4) | 2 > 2 is false: NOT refused by the cap; a helper's own helper (child depth 3) is. |
| Running Orca, stored setting | not read | [UNVERIFIED] DEPLOYED-RUNNING. |

   At the installed default the only barrier keeping a worker from starting a helper is WC-13 text, which no IronClaude code enforces (the gate does not inspect `worker-start`). Derived from reading two sources, not from a run. The preamble mirrors the cap: `canDispatchSubWorkers = dispatch.depth < maxDepth` (live sites `deliver-worker-dispatch-preamble.ts:51`, `dispatch-methods.ts:166,245`, `federation.ts:212`; `coordinator-task-dispatch.ts:123` is the retired scheduler's copy; ORCA-CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12), so at cap 2 a depth-1 worker is told how to start helpers (`preamble.ts:219-256`) while WC-13 forbids it. [CODE-VERIFIED text; which text a worker obeys is [UNVERIFIED]]
4. **Dispatch-report mailbox.** `worker_done`, `heartbeat`, `escalation`, `status` and untyped messages are dispatch reports (`dispatch-report-mailbox.ts:5-13`); gates and handoffs stay with the Run's coordinator. `dispatchReportMailbox` returns `dispatch:<creator id>` while the creator Dispatch is in the same Run and `pending` or `dispatched`, else `run:<run id>` (`:16-36`), recomputed on every send, so a helper's reports follow creator settlement without rewriting routes. Callers: `send-methods.ts:150-153`, `ask-methods.ts:113`, `worker-topology.ts:176`, `terminal-resize-listeners.ts:147`. Coordinator-started workers have no creator Dispatch, so their reports go to `run:<id>` (this part is an inference from `resolveCreatorDispatchId` returning null for a caller without an active non-self Dispatch, not a run).
5. **Worker-run binding.** `workerRunBindingRefusal` refuses a caller that works a Dispatch in a Run (or in a Run it descends from) binding that Run (`worker_cannot_coordinate_own_run`), and refuses a takeover flag from a caller working an active Dispatch (`worker_cannot_take_over_run`) (`worker-run-binding.ts:45-73`). Callers: the bind path (`run-binding.ts:49`) and `resolveRunScope` (`run-scope.ts:187`). It is narrow: it is NOT called on `orchestration.runCreate` (`runs/runs.ts:31-55`, `db/runs/run-create.ts:7-50`), so a worker with an active Dispatch can create a new Run and become its bound coordinator; the depth cap is then the only source barrier on what it starts (the earlier report records that a new Run does not reset delegation depth, HISTORICAL, not re-derived). Same source at the installed build's commit (`git diff --quiet` of `run-create.ts`: no difference); the bundle's `runCreate` handler head shows no worker refusal.
6. **Orca's federated own-Run preamble variant** (`buildOwnRunSubDispatchSection`, `preamble.ts:240-256`, selected only for remote-host preambles) tells a remote worker to `run-create` its own Run. It is Orca's text, untraced end to end, and not evidence that IronClaude ships sub-orchestration.

**Public Interface:**

```ts
resolveWorkerStartRun(runtime, args: { coordinator; from: string; callerSession; runId?: string }): { run: RunRow; helperCreator?: DispatchContextRow }
assertHelperStartScope(db, creator: DispatchContextRow, args: { task?: TaskRow; params: { on?: string; terminal?: string } }): void
dispatchReportMailbox(db, dispatch): string          // "dispatch:<id>" | "run:<id>"
workerRunBindingRefusal(db, caller, runId, attempted: { takeover: boolean; bind: boolean }): OrchestrationError | null
```

**Dependencies:**

| Depends On | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| `runtime.getNestedWorkerMaxDepth()` | Store setting | Read from Orca's settings store (`workspace-selectors.ts`, per research/05); not observable here. |
| Runtime-attested pane and incarnation identity | Orca internals | `resolveDispatchCreator` trusts runtime-attested identity; params are untrusted for remote matching. |

**Consumers:**

| Used By | How |
|---|---|
| `orchestration.workerStart`, send, ask, escalation, run-binding RPC paths | Live callers of every function above (none is dead code). |
| `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` WC-13 | Relies on these controls only partially; its ban is text (5.1). |

**Conventions & Patterns:**

- Orca fails closed for helper starts (`consumer_fenced`) and resolves report routes from current database state, not stored addresses.
- Always name the Orca build when stating a depth fact: checkout `dc57b3d6e` (1), installed files (2), running (unknown).

**Evidence and contradictions:**

| Item | Class and tag | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Checkout default 1 versus installed default 2 | ORCA-CHECKOUT and INSTALLED-FILES [CODE-VERIFIED, both] | Different builds of one repository, the installed commit 1048 commits ahead (`git rev-list --count`); neither value is wrong for its own build. |
| "Orca refuses a worker's helper start by default" | True for the checkout, false for the installed files | Never state it without the build. |
| Cited files identical at the installed commit except the depth default (`nested-worker-depth.ts`, `default-global-settings.ts`), the `runUse` takeover check (`runs.ts`) and one added line in `workers.ts` (`requires: ['accounts']` on `orchestration.workerStart`) | `git diff dc57b3d6e 9cfecdd30b2a... -- <file>` (research/05 section 1; the four differing files and the identical ones re-run 2026-11-12, ORCA-CHECKOUT git objects) | Whether the minified bundle equals that commit's output is shown only for the depth files, `runCreate` and `worker-dispatch-start` heads. |
| Orca-side depth fence for `dispatch`, `task-create`, `worktree create --agent`, `agent launch` | HISTORICAL / [UNVERIFIED] (earlier report `:402-407`, AC-0.7 quoted second-hand) | Only `worker-start` is traced; do not present WC-13's four other verbs as fenced by the cap. |
| Orca test suite that exercises helper routing (`structured-chat-worker-helpers.test.ts`) | Not run | Titles read only. |
| Subordinate (sub-orchestrator) mode | Deferred, not shipped | Not built in IronClaude (no command, skill, flag or contract role); barred by WC-13 text, not by an Orca refusal. |

**Extension points:** N/A for IronClaude: these files are Orca's. IronClaude can only (a) restate controls in the contract with the build named, and (b) pin them with a test against a recorded build, which does not exist. A change to the depth cap is an Orca setting ("Nested worker depth", Settings, Orchestration; the control is in the checkout's `OrchestrationPane.tsx`, line not re-read here). A subordinate role would need an Orca-side delegation path and an IronClaude contract change together; it is not designed here.

### 5.6 Installer, Orca Skills Install and Doctor

**Purpose:** Puts the Orca-facing components on a host and checks some of them: the worker contract and core guidance (`install_core.py`), the three guard hooks and their registrations (`install_hooks.py`), the ccsession launchers (`install_ccsession.py`), a request to Orca to install its own skills (`install_user_setup.py`), and a health check (`doctor.py`). IronClaude never ships the Orca skills; Orca is their only producer.

**Key Files:**

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/superclaude/cli/install_core.py` | `KNOWN_CORE_FILES` includes `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` (`:27`); `install_core_files` copies core `.md` files to `~/.claude/`, skipping an existing file unless forced (`:70-72`) and warning about stale kept files (`:94-105`). |
| `src/superclaude/cli/install_hooks.py` | `_FRESHNESS_SCRIPTS` (`:42`) lists the gate (`:88`), identity hook (`:90`) and guard (`:93`); `_copy_scripts` (`:251-290`); `_merge_settings` (`:298`). |
| `src/superclaude/cli/install_user_setup.py` | `_ORCA_SKILLS` (`:34`), `_ORCA_CLIS` (`:35`), `_ORCA_TIMEOUT = 25` (`:39`), `_NETWORK_BUDGET = 30` (`:40`); `_find_orca` (`:198`), `install_orca_skills` (`:230`), `install_user_setup` (`:449`). |
| `src/superclaude/cli/install_ccsession.py` | `wire_ccsession` (`:11`): links `ccsession` and `ccsession-orca` into `~/.local/bin`, seeds the env file. |
| `src/superclaude/cli/doctor.py` | `run_doctor` (`:17`) runs seven checks; `_check_ccsession` (`:57`) is the only Orca-adjacent one. |
| `src/superclaude/cli/main.py` | Order of steps: `install` calls `install_core_files` (`:178`), `wire_ccsession` (`:209`), `install_hooks(force=force)` (`:217`), `install_user_setup(force=force)` (`:236`); `update` passes `force=True` to core (`:365`), hooks (`:394`) and user setup (`:404`). |
| `tests/cli/test_install_user_setup.py`, `tests/cli/test_install_hooks.py`, `tests/unit/test_install_ccsession.py`, `tests/unit/test_cli_doctor.py`, `tests/cli/test_update_command.py` | Pins, read not run; the update-command fixture stubs `install_orca_skills` and `install_plugins` (`test_update_command.py:33-54`), so no test runs the real Orca step through `install` or `update`. |

**How It Works:**

1. **Order.** `install`: core files, slash commands, agents, skills, `wire_ccsession`, hooks, templates, user setup (rules, output styles, settings defaults, Orca skills, plugins), codegraph phase, retired-MCP step; `update` is the same without the last two (per gap-P3-G03 D). Both `install --force` and `update` run the file steps with `force=True`: they overwrite installed core files, commands, agents, templates, hook scripts, rules (`~/.claude/rules/**`) and output styles (`~/.claude/output-styles/**`) in place and delete and re-copy each installed skill directory, with no backup (`install_core.py:70-75`, `install_commands.py:55-61`, `install_agents.py:51-56`, `install_templates.py:71-76`, `install_hooks.py:279-284`, `install_skill.py:46-52`; rules and output styles through `_copy_tree`, `install_user_setup.py:67-83,116,148`, called with `force` at `:453-454` (`main.py:236,404`) and pinned for rules by `tests/cli/test_install_user_setup.py:42-49`; CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12), so local edits to those files are lost; a file in the rules or output-styles tree that the package does not ship is left alone (`_copy_tree` walks only the source tree, `:70`). What is not overwritten: `settings.json` is backed up before the hooks merge, the rules step and the settings-defaults step write it (`install_hooks.py:331-337`; `install_user_setup.py:98-107,132,191`), and those steps only add or refresh hook registrations, missing `claudeMdExcludes` entries and unset default keys (`install_hooks.py:417-471`; `install_user_setup.py:128-132,168-187`, `apply_settings_defaults` is called without `force`, `:455`); the seed file `~/.claude/auggie-projects.txt` is never overwritten, even with `force` (`install_hooks.py:97-103,226-229,556-587`); `~/.claude/ccsession.env` is seeded only when missing (`install_ccsession.py:72`), and `ccsession-tag/unseed-env.py` resets it only when it is exactly an older installer's own text (`:13-15,35-40`). One more file is written with no backup and without `force`: the pinned catalog `~/.claude/ironclaude-marketplace/.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, rewritten when its content differs, only when the `claude` CLI is on `PATH` and an enabled default plugin is pinned in it (`install_user_setup.py:289-296,322-324,362-366`; CHECKOUT); on every `install` run that is not `--list` and on every `update` run, with or without `force`, `install_all_skills` also deletes an installed `~/.claude/skills/<skill>` folder, recursively and with no backup, for each available `sc-<name>` skill that a `commands/<name>.md` file serves without activating that skill by name (`install_skills.py:40-59,88-98`, called at `main.py:204,384`; CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12). A `False` from `wire_ccsession` makes both commands exit 1. Pinned by `test_update_command.py:173-179`.
2. **Hooks.** `_copy_scripts` copies each listed script flat into `<settings dir>/hooks/` and sets mode 0755; an existing destination is skipped unless `force` (`install_hooks.py:279-281`), reported as "Skipped ... (use --force to overwrite)" (`:204`). Registration merge (`_merge_settings`, `:298-491`, per research/03 B.3): ownership by exact command identity; an already-registered managed command is skipped unless `force`, refreshed in place when forced; new entries are inserted so `launch-job-identity.sh` stays last (`:460`); a settings backup is made first and the write is atomic. Consequence: a plain `superclaude install` keeps an older installed script and an outdated registration silently; `orca_worktree.py` tells the operator to run `superclaude install --force` for that case. The installer does not check that `jq`, `awk` or `uv` exist or that `disableAllHooks` is unset; only the claim preflight does, and only for the launch-level guard path.
3. **Orca skills request (`install_orca_skills`, `install_user_setup.py:230-278`).** If not forced and all three `~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` exist: "already installed", Orca not contacted. Else `_find_orca()` probes `orca-ide` then `orca` with `skills list --json` (10 s each) and accepts only exit 0 with a topic named `orca-cli` (`:198-221`; a bare `orca` on Linux is often the GNOME screen reader). Then it runs `[orca, "skills", "install", "--agent", "claude-code", "--skill", "computer-use", "--skill", "orca-cli", "--skill", "orchestration"]` with timeout `max(1.0, min(25, deadline - now))` (`:224-227,243-253`), no `--force`, no `env=` argument. Every return path has `True` first (`:239,242,263,278`), so Orca absence, failure, timeout or partial install does not fail `install` or `update`, except a `skills list --json` reply with some `topics` shapes, which raises out of `_find_orca` (it iterates `topics` after the `try`, `:219`; the shapes are in the evidence table below, L32 and owner question C3); `force` only bypasses the "already installed" skip and is never forwarded to Orca (an edited copy is skipped and reported by Orca). One 30 s deadline (`:451`) is shared with the plugin step.
4. **Where Orca writes.** Orca's `skills install` writes the shared `~/.agents/skills` folder and links into each detected agent's folder; for Claude the root is `~/.claude/skills`, replaced by `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR>/skills` when that variable is set in the child's environment to a value Orca did not inject itself: `readUserClaudeConfigDir` ignores a value equal to `ORCA_CLAUDE_INJECTED_CONFIG_DIR` and uses the user's own value from `ORCA_CLAUDE_USER_CONFIG_DIR`, or none (`src/main/claude-accounts/claude-profile-paths.ts:230-246`, used for the global Claude root at `src/main/skills/bundled-skill-install/local-host.ts:41-49`; `src/shared/claude-profile-routing.ts:4-8`; ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`, read not run; gap-P3-G03 A.5 said "user-set"). Whether this host runs managed Claude accounts, and so whether Orca injects the variable in the terminal that runs the installer, is [UNVERIFIED]. The installer's check uses `home/.claude/skills`, so with a user-set `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` the "already installed" test and the `n/3 files present` post-check can disagree with where Orca wrote (derived from source, not observed; open question C4). Orca's own help says the request "needs no network"; the code comment `install_user_setup.py:36-38` that says it downloads is [CODE-CONTRADICTED], low impact.
5. **ccsession wiring.** `wire_ccsession` refuses (returns `False`) on missing or symlinked skill files, links `~/.local/bin/ccsession` and `ccsession-orca` (an absent `ccsession-orca` is a warning, not a failure), and seeds `~/.claude/ccsession.env` from the example only when missing. It never configures Orca's Claude command setting; pointing Orca at `ccsession-orca` is a manual step documented in `ccsession-tag/README.md:211-226` and `core/CLAUDE.md:51`, and neither installer nor doctor verifies it.
6. **Doctor.** Seven checks: pytest plugin, agents, skills, configuration, ccsession, codegraph, retired MCP (`doctor.py:29-49`). `_check_ccsession` (`:57-131`) requires the skill folder, `ccsession`, `ccsession-orca`, both hook scripts, the env file, both `~/.local/bin` links resolving to the skill scripts, and `SessionStart`, `UserPromptSubmit` and `SessionEnd` registrations; a missing `~/.local/bin` on `PATH` adds a warning to the check name only. The skills check fails only for skills named by `> Skill <name>` lines in installed commands, and no command names `orchestration`, `orca-cli` or `computer-use`, so doctor can never fail for a missing Orca skill. Doctor checks none of: an Orca CLI, the Orca skills, the three guard hooks' presence or registration, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`, the Orca Claude-command setting. It fails where the installer only warns (missing `ccsession-orca`, an unmanaged link), so `install` can exit 0 while `doctor` exits 1.
7. **Host observation (HOST-INSTALLED; this host only; research 2026-11-11, re-run at about 02:48 UTC on 2026-11-12).** The installed set is mixed relative to the refs (matrix in Section 3.0.3); an installed copy of `launch-level-pre-push.sh` exists in `~/.claude/hooks/` and equals ORIGIN/MASTER (its use as a git hook was not observed). Every `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` line number in this reference holds for HEAD and the installed copy only (ORIGIN/MASTER has 338 lines, with a coordinator preflight at `:217`, commit `146be6fc3`). The installed `CLAUDE.md` and gate still carry the older ownership wording. Host `settings.json` registers the three guard hooks and the ccsession hooks; `disableAllHooks` is unset. The Orca stub `~/.claude/skills/orchestration/SKILL.md` is Orca-shipped text identified by digest; which invocation wrote it is not established (no install log exists). No installation rollout is claimed by this reference.

**Public Interface:**

```text
install_core_files(target_path: Path = None, force: bool = False) -> Tuple[bool, str]
install_hooks(target_path: Path | None = None, force: bool = False) -> Tuple[bool, str]   # install_hooks.py:153
install_orca_skills(home: Path | None = None, deadline: float | None = None, force: bool = False) -> Tuple[bool, str]
install_user_setup(force: bool = False) -> Tuple[bool, str]
wire_ccsession(home: Path | None = None) -> tuple[bool, str]
run_doctor(verbose: bool = False) -> Dict[str, Any]    # {"checks": [...], "passed": bool}
superclaude install [--force] | update | doctor [--verbose]
```

**Dependencies:**

| Depends On | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Orca CLI on `PATH` (`orca-ide`, `orca`) | External | Optional; absence is a skipped step. |
| `~/.claude/settings.json` | Host config | Merged by `install_hooks`; malformed or non-object targets are refused. |
| `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` | Source of registrations | Plugin mirror `plugins/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` is a subset. |

**Consumers:**

| Used By | How |
|---|---|
| Operator | `superclaude install`, `update`, `doctor`, `./update.sh` (runs doctor last). |
| Claim preflight (5.4) | Tells the operator to run `superclaude install --force` for stale registrations. |
| Eval hook adapter | Calls `install_hooks(force=False)` into a per-eval HOME (per research/03 B.3). |

**Conventions & Patterns:**

- Installers take `home` or `target` parameters so tests run in a temp directory; `shutil.which` and `subprocess.run` are faked in tests.
- Existing destination files are kept without `force`; the run still reports success and prints a stale-file warning for core files.
- `make verify-sync` requires every `hooks/scripts/*.sh` to be listed in `_FRESHNESS_SCRIPTS`; the name still says "freshness" though it holds the guards.

**Evidence and contradictions:**

| Item | Class and tag | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `_find_orca` reads `topics` outside its `try` (`:216-219`) | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] | Only a reply with exit 0 and a JSON object can raise, and only through `any(t.get("name") == "orca-cli" for t in topics)` (`:219`): `TypeError` when `topics` is null, a number or a boolean (not iterable), `AttributeError` when the iteration yields a non-object before the first `orca-cli` object (a non-empty string or object, or a list element that is not an object). The exception escapes through `install_orca_skills` (`:240`), `install_user_setup` (`:456`) and `main.install` (`:236`, no `try`; `update` at `:404` likewise), aborting the command at the user-setup step. Nothing raises for an absent `topics`, an empty string, an empty object, an empty list, a list of objects, a non-object element that follows an `orca-cli` object (`any` stops first), a non-zero exit (the `and` short-circuits), or output that is not JSON or not an object (caught by the `try`, `:216-218`). Pinned as the original's behavior by `tests/issue_watch/test_find_orca_parity.py:8-15,52-53,69-90` (the raising shapes are `_ORIGINAL_RAISES`; the empty string and empty object are listed as returning). `_find_orca` runs only when `force` is set or one of the three Orca `SKILL.md` files is missing (`:236-240`). A well-formed Orca does not trigger it. Owner question C3. Not a DEFECT: the step runs; one path leaks an exception. |
| `docs/getting-started/installation.md:497-504` says the installer "always writes under `$HOME/.claude/`" | [CODE-CONTRADICTED] by derivation for the Orca step | Owner questions C4, C5. |
| `installation.md:359-360` "the step never makes `superclaude install` fail" | Imprecise | True of plugin and Orca requests; rules, output styles, settings defaults and the escaping exception can still fail it. |

### 5.7 Worker Result Channel (`return-contract.yaml`)

**Purpose:** Carries a worker command's result from the worker to the coordinator: a command run with `--unattended` writes `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml`, the worker maps its `worker_status` to `worker_done --outcome` and passes the path, Orca carries `--report-path` without opening the file, and the coordinator opens it and admits or rejects the result. The whole channel is a text protocol: the writer and the reader are language-model sessions, and no executable in `src/` produces, parses or routes on the worker fields.

**Key Files:**

| File | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `src/superclaude/core/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` | The only complete schema, WC-4 (`:32-39`); outcome mapping WC-5 (`:59`); reader rules (`:237-246`); nested-result rule (`:25`). Prose, no machine schema. |
| `src/superclaude/commands/*.md` (31 files with `## Unattended mode`) | Per-command `Contract.` bullet: where the file lands, what `report_path`, `artifacts` and the status mapping mean for that command. |
| Producer skills with a field-level spelling | `skills/sc-workflow-protocol/refs/return-contract.md` (worker block `:43-57`), `sc-brainstorm-protocol/SKILL.md` (`:384-392`), `sc-impact-protocol/SKILL.md` (`:99-102`), `sc-graph-protocol/SKILL.md` (`:111-115`), `sc-init-lite-protocol/SKILL.md` (`:61`) (per gap-P3-G04 3.1). |
| `src/superclaude/cli/reflect/contract.py` | `parse_contract` (`:65`, `yaml.safe_load` at `:77`, tolerates unknown top-level fields) and `derive_verdict`: reflect's own contract, not `worker_status`. |
| `src/superclaude/skills/sc-implement-protocol/scripts/archive_workspace.py` | Reads the workflow PACKAGE contract (`:325-327`: `contract_version == "1.1"`, matching slug, status `success` or `partial`), not the worker contract. |
| `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` | Static pins: `RULE_PHRASES` WC-4, WC-4-mapping, WC-4-missing-directory-error, WC-5 (`:102-149`); `UNATTENDED_EXPECT` (`:438`, 31 keys); `UNATTENDED_COMMANDS` derived (`:746`). |

**How It Works:**

1. **Schema (WC-4, `:33`).** Top-level keys: `worker_contract_version` (`"1"`), `worker_status` (`succeeded`, `partial`, `failed` or `blocked`), `command` (the exact command line), `report_path` (main deliverable, may be null), `artifacts` (every other file written, excluding paths git ignores and paths outside the repository, `:34`), `blocked_question` when blocked, `reason` when failed (for `/sc:implement` also when blocked, carrying the STOP code). The field is `worker_status`, not `status`, because workflow, adversarial and reflect contracts already use `status` with other values (`:35`).
2. **Where the file lands (`:15`, `:32`).** Exactly one report directory per brief: the command's own directory-valued `--output` or `--output-dir`, or `--report-dir`. Neither or both is refused: the command does no work, writes nothing and prints the exact chat error `unattended mode needs exactly one report directory: ...` (`:38`). That string occurs only in the contract and `test_worker_contract.py:120`; no command file emits it verbatim.
3. **Four layouts** (gap-P3-G04 3.3; the contract names only the first two: (a) for brainstorm and reflect, (b) for workflow and adversarial, `:35`): (a) worker fields merged into the command's own contract (brainstorm and reflect with `--output`; impact and graph with `--output-dir`; also cli-portify, release-split, auggie-review); (b) a separate worker contract at `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` that lists the command's own contract in `artifacts` (adversarial, workflow, and brainstorm and reflect with `--report-dir`); (c) a full contract, existing fields plus worker fields, in `<report-dir>` (impact and graph with `--report-dir`); (d) a worker contract at `<report-dir>` whose `Contract.` bullet does not say it is merged into an existing file (22 commands: analyze, build, business-panel, cleanup, cleanup-audit, cli-eval, design, document, explain, improve, index, index-repo, init-lite, recommend, research, review-translation, spec-panel, task, tasklist, tdd, test, troubleshoot). Whether that file is merged with the command's own contract or separate is [UNVERIFIED] from the bullets alone (`research/gap-P3-G04.md:116`, CHECKOUT text): the `troubleshoot` bullet says that with `--caller task-unified` "the TFEP fields stay as today and the worker fields are added beside them" (`commands/troubleshoot.md:175`), and the `cli-eval` bullet (`commands/cli-eval.md:83`) states no merge either way.
4. **Status mapping (`:36`).** `success` to `succeeded`, `partial` to `partial`, `failed` to `failed`, `stopped-precondition` to `failed`, and the flag-STOP `blocked` of impact and graph to `failed`. It omits `dry-run`, which reflect and brainstorm map to `succeeded`; other commands carry their own mapping in the `Contract.` bullet (release-split `split-approved`, `no-split`, `split-with-remediation`; cleanup-audit checkpoint stop). Only troubleshoot maps its own `blocked` to `blocked`.
5. **One flow (`/sc:workflow` as a worker, gap-P3-G04 section 2).** Coordinator brief with a `Command:` line and a unique report directory; worker invokes the command through its Skill (WC-7); the skill generates the plan package, writes the package contract (contract 1.1) and then the worker contract on every outcome; the worker reads its own contract's `worker_status` (never the package's `status`) and sends `worker_done` once with `--outcome failed` for `failed`, `blocked` or no contract, else `succeeded` (`:59`); the coordinator opens the file (`:39`, `:93`) and applies `:237-246`. Any other `worker_status` value is treated as `failed` (`:59`): the designed safety net, because nothing validates the file.
6. **Readers.** Worker side: the worker LLM (`:59`). Coordinator side: the coordinator LLM and its run notes (`:242`, `:246`). Nested: the calling command reads the nested contract per `:25`. Code readers exist only for other contracts: the reflect wrapper (`contract.py:65-82`; `derive_verdict` routes child exit 124 to blocked, reason `timeout` (`:161-164`), every other non-zero child exit, even with a contract present, to blocked, reason `child-crash` (`:169-172`), and a missing contract with child exit 0 to blocked, reason `contract-missing` (`:173-177`)), the workflow-package archive check, and the swarm and cli-portify pipelines for their own contracts. Orca's role ends at transporting `--report-path`; Orca-side use beyond settlement was not traced (research/02 5.2; G04 R8).
7. **Negative result.** A search of `src` for `worker_status`, `worker_contract_version` and `blocked_question` in `.py`, `.sh`, `.ts`, `.js`, `.json`, `.yaml`, `.yml` and `.toml` files returned zero matches (re-run 2026-11-12, CHECKOUT). Every hit is in a `.md` file. The `plugins/` mirror carries the `## Unattended mode` section for 2 of the 31 converted commands in `src/superclaude/commands` (`plugins/superclaude/commands/recommend.md` and `workflow.md`, `grep -rl '^## Unattended mode' plugins/superclaude/`, 2026-11-12) and `plugins/superclaude/core/` has no `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` and no `CLAUDE.md`; the plugin build is not equivalent to the source (G04 R7, answered by this read).
8. **`/sc:implement` has no producer.** The contract speaks of an implement worker contract (`:33`, `:243`, `:250`), but `commands/implement.md` and `skills/sc-implement-protocol/**` contain no `unattended`, `worker_status` or `WORKER-CONTRACT` text (count 0, re-run 2026-11-12), and implement is in neither the P1 nor the P2 list. Scope decision of record: the worker-contract plan `.dev/tasks/done/TASK-WF-orcaWorkerContra-20261103-012232/source.md` (section 3 row `implement`, section 6 phase P4, section 9 approved decisions of 2026-11-02; lines per gap-P3-G04 5.1), pinned by the deferral legend `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:1932` and the rows at `:2349,2357,2365` ("/sc:implement is converted in P4"). This is a future-phase item, not a DEFECT; the contract text is forward-looking. How a coordinator should read a successful implement run today is [UNVERIFIED]; the ledger is the evidence until then. Open question C7.
9. **Seven command files with no `## Unattended mode` section and no bar.** Of the 46 command files, 31 carry the section and 8 names are barred (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:23`); the other 7 are `implement`, `load`, `post-release`, `roadmap`, `save`, `validate-roadmap` and `validate-tests` (a loop over `src/superclaude/commands/*.md` testing for `^## Unattended mode`, re-run 2026-11-12; CHECKOUT, [CODE-VERIFIED]). The contract's P1 and P2 lists name none of them (`:303-304`). The plan of record (HISTORICAL, `.dev/tasks/done/TASK-WF-orcaWorkerContra-20261103-012232/source.md:223-224,233-234,236`) assigns roadmap, validate-roadmap, validate-tests and save to phase P3 (running total 35), implement and post-release to P4 (pr-submit only if D3 (b) is chosen), and says `/sc:load` never gains the flag because it is used inside another worker's brief; the contract itself shows a brief line `Command: /sc:load ...` (`:204`). The checkout has no P3 or P4 conversion, and this reference does not claim any of the seven is wired. What the guard text does with them (NORMATIVE, text only, no executable): a `Command:` line that adds `--unattended` to one of the seven is refused with `guard refused: loaded command has no Unattended mode section ...` (case 2, `:18`) when no copy the worker reads has the section (true of the checkout files; installed command copies were not checked for these seven); the guard refuses a line for a missing flag only where every copy has the section (case 1, `:17`) and refuses by name only the never-as-worker list (case 3, `:20`), so the contract text does not say how a coordinator should dispatch one of the seven without the flag [UNVERIFIED how a coordinator is meant to treat that]. `/sc:implement` has its own account in item 8.

**Public Interface:**

```yaml
# <report-dir>/return-contract.yaml (WC-4; types as implied by the prose and producer examples)
worker_contract_version: "1"
worker_status: succeeded | partial | failed | blocked
command: "<exact command line>"
report_path: <path> | null
artifacts: [<paths>]
blocked_question: "<question>"      # when blocked
reason: "<text>"                     # when failed (implement: also when blocked, STOP code)
```

```text
worker_done --task-id <T> --dispatch-id <D> --outcome succeeded|failed --report-path <report-dir>/return-contract.yaml   # syntax authority: the preamble
```

**Dependencies:**

| Depends On | Type | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Each command's `## Unattended mode` section | Text | Fixes directory flags, gate resolutions, status mapping. |
| Orca `worker_done` transport | External | Carries `--report-path`; "does not check the file" (`:39`). |
| The coordinator reading `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` | Text | Not auto-loaded (5.2). |

**Consumers:**

| Used By | How |
|---|---|
| Worker session | Maps `worker_status` to `--outcome` (`:59`). |
| Coordinator session | Opens the file and admits or rejects (`:237-246`). |
| Calling commands (nested propagation) | Read the nested contract (`:25`; for example `commands/reflect.md`, `commands/task.md`, `commands/workflow.md`). |
| `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` | Pins phrases of the contract and 31 command sections; parses no produced file. |

**Conventions & Patterns:**

- `artifacts` leaves out gitignored and out-of-repository paths because WC-9 stages every listed path.
- A contract is written on every outcome, including stops before other output exists (`:37`); under the contract this is an instruction, so "no contract" maps to `--outcome failed`.
- Package contract (`<pkg>/return-contract.yaml`, `status: success|partial|failed`, read by code) and worker contract (`<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml`, `worker_status`, read by no code) are different files; never conflate them.

**Evidence and contradictions:**

| Item | Class and tag | Note |
|---|---|---|
| No code writes, parses or validates the worker fields; no test parses a produced file | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] negative | A producer writing a wrong enum or `status` for `worker_status` would pass every test; the coordinator's admission rule is the net. |
| No produced contract was examined | [UNVERIFIED] | G04 R1: the field set is specified in prose and pinned as text. |
| `report_path` form (absolute versus repo-relative) differs by command | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] | Reflect says absolute, workflow ref says repo path; contract gives no form (G04 R2). Open question C13. |
| `:36` mapping is incomplete (omits `dry-run` and per-command values) | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] | Open question C14. |
| `blocked` has two meanings (person gate `:31`; flag STOP mapped to `failed` `:36`) | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] | Open question C15. |
| `:35` names layout (a) for brainstorm and reflect and layout (b) for workflow and adversarial; layouts (c) and (d) are not named | Stale-incomplete | Item 3 of How It Works above (the four layouts) is authoritative. |
| `/sc:implement` producer absent | Future-phase (P4), cited decision | Item 8. |
| `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:59,89,295,303-307` P0 and P1 conditionals read as historical | CHECKOUT [CODE-VERIFIED] stale | Pinned in part: a phrase of `:59` (`RULE_PHRASES` `WC-5-P0-override`, `test_worker_contract.py:144-149`), of `:89` (`brief-contents`, `:255-261`), the `:295` line and the "from P1" fragment of `:307` (`:360-367`) are pinned; `:303` and `:305` are not. |

**Extension points:** To convert a command: add an `## Unattended mode` section (report-directory rule, gate resolutions, `Contract.` bullet naming `report_path`, `artifacts` and the status mapping, nested directories `<caller report-dir>/nested/<command>-<n>/`), add an entry to `UNATTENDED_EXPECT` (`test_worker_contract.py:438`; its checker requires `return-contract.yaml`, `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` and `--report-dir` in the section and no `src/superclaude/` path or Orca command name) and a phase tuple, update the flags table in the contract brief template (`:307-327`), run `make sync-dev` and reinstall. To add a field: edit WC-4 and the five producers that spell fields out, plus the `WC-4` entry of `RULE_PHRASES`. To validate produced files: no hook exists; a validator would be new code and a new test. Producer for `/sc:implement`: planned P4, not built.

## 6. State Management

> **N/A (conditional section, "if applicable"; the template marks Section 6 for frontend features, template line 329).**

Supervised orchestration has no client-side state, store or slice. Its state is files and records: the worker contract `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:32-39`), the launch-level session store under `~/.claude/state/launch-level/` (`launch-level-guard.sh:257-272`), Orca's own run and dispatch database (ORCA-CHECKOUT, `research/05`), and ccsession tab records (`research/gap-P3-G05.md` F2.4). Those belong to Sections 4, 5, 8 and 9. [CODE-VERIFIED by `src/superclaude/` containing no frontend tree for this subject (`research/04` F1-F2, `research/03` scope)]

## 8. API & Integration Points

> **Note:** Supervised orchestration has no HTTP API. "API" here means the Orca CLI verbs (and the RPC methods behind them) that IronClaude code calls or that its normative contract tells a coordinator or worker to call. IronClaude itself defines no Orca verb: no Python launcher calls `worker-start`, `dispatch`, `task-create` or any `orchestration` verb (`research/04-launchers.md` F1 and F2, grep of `src/superclaude` re-run in fix cycle 1: `worker-start` occurs only in four documentation or message files). The coordinator and the worker are LLM sessions that follow `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`; the verbs in 8.1b are therefore NORMATIVE instructions, not code.

### 8.1 API Endpoints Used

Columns follow the template (Endpoint, Method, Purpose, Request Shape, Response Shape); "Method" is the caller and transport because there is no HTTP verb.

#### 8.1b Orca verbs named by the normative contract (instruction text only, no IronClaude code issues them)

| Endpoint (Orca CLI verb, RPC method) | Method (who is told to call it) | Purpose | Request Shape | Response Shape |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `orchestration worker-start` (RPC `orchestration.workerStart`) | Coordinator (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md` section 4); forbidden to a worker by WC-13 (`:62`) | Start one worker per Task | `--task`, `--spec`, `--worktree id:<repo-id>::<path>` or `path:<dir>`, `--agent`; never `--retry-of` (WC-6, `:73`) | Receipt with the Dispatch id once the preamble is delivered (`gap-P3-G01.md` S10 row 02 G8) |
| `orchestration check --wait --types <9 types> --timeout-ms 300000`, `check --ack <delivery_id>` | Coordinator (`:101`); worker uses plain `check --terminal <h> --json` (preamble) | Mailbox reads | `--types` only decides when a waiter wakes (`runtime-message-waiters.ts:29`, [CODE-VERIFIED] ORCA-CHECKOUT) | Delivery batch; replays until acked; a reply `status` stored already read is not returned by a later wait (`gap-P3-G01.md` R5) |
| `orchestration send --type worker_done\|heartbeat\|escalation`, `ask --timeout-ms 600000`, `check --terminal <h> --json` | Worker (dispatch preamble, `IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/preamble.ts:77-133`: heartbeat `:77`, worker_done `:106`, ask `:118`, escalation `:122`, check `:128,:133`; ORCA-CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12) | Lifecycle reports; the preamble, not `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`, is the authority for syntax (`:7`) | `worker_done` needs `--task-id`, `--dispatch-id`, `--outcome succeeded\|failed` | Orca settles the Dispatch and Task on a valid `worker_done` and, after a `succeeded` outcome only, promotes dependents `pending -> ready`; it starts nothing (`worker-report-settlement.ts:60-285`, `task-store.ts:208-233`, ORCA-CHECKOUT) |
| `orchestration send --type status` (WC-12 acks), `orchestration reply --id <message_id> --body <text>` | Worker sends `status` acks; coordinator answers with `reply`. Neither is preamble syntax: `status` is instructed by `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:61` and is a valid type per the CLI spec (`IronOrca/src/cli/specs/orchestration.ts:74`); `reply` appears in the preamble only in its SUB-DISPATCH section (`preamble.ts:231`), present only when `canDispatchSubWorkers` is true | Acks of coordinator follow-ups; answers to a worker's `ask` | `status` body starts `ack <message_id> applied\|queued\|refused` (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:61`) | No response shape recorded (instruction text; ORCA-CHECKOUT CLI spec) |
| `orchestration worker-show --dispatch <id> --json`, `worker-list`, `worker-read` | Coordinator | Liveness and evidence | `--include-remote` adds federation reads | Two liveness words: `projection.liveness.verdict` (fleet) and `observation.status` (PTY); text mode prints only the fleet verdict (`gap-P3-G01.md` S5) |
| `orchestration task-create --spec`, `task-update --status [--result]`, `run-create` | Coordinator | Task and Run records | `task-update` changes only `--status` and `--result` | `orchestration.runCreate` has no worker refusal: a worker with an active Dispatch can create a new Run (`research/05-orca-provenance.md` 4.2) |
| `worktree ps --agent-wait` | Coordinator sweep (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:110`) | Per-row agent wait | flag | **State divergence SD-1:** accepted by INSTALLED-FILES (`out/cli/specs/core.js:157-161`), absent from ORCA-CHECKOUT (`src/cli/specs/core.ts:152-156`); in the installed handler the flag reaches the runtime only together with `--json` (`out/cli/handlers/worktree.js:52`: `json && flags.has('agent-wait')`), and the contract's sweep command (`:110`) has no `--json`; running build [UNVERIFIED] (`research/02-lifecycle.md` 3.3, 6.5) |

The retired automatic Task scheduler (`orchestration.run`, `orchestration.runStop`, CLI `coordinator-start` and `coordinator-stop`) is not an interface: CLI handlers throw `orchestration_migration_required`, the RPC contract lists both methods as retired, and `admitRpcRequest` rejects them before any handler (`gap-P3-G01.md` S7, R3; ORCA-CHECKOUT, installed bundle checked textually only).

### 8.2 Internal Integration Points

| Integration | Direction | Mechanism | Description |
|---|---|---|---|
| Claude Code hook host to the three guards | Inbound (host calls the script) | JSON on stdin, exit code and stderr out; registered in `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` (installed flat as `~/.claude/hooks/<name>.sh`) | `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` (PreToolUse, matcher `Bash`, 3 s, `hooks.json:105-114`); `launch-level-guard.sh` (UserPromptSubmit no matcher, 5 s, `:63-71`; PreToolUse long tool matcher, 5 s, `:115-124`); `launch-job-identity.sh` (PreToolUse `Bash`, 5 s, `:125-134`). Contract: allow = exit 0, block = message on stderr plus exit 2, never exit 1 (`require-orca...sh:11`, `launch-level-guard.sh:4-5`). No guard is registered on SubagentStart or SubagentStop (`hooks.json:159-190`). [CODE-VERIFIED] CHECKOUT |
| Claim script to launch-level guard (nonce handoff) | Outbound file, then inbound read | Pending record `~/.claude/state/launch-level/pending/<nonce>` (mode 0600) written by `orca_worktree.py` (`PENDING_PARTS` `:773`, `publish_pending`), marker `[sc-launch:v1:<32 hex>]` in the launch prompt, consumed once by the guard with `mv` then published as `sessions/<session_id>` by hard link (`launch-level-guard.sh:334-378`, as read in `research/03-guards.md` C.2) | The only producer of the marker is `orca_worktree.py:1351-1355`. Producer and consumer are each tested, but no test runs both together (`research/04-launchers.md` F3, count grep of `tests/`). A leading marker with a missing or used nonce publishes a `degraded` record that reads as the strictest level `W` (`launch-level-guard.sh:264-268,380-382`, per research 03). [CODE-VERIFIED] CHECKOUT |
| Governed session to Orca CLI | Constraint on outbound calls | Guard classifier: a word whose basename is exactly `orca` is allowed only for `status`, `help`, `--version`, `worktree current\|list\|show`, `terminal list\|show\|read`, `orchestration check\|inbox\|run-current\|task-list\|worker-show`; every other `orca` form is ESCAPE and denied at every level, unless the segment ends in `--help` or `-h` (`launch-level-guard.sh:458-478`, the exemption at `:477`, denial at `:737`, re-read) | Applies only to sessions bound by the marker. It is a restriction on claim-launched agents, not a mechanism that identifies workers or coordinators (`research/03-guards.md` C.6 item 7). **See the DEFECT below for the name limit.** |
| Job identity hook to launch-level guard | Same Bash call, two hooks | `launch-job-identity.sh` returns `hookSpecificOutput.updatedInput` that replaces the whole input with `/bin/bash <runner> execute <sid> <tool_use_id> <original command>`, without `permissionDecision` (`:475-483` per `research/03-guards.md` E.3; header `:14-15` re-read) | Order and combination of matching hooks are unspecified in the script (`:15`). DOCUMENTED: matching hooks run in parallel and several `updatedInput` rewrites are neither merged nor ordered (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide, "Limitations", re-fetched 2026-11-12 per `web-01`). If the guard ever saw the rewritten command it would block it as LL-TAMPER (the rewrite contains `.claude/hooks`; `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard_with_identity.py:135-154`); live order is [UNVERIFIED] (OB-H1 skipped). Whether the whole-input rewrite reaches the tool when Claude Code runs in auto permission mode was not observed: the hook header says live auto-mode rewrites need Task4 (`launch-job-identity.sh:17-18`, `research/03-guards.md:103`), and the documentation exempts a settings `command` hook that rewrites Bash with no `permissionDecision` from the auto-mode denial in the sessions that show that text, with residual cases (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, `web-01` Q2); DOCUMENTED, not observed on this host [UNVERIFIED]. A session record `degraded` makes the guard allow read-only commands while the identity hook exits 2 on every Bash call (`launch-job-identity.sh:74` `LEVELS` holds three words, re-read) |
| Claim preflight (IR-4) to host settings and hooks | Outbound read-only | `enforcement_preflight` (`orca_worktree.py:925-1015`) reads `PATH`, `~/.claude/hooks/launch-level-guard.sh` (must contain `# launch-level-guard-version: 1`, `:810`), `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` (`:959-967`, re-read), user, worktree and worktree-local `settings.json` for `disableAllHooks: true` (`:972-982`, re-read), and the guard and identity registrations | Refusal is `status: failed`, warning `W-ENFORCEMENT-ABSENT`, no record, no agent (`:1308-1325`). It never checks registration of `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` (that name does not occur in the file) and verifies only the claim process, not the agent's environment (`:1318-1320`) |
| ccsession to claim shim check (tab title) | Writer to reader through Orca | `ccsession --auto-name "<launch prompt>"` builds `<folder>-sc-launch-v1-<12 hex>-<MMDD>-<HHMMSS>-<4 hex>`; shown as `<label> · Claude Code` by an OSC 0 title (`ccsession:1875`) and by `orca terminal rename` from the SessionStart hook (`ccsession:537`, `session-name.sh:196-203`); read back by `SHIM_TITLE` (`orca_worktree.py:779`, re-read) | Advisory only. A miss yields `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION`, status stays `launched`, nothing stops the agent (`:1378-1386`, re-read). Stale second screen marker `SHIM_MARKERS[1]` (`:774`) and no test joining producer to matcher are recorded debt (`gap-P3-G05.md` F4.4, F5; owner questions C17 and C19) |
| Issue watcher to claim flow | Outbound launch, then the launched session runs the claim | `superclaude issue-watch once` runs `agent launch ... --prompt "/sc:issue --claim <n>"` in one configured checkout (`launch.py:95-116`); that unmarked session then runs the claim script, which launches the marked second agent | The watcher prompt carries no `[sc-launch:` marker, so the first session is not governed (`launch-level-guard.sh:366-368`, B9, per research 04). The combined chain was inferred from two verified hops and never run end to end (`research/04-launchers.md` Gaps item 4). Neither launcher calls `worker-start`, so neither starts a supervised worker |
| Installer to hooks, scripts, Orca and ccsession | Outbound install steps | `superclaude install` order: core files, commands, agents, skills, `wire_ccsession`, `install_hooks`, templates, `install_user_setup` (Orca skills, plugins), codegraph, retire step (`main.py:178-263`; `gap-P3-G03.md` D.1) | `install_hooks` lists the guards in `_FRESHNESS_SCRIPTS` (`install_hooks.py:88,90,93`), copies them flat to `~/.claude/hooks/` and merges registrations; without `--force` an older installed script or outdated matcher is kept (`:279-281`, `:417-419`, per research 03 B.3). `wire_ccsession` links `~/.local/bin/ccsession-orca` but never configures Orca's "Claude command" setting (a manual step: `ccsession-tag/README.md:211-226`) |
| Worker result channel (`return-contract.yaml`) | Worker writes, coordinator reads | Instruction level only: the command's LLM writes `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml`; the worker passes its path as `worker_done --report-path`; Orca "does not check the file" (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:39`, re-read) | No executable in `src/` produces, parses, validates or routes on `worker_contract_version`, `worker_status` or `blocked_question` (negative grep, `gap-P3-G04.md` 4.4). Code that reads other contracts: `cli/reflect/contract.py:65-82` (read-and-ignore of unknown keys) and `sc-implement-protocol/scripts/archive_workspace.py:325-334` (package contract 1.1, not the worker contract). `/sc:implement` has no producer yet: deferred to P4 by a cited scope decision (`gaps-and-questions.md` D) |
| Orca dispatch preamble to `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` | Text against text | When the depth cap allows, the preamble adds a `SUB-DISPATCH` section telling a worker it may run `worker-start`; WC-13 forbids that for an IronClaude worker (`preamble.ts:142-146,219-256`, per `gap-P3-G01.md` S1; `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:62`, re-read) | The two texts instruct opposite things; the only machine barrier is the depth cap (see 9.3). Orca's federated own-Run variant (`buildOwnRunSubDispatchSection`) is Orca text, not IronClaude sub-orchestration, and was not traced at runtime |
| L2 pre-push layer to identity helper | Inbound call | `launch-level-pre-push.sh` calls `launch-job-identity.sh identify "$$"` | **ORIGIN/MASTER only** (`launch-level-pre-push.sh`, 208 lines). Absent from CHECKOUT, where nothing at runtime calls `identify`, `prepare` or `run` except the hook rewrite (`research/03-guards.md` E.5). No research file read the L2 script |

#### DEFECT (launch-level guard) and documented ceiling (worktree gate): the Orca CLI is recognized only under the command word `orca` (recorded; not fixed)

- **Statement.** `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh` accepts a command word only when its basename is exactly `orca` or the word starts with `$` (`:145`, re-read). `launch-level-guard.sh` classifies an Orca word as ESCAPE only in the exact-match arm `orca)` (`:458`, re-read). `orca-ide` (the installed command name on Linux and WSL), `orca-dev` (dev builds) and `orca.cmd` (Windows) match neither. [CODE-VERIFIED] CHECKOUT; 29 fixed-input probes (`gap-P3-G02.md` 2.2; cite its ids with the prefix `G2-`, because `research/03` uses `P01-P28` for its own series): bare `orca worktree create --name x` returned 2 from both (`G2-P01`); the same command under `orca-ide`, `orca-dev`, `/opt/Orca/bin/orca-ide` and `orca.cmd` (`G2-P02` to `G2-P05` and `G2-P08`), and under `orca-ide` behind `env X=1`, `sudo`, `command`, `time` and `bash -c "..."` (`G2-P11` to `G2-P15`), returned 0 from both. A bare `orca` behind a wrapper is still seen by the guard (`env orca worktree create --name x` returned 2 from the guard on 2026-11-12; `bash -c "orca worktree create --name x"` returned 2, `G2-P16`). In a session bound at levels `worktree` and `full-submit` the guard also returned 0 for `orca-ide terminal send`, `orca-ide agent launch` and `orca-ide orchestration worker-start` (`G2-P24`, `G2-P28`, `G2-P06`). Key rows were re-run in fix cycle 1 (`gap-P3-G02.md` 3.6) and eleven probes again on 2026-11-12 (Section 13.2.1).

  Probe results for the statement above (fixed-input probes of 2026-11-11 from `gap-P3-G02.md`; the key rows were re-run in its fix cycle 1 and independently in `qa/phase-3-verification-content.md`; eleven were re-run on 2026-11-12). Gate rc is the exit code of `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh`; guard rc is that of `launch-level-guard.sh` for a bound session at levels `worktree` and `full-submit`:

  | Probe | Gate rc | Guard rc (bound session, W and FS) |
  |---|---|---|
  | G2-P01 `orca worktree create --name x` | 2 | 2 (ESCAPE) |
  | G2-P02 `orca-ide worktree create --name x` | 0 | 0 |
  | G2-P03 `orca-dev worktree create --name x` | 0 | 0 |
  | G2-P08 `orca.cmd worktree create --name x` | 0 | 0 |
  | G2-P06 `orca-ide orchestration worker-start --name w` | 0 | 0 (bare name: 2, G2-P07) |
  | G2-P24 `orca-ide terminal send --terminal t1 --text approved` | 0 | 0 |
  | G2-P28 `orca-ide agent launch --agent claude --worktree path:/x` | 0 | 0 (bare name pinned ESCAPE, `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:3102`) |

- **Why it is not exotic.** `src/shared/orca-cli-command-name.ts:3-11` (ORCA-CHECKOUT) names `orca-ide` on Linux; Orca's installed CLI prints recovery commands with `orca-ide` on Linux (`out/cli/runtime/orchestration-recovery-command.js:6-15`, INSTALLED-FILES); this repo's `_ORCA_CLIS` tries `orca-ide` first (`install_user_setup.py:35`, re-read); on the research host `orca` and `orca-ide` run the same launcher (`gap-P3-G02.md` 3.2, host observation). Which name a launched or supervised session actually types is [UNVERIFIED] (`gap-P3-G02.md` R1).
- **What depends on it.** The design's approval-forgery argument (`docs/designs/sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:193`, "terminal and messaging tools are ESCAPE"; the approval rule that a forged approval would trigger is defined in 10.1b) and residual risks R5 and R15 (`:443`, `:453`). No scope decision accepts the alternate names (accepted limits O-7 and R1 cover built or obfuscated names; R12 a hostile agent).
- **Disposition.** The owner confirmed on 2026-11-11 (question C6, "Record as DEFECT, create task"): `gaps-and-questions.md` section D, row P3-G02. Remediation task: `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-FIX-launch-guard-orca-names-20261111-235205/TASK-FIX-launch-guard-orca-names-20261111-235205.md`, a documentation-only draft plan (size, tasks, staging state and review status: 14.2 TD1). **The guard is not fixed, deployed or accepted as a ceiling.** The worktree gate keeps the status "documented ceiling" in the register (every statement of its reach names the literal `orca worktree create`), but it is blind to the same names, and its header list of known ceilings (`:39-61`) omits them. The guard's classification is a DEFECT of partial wiring: effective for the one name `orca`, which is a name Orca itself uses. Counter-considerations recorded in the register: full enforcement is not runtime-approved (issue #442 open), the arm is wired for `orca`, and the live effect on approval forgery was not observed.
- **Related bare-name calls.** `ccsession` (`orca terminal rename`, `show`) and `orca_worktree.py` (every argv; `:193,300,574,601,668,1132,1146,1352` re-read) call the literal `orca`; the issue watcher resolves `orca-ide` first. Inside a governed session an `orca terminal rename` is outside the guard's allow-list (the list holds `terminal list|show|read`), so under the bare name it would be ESCAPE; not probed (`gap-P3-G02.md` 4.5 last row).
