| Service | Purpose | Failure Mode | Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|
| Orca desktop app and CLI (`orca`, `orca-ide`, `orca-dev`) | Worktrees, agent launch, terminals, the whole orchestration surface (Runs, Tasks, Dispatches, mailbox) | Claim: preflight `ok: false` is STOP `E-ORCA-REQUIRED` before any GitHub or ClickUp write (`SKILL.md:148`). Install: no working Orca CLI found, Orca step skipped with a one-line notice; every Orca failure still returns `True`, so install does not fail on it (`install_user_setup.py:239,242,263,278`; test `tests/cli/test_install_user_setup.py:299-310`, per `gap-P3-G03.md` A.3), except a `skills list --json` reply with some `topics` shapes, which raises out of `_find_orca` (it iterates `topics` after the `try`, `install_user_setup.py:219`; shapes in Section 5.6 evidence table; 10.2, L32, owner question C3). Watcher: no Orca stops the cycle with `orca_not_found` (`cycle.py` `_launch`, per research 04 F2) | A list that cannot be trusted never causes a create; the recorded `worktree.json` path is reused only when it exists with the same git common dir, flagged `W-ORCA-LIST-FAILED` (`orca_worktree.py:617-624`, per research 04). Build skew: ORCA-CHECKOUT, INSTALLED-FILES and the running build differ (depth default, Section 9.3; SD-1), so every statement about Orca names its build. The installed build on the research host is `1.4.245-aidev02.9cfecdd30b2a`; the running process is [UNVERIFIED] |
| Orca "Claude command" setting (`ccsession-orca`) | Puts launched agents behind ccsession and its tab title | Not set: warning `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION`; the agent keeps running. The setting is not visible to IronClaude code (`REMEDY` text `orca_worktree.py:786-789`, re-read) | The remedy is a person action ("close it"); no code acts. Host value [UNVERIFIED] |
| Claude Code (host that runs hooks, Skills, subagents) | Executes PreToolUse and UserPromptSubmit hooks, Skill calls, Agent calls; supplies `session_id`, `agent_id`, `agent_type` payload fields | DOCUMENTED (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks): a `PreToolUse` command hook that reaches its timeout is cancelled, its output discarded and the call is not blocked unless `onFailure: "block"` is set (Claude Code v2.1.295 or later); no registration in `hooks.json` sets `onFailure` (grep of the file 2026-11-12: the key does not occur; `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard_registration.py:63-65` pins it for the guard). `--bare`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE=1`, `--safe-mode`, `disableAllHooks` and `allowManagedHooksOnly` switch settings-registered guards off (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, `web-02` findings 4 and 7). Installed Claude Code version, the signal used at a timeout and the subagent `session_id` are [UNVERIFIED] | None in code. The claim preflight detects only `disableAllHooks` (`orca_worktree.py:977-982`); the other routes are documented ceilings, not observed bypasses |
| GitHub (`gh`, `repos/...` API; fork `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`) | Issue read, assignment, comments, issue creation; fork-only origin check | `gh auth status` failure is STOP `E-GH-AUTH`; assign failure `E-ASSIGN-FAILED`; comment or edit failure `E-POST-FAILED`; create failure `E-CREATE-FAILED` (`SKILL.md:147,156,159,160`). The watcher refuses any origin other than `ironbellyorg/ironclaude` (`launch.py:50`, reason `origin_mismatch`) | Files stay in `.dev/issues/<N>/`; a re-run edits comments in place by marker (`SKILL.md:159`). The upstream `SuperClaude-Org/SuperClaude_Framework` is never contacted (project `CLAUDE.md` rule) |
| ClickUp (`scripts/clickup_claim.py`) | Mirror of a claim | Every ClickUp outcome except an argument error is non-fatal (`SKILL.md:87`) | Claim continues without it |
| Local tools: `jq`, `awk`, `uv`, `cat sed tr mkdir mv ln rm`, `git`, Linux `/proc`, `pidfd_open`, `PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER` | Hooks and the identity helper; the IR-4 preflight lists them (`GUARD_COMMANDS` `:841`, `process_interface_gaps` `:847-877`, per research 04) | Worktree gate: missing `jq` or `awk` fails open (`:65-72`, re-read). Identity helper: bound session without `uv` exits 2 "launch job: UV is required for this invocation"; an ungoverned session exits 0 (`launch-job-identity.sh:25-54`, re-read). Non-Linux: claim launch refused (`tests/skills/test_issue_orca_worktree.py:2036`, per research 04) | The claim refuses a governed launch when a tool or interface is missing rather than launching unguarded |
| ccsession gateway and shim (`CCSESSION_GATEWAY_URL`, `CCSESSION_SHIM_URL`) | Model routing behind ccsession | Not traced by any research file (variable names only, `gap-P3-G05.md` F8 tail) | [UNVERIFIED]; outside this reference |

The watcher is off by default: `enabled` defaults to false (`:228`) and a cycle that is not a `--dry-run` stops with `not_enabled` (`:685-686`; Section 5.4, item 7). Configuration surface (CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12): the default file is `~/.config/superclaude/issue-watch.json` (`cli.py:31`; `--config PATH` overrides it, `cli.py:49-59`). Required keys: `schema_version` (the integer 1), `project_id` (`PVT_` plus 1 to 64 letters, digits, `_` or `-`), `worktree` and `approvers_file` (absolute paths; a `~` inside the JSON is not expanded) (`cycle.py:222-226,245-259`). Optional keys: `enabled` (default false, `:228`), `labels.trigger` and `labels.approval` (defaults `issue-watch:trigger` and `issue-watch:approved`, 1 to 50 characters, must differ, `:54-56,232-243`), `owner_account_id` (integer from 1 to `10**20-1`; a login whose account id differs stops the cycle with `account_id_mismatch`, `:251-255,768-769`) and `state_dir` (absolute; default `$XDG_STATE_HOME` or `~/.local/state`, then `superclaude/issue-watch`, `:187-192,261-268`). An unknown top-level or `labels` key is refused (`:216-220,235-239`); a `state_dir` on a network filesystem (`nfs`, `nfs4`, `cifs`, `smb3`, `sshfs`, `fuse.sshfs`, `9p`) is refused when the mount table is readable (`:50-52,270-272`); every such failure is `config_invalid`, and a non-Linux platform is `platform_unsupported` (`:286-291`). Operator commands: Section 5.4 Public Interface. Per-cycle constants and launch limits in 11.1b (row "Issue watcher constants"). A `DISABLED` marker and a day cap stop launches (`cycle.py:1061-1063`, per research 04); the marker is the file `state_dir/DISABLED` and a dangling symlink counts (`record.py:283,422-423`) |
| Orca settings store (Orca desktop app; not a repository file) | Orca-side settings that change IronClaude-visible behavior | `nestedWorkerMaxDepth`, `orchestrationHeartbeatsEnabled`, `orchestrationHeartbeatIntervalMinutes`, `agentCmdOverrides` (the per-agent "Claude command", the setting that must point at `~/.local/bin/ccsession-orca`). **DEPLOYED-RUNNING: never read, [UNVERIFIED].** Defaults in 9.3 come from source files only |
| `~/.agents/skills/{computer-use,orca-cli,orchestration}/SKILL.md`, `~/.agents/.skill-lock.json` | Orca-written discovery stubs (IronClaude ships none) | Linked or copied into `~/.claude/skills/`; written by Orca, writer on the research host not established (`gap-P3-G03.md` F). Host observation |

### 9.2 Environment Variables

Table of the 23 interface variables of the ccsession, Orca tab and claim flow (`gap-P3-G05.md` F8), re-checked where marked. "Writer none" means a grep of `src/superclaude` found no assignment, so the person, an orchestrator or the outer program sets it. `cct/` = `src/superclaude/skills/ccsession-tag/`. "Required" is No for every variable unless stated: each has a documented default or fail-open behavior.

| Variable | Purpose | Default | Required |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ORCA_PANE_KEY` | Non-empty means "in an Orca tab"; names the tab record file (`[A-Za-z0-9_:-]+`, `:` becomes `+`); gates the OSC title, the rename and the reopen. Writer: Orca at PTY spawn (ORCA-CHECKOUT `src/main/ipc/pty/spawn-pane-env.ts:46-63`). Readers: `ccsession-orca:164`; `ccsession:533,568-571,1868,1881-1882`; `session-name.sh:177`. Stability across a restart [UNVERIFIED] | unset outside an Orca terminal | No |
| `ORCA_TERMINAL_HANDLE` | Argument of every `orca terminal ...` call ccsession makes (must match `[A-Za-z0-9_-]+`). Writer: Orca (`local-configure.ts:114-124`). Readers: `ccsession:521-523,537,550,555,585-590,617-622`; `session-name.sh:172` | unset | No |
| `CCSESSION_AUTOSTART` | Only the value `0` is special: untagged launch, never asked (`ccsession-orca:147-148,221-224`, re-read `:147`). Writer none | `1` | No |
| `CCSESSION_LAUNCH_ID` | `$$.$RANDOM$RANDOM`, exported by ccsession (`ccsession:1889-1890`, re-read); the part before the dot is compared with the firing Claude's PID to decide hook ownership (`session-name.sh:144-163`). Forging it grants nothing to any guard (`tests/hooks/test_launch_job_identity.py:318-355`) | unset until ccsession runs | No |
| `CCSESSION_LAUNCH_CWD` | Folder in which the tab record is written (`ccsession:1893-1894`, re-read; read `session-name.sh:206`) | unset until ccsession runs | No |
| `CLAUDE_TOPIC` | The launch label; replaces Claude's `session_title` on SessionStart (`ccsession:1930`, re-read; unset `:1900`; read `session-name.sh:166-169`) | unset | No |
| `CCSESSION_TOPIC_CWD` | Label applies only if the hook's folder equals it (`ccsession:1934-1935`; `session-name.sh:167-168`) | unset | No |
| `CCSESSION_HOOK_EVENT` | Event fallback when the payload has none (`session-start.sh:44` writes, `session-name.sh:77` reads) | unset | No |
| `CCSESSION_HOOK_PARENT` | Start of the walk to the firing Claude process, digits only (`session-start.sh:44`; `session-name.sh:114-117`) | unset (`$PPID` used) | No |
| `CCSESSION_NAME` | Valid name wins over auto-naming and is never asked; invalid: warning and ignored (`ccsession-orca:130-139,195-196`, re-read `:130,136`); removed from Claude's environment (`:183,242`). With a non-conforming name the claim's `SHIM_TITLE` does not match. Writer none (README: "how an orchestrator names a session") | unset | No |
| `CCSESSION_ASK_TIMEOUT` | Seconds before the session-name question auto-names (`ccsession:1642`, re-read). Any non-digit value (for example `30s`) becomes `0`, meaning no limit (`:1643-1647`, per `gap-P3-G05.md` F2.6); the README states it in the `--ask` exit-status list (`README.md:397-399`, example `30s`) and not in its Time limit section (`:320-330`) | `30`; `0` removes the limit | No |
| `CCSESSION_AUTO_NAME_STAMP` | Replaces the `MMDD-HHMMSS-xxxx` stamp, cut to 24 characters; test hook (`ccsession:652-656`) | unset | No |
| `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_TERMINAL_TITLE` | Exported as `1` by ccsession when `ORCA_PANE_KEY` is set, so ccsession owns the tab title (`ccsession:1869`, re-read; read `:533`). Orca documents that it drops the title Orca's idle-wait uses (ORCA-CHECKOUT `tui-agent-config.ts:29-31`); runtime effect [UNVERIFIED] | unset | No |
| `CLAUDECODE` | Set by Claude Code; a ccsession run from Claude's Bash tool then drops `ORCA_*` and refuses reopen (`ccsession:615,1881-1883`). Writer is outside the repository [UNVERIFIED in repo] | unset | No |
| `CLAUDE_ENV_FILE` | Set by Claude Code; `session-start.sh` appends `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID=<id>` to it (`session-start.sh:29-31`) | unset | No |
| `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` | Made visible to Claude's Bash tool through the file above; not part of the title flow | unset | No |
| `SC_ORCA_TIMEOUT` | Seconds; a float above 0 replaces **every** `run()` timeout, including the 15 s terminal reads; empty, non-numeric or non-positive keeps the default (`orca_worktree.py:83,112-121`, re-read) | unset (120 s most calls, 30 s preflight, 15 s terminal read/show: `:80-82`) | No |
| `SC_ORCA_SHIM_INTERVAL` | Seconds between the 5 shim-check tries; invalid gives 2.0, negative gives 0 (`orca_worktree.py:781-782,1122-1126`; tests set `0`) | `2.0` | No |
| `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` | Read by the claim preflight: set and not equal to `$HOME/.claude` fails the launch with `W-ENFORCEMENT-ABSENT` (`orca_worktree.py:959-967`, re-read), and by `install_mcp.py:721`. The installer drops it from the environment of the plugin step (`install_user_setup.py:420-421`, a removal, not a read) and leaves it in the environment of the Orca step, which then targets `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR>/skills` when the value is the user's and not one Orca injected for a managed Claude account (Section 5.6 item 4; Orca writes `ORCA_CLAUDE_INJECTED_CONFIG_DIR` and `ORCA_CLAUDE_USER_CONFIG_DIR` beside an injected value, `claude-profile-routing.ts:4-8`, ORCA-CHECKOUT; whether this host uses managed accounts is [UNVERIFIED]; the claim preflight above reads the same variable regardless of who set it, `orca_worktree.py:959-967`) (see 10.2). ccsession and its hooks do not read it (they use `$HOME/.claude`) (`gaps-and-questions.md` E) | unset | No |
| `HOME` | Home of every state path above; hooks exit 0 when it is missing or the store is absent (`launch-job-identity.sh:20-24`, re-read; `launch-level-guard.sh:23`) | account home | No (the guards and the identity hook exit 0 when it is missing or the store is absent) |
| `XDG_STATE_HOME` | Tab records base (`ccsession:564`, re-read) | `$HOME/.local/state` | No |
| `XDG_CACHE_HOME` | Title marks base (`ccsession:518-525` per `gap-P3-G05.md` F2.2) | `$HOME/.cache` | No |
| `CLAUDE_BIN` | Claude executable (`ccsession:134`, re-read; `:1964-1973`); tests substitute a recording fake | `claude` | No |

Also read by the same scripts but internal, not interface: `CCSESSION_NAME_KEY`, `PS_TABLE` (`ccsession:310,387,454,478`; `session-name.sh:89`). Other `ccsession-tag` variables for model, tier, profile and shim machinery were not traced for behavior (names and first lines only, `gap-P3-G05.md` F8 tail): `CCSESSION_PERMISSIONS`, `CC_CLAUDE_FLAGS`, `CC_SHIM_SCRIPT`, `CC_SHIM_PORT`, `CCSESSION_ENV_FILE`, `CCSESSION_PROFILES_DIR`, `CCSESSION_GATEWAY_URL`, `CCSESSION_SHIM_URL`, `CCSESSION_PROFILE`, and the tier variables in `models.py`. No `CCSESSION_*` variable is read under `src/superclaude` outside `skills/ccsession-tag` (the only hit is the Python constant `CCSESSION_SCRIPT`, `install_hooks.py:107,115`).

Bearing on the DEFECT in 8.2 | unset | No |

Not observed on this host; nothing in this repository sets them | unset | No |

### 9.3 Feature Flags

Settings and flags that change behavior, with the default of each build kept separate.

| Flag | Description | Default | Impact When Toggled |
|---|---|---|---|
| `nestedWorkerMaxDepth` (Orca setting) | Cap on dispatch depth; a coordinator-started worker has depth 1, a helper it starts would be child depth 2 (refused when `childDepth > maxDepth`, `db/dispatch-depth.ts:165-179`, ORCA-CHECKOUT) | **ORCA-CHECKOUT `dc57b3d6e`: 1** (`src/shared/nested-worker-depth.ts:4`, `default-global-settings.ts:153`, re-read). **INSTALLED-FILES `1.4.245-aidev02.9cfecdd30b2a`: 2**, a stored legacy value of 1 migrated once to 2 (`out/shared/nested-worker-depth.js:10,12,37-41`; `default-global-settings.js:161-162`, re-read); the same file records "upstream v1.4.251+ defaults this to 4" (`:7`). **Running Orca: [UNVERIFIED]** (never read). The IronClaude CHECKOUT stores no value | At 1 a worker's helper start is refused with `nested_worker_depth_exceeded` (`db/dispatch-depth.ts:170-177`, ORCA-CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12; `consumer_fenced` is the separate refusal for a caller with no worker-start authority, Section 5.5 item 1); at 2 it is not, and the only barrier left is contract rule WC-13 (text, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:62`; the Orca hook does not inspect `worker-start`). Non-integer or below-1 values fall back to the default. The preamble advertises SUB-DISPATCH when `depth < max` (`canDispatchSubWorkers`), so at 2 the preamble invites the helper start that WC-13 forbids |
| `orchestrationHeartbeatsEnabled` | Whether new preambles tell workers to heartbeat | on (absent means on); `false` strictly turns it off (`orchestration-heartbeat.ts:4,34-40`, re-read); same in INSTALLED-FILES (`default-global-settings.js:164-165`) | Off: the preamble contains no heartbeat command at all, so a missing heartbeat means nothing. Renderer-only setting, so workers cannot change their own policy (`:1`). The value is baked into each preamble at dispatch time; changing it does not affect workers already dispatched. Running value [UNVERIFIED] |
| `orchestrationHeartbeatIntervalMinutes` | Minutes between heartbeats | 5; range 1-60; anything else (0, 61, 2.5, strings) becomes 5 (`orchestration-heartbeat.ts:6,10-12,15-31`, re-read) | The "two missed intervals" threshold (`:9`) is a warning only in the retired scheduler (`coordinator-task-dispatch.ts:16-34`); on the live surface Orca derives no verdict from heartbeat age. Heartbeat age as a stall indicator is IronClaude policy (S-table, deadline W) |
| `IR7_REFUSE` (L2 pre-push refusal) | Whether a missing L2 hook refuses the launch | CHECKOUT: no such switch; the claim warns `W-L2-ABSENT` only (`orca_worktree.py:800-801`). ORIGIN/MASTER and the installed copy: `IR7_REFUSE = False` (research 04 F5, installed line 819, not traced further) | Warning only in both; ORIGIN/MASTER also installs an owned L2 pre-push hook |
| `--unattended` and `--report-dir <dir>` (command flags, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:12-15`) | Switch a command to worker mode; the one report directory | off; `--unattended` counts only as a standalone token outside quotes | Person-mode flags (`--interactive`, `--format interactive`, `--mode socratic`, `--watch`, `--monitor 1`) are refused with `worker_status: failed`; neither or both report directories is refused with the exact chat error at `:38`. The `--monitor 1` clause has no implementing `## Unattended mode` section in pr-submit (owner question C2) |
| `/sc:issue --claim` launch level: command flags `--pipeline` and `--submit`; claim-helper argument `--level` | The command takes `--pipeline worktree\|full\|none` and `--submit` (only with `--pipeline full`) (`src/superclaude/commands/issue.md:43-44`). `--level worktree\|full` is not a command flag: it is the required argument of the helper subcommands `prompt` and `launch` (`orca_worktree.py:1448,1458`), together with `--submit`; the skill passes the level `<L>` to `launch` (`refs/claim.md:118`). The level word written to `pending/<nonce>` is `worktree`, `full`, or `full-submit` when `--submit` is given (`orca_worktree.py:1334`); `main` rejects `--submit` without `--level full` (`:1467-1468`) | `--pipeline`: `worktree`; `--submit`: off (`issue.md:43-44`); the helper's `--level` has no default (required) | `--pipeline none` ends the claim before Wave 7 and 8: no worktree, no agent (`refs/claim.md:93`). `--submit` with a pipeline other than `full`, or with a value, is refused before Activation with `E-SUBMIT-NEEDS-FULL` (`sc-issue-protocol/SKILL.md:144`) |
| `onFailure` (Claude Code hook field) | Makes a failing or timed-out hook block | Not set on any registration here | DOCUMENTED: with `"block"` a timeout blocks (Claude Code v2.1.295 or later); the design rejects it because a broken hook would then block ungoverned sessions (`merged-requirements.md:98,694,701`, per `gap-P3-G06.md` 2) |
| `disableAllHooks`, `allowManagedHooksOnly`, `--bare`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE`, `--safe-mode` (Claude Code) | Switch the settings-registered guards off | not set on the research host (`disableAllHooks` only) | DOCUMENTED routes, not observed bypasses; `disableAllHooks` alone is detected by the claim preflight |
| Worker start readiness `timeoutMs` (Orca) | Deadline for the agent to become ready | 60 s (`local-worker-start.ts:145,228`, ORCA-CHECKOUT) | Expiry throws "Agent did not become ready" for a terminal worker (`:245-257`); the 30 s ask limit of ccsession is chosen to stay under it. The resulting Dispatch state was not traced |

## 10. Error Handling & Edge Cases

### 10.1 Error Handling Patterns

#### 10.1a Pattern table

| Error Category | Handling Pattern | User Experience | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| Hook exit-code contract | All three guards: allow = exit 0; block = one message on stderr plus exit 2; never exit 1 (`require-orca...sh:11`, `launch-level-guard.sh:4-5`; the Orca gate "runs no Python", `research/03` A.1). DOCUMENTED: exit 2 blocks and stderr is the reason; exit 0 applies any JSON; any other code with plain-text, empty or invalid-JSON stdout is a **non-blocking** error (the call goes ahead, a `<hook name> hook error` notice shows the first stderr line); any other code with schema-valid JSON is decided by the JSON (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, "Exit code output", per `web-01` Q2). "Never exit 1" is the repository's own convention, not a documented requirement | A block shows the message to the model; the person sees the tool call refused | Fix the command (add `--agent`, drop the forbidden class) or, for a governed session, a person deletes `sessions/<sid>` and `.ok` in their own terminal (the LL-TAMPER message, `launch-level-guard.sh:97`) |
| Worktree gate (`require-orca-worktree-agent.sh`) | Blocks only a lexically recognised `orca worktree create` with no `--agent` and no `--help`/`-h`, on Bash calls; fails open (exit 0) on missing `jq` (`:65-68`), missing `awk` (`:69-72`), empty stdin (`:343`), invalid JSON, non-Bash tool, missing or non-string command, any scanner error (`:346-349`) | Message at `:351-357` (rule, the exact form `orca worktree create --name <name> --agent claude --prompt "<task>"`, ownership sentences). Role sentences are advice; the script has no role, session or worker predicate (grep: only `:313,355,356`) | Re-issue with `--agent claude --prompt "<task>"`. A coordinator starts supervised workers with `orca orchestration worker-start`; a worker never does (WC-13, `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:62`). The last line of the block message ("Supervised workers use `orca orchestration worker-start`", `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:356`) describes how a coordinator starts supervised workers; it is not an instruction to a blocked worker |
| Launch-level guard, governed session | Fail closed on its own failure paths: EXIT trap `ll_on_exit` turns any exit other than 0 or a deliberate deny into LL-BROKEN (tool branch) or LL-NOBIND (prompt branch), exit 2 (`:147-163`, re-read); jq failure with a bound id in the text, input errors, unknown class/level pairs and the last line also end in LL-BROKEN (`:740,747`). **Qualified by 10.3**: this does not cover a hook stopped at its timeout, identity loss, or an ungoverned session | One of the denial messages in 10.1b | Per message (release by a person; restart the claim) |
| Launch-level guard, ungoverned session | Never blocked by a failure; EXIT trap exits 0 when the session is not governed (`:153-155`); no usable `session_id` means ungoverned for a tool call (`:390-394`); a prompt that carries a launch marker and has no usable `session_id` is denied `LL-NOBIND` instead (5.3 Failure behavior) | Nothing | None needed |
| Identity hook | Governed Bash call that cannot be wrapped safely: exit 2 "launch job: governed Bash call cannot be wrapped safely" (unsafe store, bad level word, duplicate JSON keys, an envelope of 65,537 to 1,048,576 bytes (`:446-447`; above 1,048,576 bytes the hook exits 0 and does not wrap the call, `:417-419`, row in 10.3), non-string `agent_id`/`agent_type`, command over 32768 bytes or with NUL, nested runner, non-Linux, no pidfd, missing runner); bound session without `uv`: exit 2 "launch job: UV is required for this invocation"; every unbound (ungoverned) case exits 0 (`launch-job-identity.sh:20-54`, re-read; hook body per `research/03` E.3) | Every Bash call of a `degraded`-bound session is refused. The wrap-failure message is one line with no cause or remedy (`launch-job-identity.sh:485`) | Missing `uv`: install `uv` (the message names it, `:52`). For the ten wrap-failure causes listed the code states no recovery: a person inspects the launch-level store and releases the session by deleting its `sessions/<sid>` files in their own terminal (the same release as the LL-TAMPER message, `launch-level-guard.sh:97`) |
| Claim helper (`orca_worktree.py`) | Every runtime outcome prints one JSON object and exits **0** (`emit`, `:186-189`, re-read); exit 2 only for argparse errors, including `--submit` without `--level full` (`:1467-1468`); every subprocess goes through `run()` without a shell and never raises: timeouts and missing binaries return a failed `Result` (`:124-147`, re-read) | The skill reads the JSON `status`, `warning`, `detail`; W- codes are advisory | Per code in 10.1c; `already_launched` blocks a second agent once a launch is recorded; re-running the claim is idempotent except after a launch timeout, when check Orca first (5.4 item 4, 10.1c) |
| Claim skill STOPs | `E-` codes stop the claim before further writes (10.1d) | STOP message naming the code | Fix the cause and re-run; files stay in `.dev/issues/<N>/` |
| `src/superclaude/skills/sc-issue-protocol/scripts/origin_repo.py` | Exit codes `0` success, `1` `E-NO-ORIGIN`, `2` argparse, `3` `E-FOREIGN-REPO`, `4` `E-NOT-AN-ISSUE`; message on stderr (`origin_repo.py:45-50`, re-read) | The skill prints the stderr line with the STOP (`refs/claim.md:15`) | Correct the argument |
| Issue watcher launch | `outcome_of` maps: spawn error to `spawn_error`; timeout, non-zero exit, non-JSON, no id or the text `agent_session_operation_unknown` to `unknown`; only exit 0 plus a JSON object with a valid operation id gives `launched` (`launch.py:326-349` per research 04). The pending record is written durably before the launch call. Stop codes `not_enabled` (the default until the config sets `enabled`, `cycle.py:685-686`), `orca_not_found`, `origin_mismatch`, `prompt_invalid`; a launch started after `LAUNCH_CUTOFF_S` is refused | `status` prints a retry command with `--retry-launch <id>` for `unknown` entries; it never runs it (`cycle.py:1308-1325`) | Operator pastes the retry command; ids must match `^[A-Za-z0-9._:-]{1,128}$` |
| Installer | Orca and plugin steps do not fail `install`/`update`: every return path of `install_orca_skills` has `True` first (`install_user_setup.py:239,242,263,278`), except a `skills list --json` reply with some `topics` shapes, which raises out of `_find_orca` (it iterates `topics` after the `try`, `:219`; shapes in Section 5.6 evidence table; 10.2, L32, owner question C3). `wire_ccsession` returning `False` makes `install` and `update` exit 1 (`main.py:274`, `:423`). `doctor` exits 1 when any of seven checks fails, and fails where the installer only warns (missing `ccsession-orca`, an unmanaged link) | One-line notices; doctor details only with `--verbose` | `superclaude update` adds a missing `ccsession-orca` (it also overwrites the shipped files it copies with no backup, apart from the exceptions listed in Section 5.6 item 1); to retry the Orca skills, rerun the installer's own request, `<orca> skills install --agent claude-code --skill computer-use --skill orca-cli --skill orchestration` (Section 5.6 item 3); the installer's warning prints only `<orca> skills install`, which with no `--skill` or `--all` lists installable names and installs nothing (`install_user_setup.py:243-245,256,269`; ORCA-CHECKOUT `src/cli/specs/skills.ts:90`, `src/cli/skills.test.ts:217-233`) |
| Worker contract outcomes | `--outcome failed` when `worker_status` is `failed` or `blocked` or no contract exists; `succeeded` for `succeeded` or `partial`; `guard refused:` bodies for a brief that fails the brief guard; exactly one `worker_done` (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:16-22,59`, re-read). Nothing in code enforces these | Coordinator sees `worker_done` plus the contract file | WC-6: every retry is a new Task; at most 2 brief fixes (3 attempts) after `guard refused:` (`:244` per research 02) |
| Orca-side refusals | `consumer_fenced` (caller is neither the bound coordinator nor a worker on one provable active Dispatch, or a creator Dispatch no longer active); `nested_worker_depth_exceeded` (`childDepth > maxDepth`); `worker_cannot_coordinate_own_run`, `worker_cannot_take_over_run`; `dispatch_run_mismatch`; `orchestration_migration_required` (`command_retired`, `client_contract_missing`, `client_contract_unsupported` against `ORCHESTRATION_CONTRACT_VERSION = 1`); report rejections `unknown_task`, `unknown_dispatch`, `task_dispatch_mismatch`, `inactive_dispatch`, `stale_dispatch`; `worker_list_cursor_expired`, `worker_list_snapshot_too_large` (ORCA-CHECKOUT `worker-start-authority.ts`, `worker-run-binding.ts`, `dispatch-depth.ts:165-179`, `orchestration-contract-fence.ts:19-25`, `worker-report-settlement.ts:73-161`, `worker-list-method.ts:107-160`, per research 05 and `gap-P3-G01`) | Orca prints "No effects were applied." with next steps | Follow the printed recovery command; a worker stops without `worker_done` on `consumer_fenced` (WC-14) |

#### 10.1b Launch-level guard messages and decision order

Classes are checked in this order and the first blocked class supplies the message: `BIG TAMPER MERGE GHWRITE PROTECTED ESCAPE MCP UNKNOWN SUBMIT REPO PUSH PUSHFORM COMMIT TURN IMPL SKILL` (`launch-level-guard.sh:722`, re-read). Message ids: `LL-BIG`, `LL-TAMPER`, `LL-MERGE`, `LL-GH-WRITE`, `LL-PROTECTED`, `LL-ESCAPE`, `LL-MCP`, `LL-UNKNOWN`, `LL-SUBMIT`, `LL-REPO`, `LL-PUSH`, `LL-PUSH-FORM`, `LL-COMMIT`, `LL-TURN`, `LL-IMPL`, `LL-SKILL`, plus `LL-BROKEN` and `LL-NOBIND` for internal failures; `LL-STATE` and `LL-TEXT` are notes appended to a block (`:724-747`, re-read). Levels: `W` worktree, `WA` worktree approved, `F` full, `FS` full-submit. **Approval.** The record `sessions/<sid>` holds the level word the claim script chose. An approval exists only at level `W`: when the prompt, trimmed and lower-cased, is exactly `approved`, `approved.` or `approved!`, or its first line starts with `/sc:implement`, `/sc:task` or `/task` as a whole word, the guard creates the file `sessions/<sid>.ok` (`launch-level-guard.sh:341-363`), and a `.ok` file beside a `W` record makes the level `WA` (`:270-272`). The approval lasts until a person deletes the session's files (`:364`); at `F` and `FS` no approval is read. Which classes block at which level is in the table below. The classification works on text, not parsed shell: `bash -c "git commit -m x"` and `echo "git commit"` are both treated as a commit (G19, G20 in `research/03` G.2, FULL).

| Class | Trigger (source line, `launch-level-guard.sh`) | Levels that block | Message id |
|---|---|---|---|
| BIG | A Bash or Monitor command over 65,536 bytes (`:422-423`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-BIG` |
| TAMPER | Text naming `install_hooks`, a word ending `.claude`, `install` after a `superclaude` word, the guard's own store, `.claude/settings`, `.claude/hooks`, `disableAllHooks`, `.gitconfig`, `.git/config` or `.config/git`; an Edit or Write path holding `..` (`:443-452`, `:680`, `:705-713`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-TAMPER` |
| MERGE | git `merge` or `pull`; a `gh` merge, `update-branch` or `sync`; a `repos/.../merge` endpoint; an MCP tool name holding `merge` (`:526-527`, `:562-563`, `:591`, `:647`, `:696`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-MERGE` |
| GHWRITE | `gh api` other than one plain GET; a GitHub API host in any word (`:495-523`, `:565`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-GH-WRITE` |
| PROTECTED | A canonical `git push` whose destination is `master` or `main` (`:665`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-PROTECTED` |
| ESCAPE | A word `ccsession*`, `tmux`, `screen`, `xdotool` or `ydotool` (`:457`); an `orca` word outside the read-only allow-list unless the segment ends in `--help` or `-h` (`:458-478`); `claude` with a launch flag (`:482`); Skill `issue` (`:414`); a remote-isolated Agent or Task (`:683`); RemoteTrigger, Workflow or SendMessage (`:687`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-ESCAPE` |
| MCP | An MCP tool whose name holds push, commit, pull_request, create_or_update_file, delete_file, update_ref, create_ref or create_branch, unless it starts `list_`, `get_`, `search_` or `read_` (`:693-698`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-MCP` |
| UNKNOWN | A git or gh form the tables do not list, including a git `remote`, `config` or `worktree` form outside the read-only ones and an alias, `pushurl`, `insteadof` or `hookspath` word; a backslash-newline inside a word; a `gh` repo option without a value (`:429`, `:532-533`, `:586`, `:590-596`, `:634`, `:641`, `:650-653`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-UNKNOWN` |
| SUBMIT | `gh pr create`, `edit`, `comment` or `ready`; `gh issue create`, `edit` or `comment`; Skill `pr-submit` (`:595`, `:413`) | W, WA, F | `LL-SUBMIT` |
| REPO | `gh pr create` without a repo operand in its own segment (`:599-607`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-REPO` |
| PUSH | `git push`, `send-pack` or `http-push` (`:528-529`, `:648`) | W, WA, F | `LL-PUSH` |
| PUSHFORM | A push that is not `git push [-u\|--set-upstream] origin <branch>[:<dest>]`, or whose destination is `HEAD` (`:661-667`) | W, WA, F, FS | `LL-PUSH-FORM` |
| COMMIT | `git commit`, `cherry-pick`, `revert`, `am`, `commit-tree`, or `update-ref` on `HEAD`, with `--stdin` or on `refs/heads` (`:530-531`, `:642-646`) | W, WA, F | `LL-COMMIT` |
| TURN | A Cron or Schedule tool call (`:684-685`) | W | `LL-TURN` |
| IMPL | Skill `implement`, `task`, `improve`, `cleanup`, `spawn` or `build` (`:412`) | W | `LL-IMPL` |
| SKILL | A Skill call with an empty name (`:415`) | W, WA, F | `LL-SKILL` |

The decision table is at `:722-742`; a class and level pair it does not list ends in `LL-BROKEN` (`:740`).

#### 10.1c Claim helper warning codes (advisory `W-`)

| Code | Meaning | Recovery (`sc-issue-protocol/refs/claim.md:103-112` worktree codes, `:122-127` launch codes) | Source (`orca_worktree.py`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `W-ORCA-LIST-FAILED` | `worktree list` unusable; a recorded path is reused only if it exists with the same git common dir, flagged unverified | Check Orca, re-run the claim | `:213` (order), `:620-622` (emitted), `:310-331` (list rejection rules) |
| `W-ORIGIN-BASE-MISSING` | Base ref missing; no create | `git fetch origin`, re-run | `:214` |
| `W-WORKTREE-CREATE-FAILED` | Not a git checkout, create failed, zero new rows, or a created path not on disk | Re-run; a finished create is reused | `:215` (order), `:613`, `:683-685`, `:699-701`, `:702-706` |
| `W-WORKTREE-AMBIGUOUS` | Several live matching worktrees, or several new worktrees after a create | Remove the extra worktrees, re-run | `:216` (order), `:637-640`, `:696-698` |
| `W-WORKTREE-STALE` | No live match, or git common dir differs | `git worktree prune`, then `orca worktree rm` | `:217` |
| `W-WORKTREE-COPY-FAILED` | Copy of the issue directory into the worktree failed | Copy the named files by hand | `:218` |
| `W-ORIGIN-BASE-STALE` | `git fetch origin` failed (note only) | `git fetch origin`, rebase in the worktree | `:219` |
| `W-FIRST-TAB-LEFT` | Empty first tab left open (cosmetic; ranks last) | Close the plain tab in Orca by hand | `:220` |
| `W-LAUNCH-FAILED` | Launch refused or failed | Re-run the claim; the hand command is printed for reference (an agent started by hand is not governed by the hook). Exception 1, a timeout: when the `detail` says "orca agent launch timed out; an agent may have started, check Orca before re-running" (`:1359-1360`), check Orca first and close any stray agent, because nothing was recorded and `already_launched` will not stop a second launch (Section 5.4 item 4); the `REMEDY` text itself says only "re-run the claim" (`:785`). Exception 2: when the `detail` says the worktree's `launch-prompt.md` is not the one rendered for this claim, no agent was started and no hand command is printed; make that file match `<D>/launch-prompt.md` first, because a re-run alone fails again | `:785`, `:1276` |
| `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION` | Shim check found neither screen text nor title; status stays `launched` | This agent is NOT behind the ccsession shim, or could not be verified; close it. Orca's Claude command must be the `ccsession-orca` wrapper | `:786-789`, `:1378-1386` |
| `W-ENFORCEMENT-ABSENT` | IR-4 preflight failed; no agent started, no record written | Fix each condition the `detail` names (most are fixed by `superclaude install`; a stale installed guard or registration needs `superclaude install --force`, which overwrites the shipped files it copies with no backup, apart from the exceptions listed in Section 5.6 item 1), then claim the issue again | `:790-794`, `:1308-1325` |
| `W-L2-ABSENT` | No pre-push hook at git's hook path; launch goes ahead (CHECKOUT wording "not built yet" is stale against ORIGIN/MASTER) | None required: the agent was started and only the L1 guard enforces the limits (the design's IR-7 asks for a refusal here; the claim only warns) | `:795-798`, `:1332-1333` |

`WARNING_ORDER` (`:212-221`) holds only the eight worktree codes and is read only by `Outcome.as_dict` (`:247-252`): a code raised through `Outcome.fail` (`:241-245`) is the `warning` and `WARNING_ORDER` is not used; otherwise the earliest listed code among the notes becomes the `warning`. Either way the other notes are appended to `detail`. Launch codes are not in that list: `launch_result` (`:1224-1228`) takes the first note appended as `warning` and looks up `REMEDY[code]` (`:784-799`) for every note. Result statuses seen in `launch`: `launched`, `failed`, `already_launched`; `prompt` returns `kept` when nothing needs rewriting.

#### 10.1d Claim skill STOP codes (`E-`)

The 13 STOP codes of `sc-issue-protocol/SKILL.md:144-160`, with the recovery the source states ("none stated" means the source gives the cause only):

| Code | Cause | Recovery |
|---|---|---|
| `E-CREATE-ONLY-FLAG`, `E-CLAIM-ONLY-FLAG`, `E-SUBMIT-NEEDS-FULL` | A flag that belongs to the other mode (`--depth`, `--mode`, `--type` with `--claim`; `--pipeline`, `--submit` with `--create`), or `--submit` with a value or without `--pipeline full` (`SKILL.md:144`) | The command stops with usage before Activation; correct the flags. Remedy beyond that: none stated |
| `E-NO-ORIGIN` | No `origin` remote, git failed, or `origin` is not a GitHub URL; `origin_repo.py` exit 1 (`SKILL.md:145`; `origin_repo.py:45-50`) | The skill prints the script's stderr line with the STOP (`refs/claim.md:15`); no further remedy stated |
| `E-FOREIGN-REPO` | The claim URL names a repository other than `origin`; `origin_repo.py` exit 3 (`SKILL.md:146`) | Same: stderr line printed; no further remedy stated |
| `E-NOT-AN-ISSUE` | The argument is a pull request or not an issue number or URL; `origin_repo.py` exit 4 (`SKILL.md:153`; `refs/claim.md:40`) | Message "#N is a pull request, not an issue; not claimed."; no further remedy stated |
| `E-GH-AUTH` | `gh auth status` fails (`SKILL.md:147`) | None stated |
| `E-ORCA-REQUIRED` | `orca_worktree.py preflight` prints `ok: false` (Orca not installed, not running or not reachable), before any GitHub or ClickUp write (`SKILL.md:148`) | Print "/sc:issue --claim needs Orca running; start Orca and re-run" and then the script's `detail` (`refs/claim.md:17`) |
| `E-TIER-UNAVAILABLE` | A worker spawn failed because the picker ID is not served (`SKILL.md:154`) | The STOP names the agent file and the picker ID; no alias fallback (`refs/tiers.md:32`) |
| `E-WORKER-NO-OUTPUT` | A worker returned no file, or an empty one (`SKILL.md:155`) | The worker is retried once; then STOP with the expected path |
| `E-ASSIGN-FAILED` | `gh issue edit <N> --repo <R> --add-assignee @me` fails (`SKILL.md:156`) | None stated; the STOP comes before ClickUp and before any comment, so nothing was written |
| `E-POST-FAILED` | The `gh issue comment` or the comment `PATCH` fails (`SKILL.md:159`) | Files stay in `.dev/issues/<N>/`; a re-run edits the comments by marker instead of duplicating them |
| `E-CREATE-FAILED` | `gh issue create --repo <R>` fails (`SKILL.md:160`) | The draft directory is kept; no further remedy stated |

Watcher and worker-contract codes of other families (`E-LEGACY`, `E-HALT-QA`, `E-NO-RULING`, `E-RESUME-NEEDED`, `E-ARCHIVE-PACKAGE`) belong to `/sc:workflow` and `/sc:implement` and are cited here only so the reader does not search for them (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:57`; `gap-P3-G04.md` 4.2).

### 10.2 Known Edge Cases

| Scenario | Current Behavior | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **DEFECT (launch-level guard) and documented ceiling (worktree gate):** Orca CLI invoked as `orca-ide`, `orca-dev` or `orca.cmd` | Neither the worktree gate nor the guard's ESCAPE class sees it (rc 0 in the probes of 8.2) | Recorded DEFECT for the guard, owner-confirmed 2026-11-11 (the gate stays a documented ceiling); remediation draft `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-FIX-launch-guard-orca-names-20261111-235205/`; **not fixed** |
| `$ORCA worktree create` versus `echo orca` | Gate blocks the `$`-led word (a guess, never expands the variable); guard does not treat `$ORCA` as Orca but blocks `echo orca` (any `orca` word with no allowed sub-command) and `bash -c "orca ..."`, `xargs orca ...`, which the gate allows | The two scripts disagree in both directions (`G2` probes P16, P18, P19, P22, P23 in `gap-P3-G02.md`) |
| Compliant bare-name create inside a bound session | `orca worktree create ... --agent claude` is ESCAPE (blocked); the same under `orca-ide` passes | An inversion produced by the DEFECT (`G2-P26`, `G2-P27`) |
| Forged or replayed leading launch marker | Publishes `degraded`; guard treats it as `W`; identity hook then blocks every Bash call | A forged marker can only bind at the strictest level; the proposed alignment (add `degraded` to `LEVELS`, `launch-job-identity.sh:74`) is not applied (owner question C12) |
| Subagent calls inside a governed session | The guard never reads `agent_id` on PreToolUse; a subagent is governed only if its payload carries the parent's `session_id` | Subagent `session_id` is [UNVERIFIED]: the documentation is silent; tests use hand-written payloads; OB-H1 item 1 skipped (`gaps-and-questions.md` B) |
| Hooks run inside subagents | The gate's header claims it (`:5-6`) | DOCUMENTED (hooks reference, subagents page, `web-02` finding 1), not observed |
| Claim worktree is created without `--agent` | Allowed by the claim script; Orca opens a plain first tab, closed afterwards; agent started by a later `agent launch` | Accepted exception (owner decision 2026-11-08, `sc-issue-claim-pipeline-flag.md:11,194,380`); rule text in `core/CLAUDE.md:55` and `CLAUDE.md:312` does not name it (owner question C1) |
| Watcher-started session | First session is unmarked and ungoverned; it can run `orca agent launch` through the claim script | Chain inferred, not run (research 04 Path B) |
| `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` set | Claim preflight rejects it; ccsession ignores it; the installer's Orca child inherits it and, for a user-set value that Orca did not inject (Section 5.6 item 4), writes to `<CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR>/skills` while the installer checks `~/.claude/skills`, so the post-check can report `0/3 files present` as a warning | Derived from source, not run (owner questions C4, C5, C18); `docs/getting-started/installation.md:497-504` says the installer "always writes under `$HOME/.claude/`", [CODE-CONTRADICTED] for this step by derivation |
| `_find_orca` and a malformed `skills list --json` reply | `topics` is read outside the `try` (`install_user_setup.py:216-219`), so a reply with exit 0 whose `topics` is null, a number, a boolean, a non-empty string or object, or a list holding a non-object element before the first `orca-cli` object raises out of `superclaude install` at step 7 (and out of `update`) with a traceback, while an empty string, an empty object, an absent `topics` or a list of objects does not (exact shapes: Section 5.6 evidence table); pinned by `tests/issue_watch/test_find_orca_parity.py:8-15,69-90,173-188` | A well-formed Orca does not trigger it (owner question C3) |
| doctor versus installer | `superclaude install` can exit 0 while `superclaude doctor` exits 1 (missing `ccsession-orca`, unmanaged link) | Doctor checks no Orca skill, Orca CLI or guard script (`gap-P3-G03.md` C.2-C.3) |
| Session name given by `CCSESSION_NAME` or Claude `--name` | Label does not take the auto-name shape, so `SHIM_TITLE` misses and the claim warns `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION` | The claim itself passes neither (`gap-P3-G05.md` F1) |
| Stale `SHIM_MARKERS[1]` | `Session name (Enter or Ctrl+C` is the old question text; a prompt-carrying launch is never asked, so no effect on the claim path today | Test `test_la5_...[question]` passes on text the wrapper no longer prints (owner question C17) |
| Malformed `CCSESSION_ASK_TIMEOUT` (for example `30s`) | Silently means no limit | Documented in the README `--ask` exit-status list (`README.md:397-399`), absent from its Time limit section (`:320-330`) |
| Worker creates a new Run | `orchestration.runCreate` has no worker check, so a worker with an active Dispatch can become the bound coordinator of a new Run (and `resolveWorkerStartRun` then takes the coordinator path) | The nested-worker depth cap is the only Orca barrier in source; subordinate mode is deferred, not shipped, and forbidden only by WC-13 text (`research/05` 4.2, 7.5) |
| Orca preamble advertises helper start (`SUB-DISPATCH`) while WC-13 forbids it | Happens when depth cap allows (installed default: Section 9.3) | Opposite instructions; which one a worker obeys is [UNVERIFIED] |
| `finished_unverified` and the 60 s liveness window | Defined in Orca code, never produced: the only production attempt-fact writer records `worker_report` | Do not rely on it (`gap-P3-G01.md` S3, C4) |
| `unverifiable` liveness | Absence of positive evidence, never proof of death; ten reasons (`missing_status`, `stale_status` after 30 min of no agent-hook activity, `restored_unconfirmed`, `future_status`, `unsupervised_settled`, `host_unavailable`, `capability_unsupported`, `home_budget_exhausted`, `host_indeterminate`, `peer_changed`) | Four of them only with `--include-remote`; `host_unavailable` also for a local chat-session worker (`gap-P3-G01.md` S5a) |
| Heartbeats turned off in Orca | The preamble holds no heartbeat command; a healthy worker sends none | The contract does not tell the coordinator to check the setting (`gap-P3-G01.md` S9) |
| Late `worker_done` while a sibling supervised Dispatch of the same Task is active | Rejected as `inactive_dispatch` | Not stated in the contract (`gap-P3-G01.md` S7) |
| WC-6 forbids `--retry-of` | `circuit_broken` is set when a Dispatch's inherited-plus-new failure count reaches 3 (`dispatch-completion.ts:166-167`); on `worker-start` the count is inherited only along a `retryOf` chain (`worker-dispatch-start.ts:94,126-127`) and the refusal to retry such a Dispatch exists only on a `retryOf` start (`:95-104`), so a coordinator that follows WC-6 (fresh Task, fresh start) reaches neither through `worker-start`. The coordinator's own three-failed-starts comparison is the only guard on repeated starts (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:75`; ORCA-CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12; Section 5.2 item 1) | Policy choice that overrides Orca's guide (`research/02` section 2) |
| `/sc:implement` as a worker | Contract text describes a producer; none exists | Deferred to P4 by a cited scope decision (owner question C7) |
| Scanner ceilings of the worktree gate | `bash -c`, `sh -c`, `eval`, `env -S`, `ssh host '...'`, `xargs orca`, `find -exec orca`, heredoc piped to a shell, quoted `--agent` value, command word assembled from pieces, global options with a separate value before `worktree`, unlisted wrapper options, non-Bash tools such as Monitor, and the identity hook's rewritten input of a governed session if the gate were shown it (order unobserved) | Header `:39-61`; `echo "orca worktree create"` is allowed. Nested-backtick `\` form was not probed. The rewrite-order ceiling is stated in 5.3 ([UNVERIFIED]; only the original-command case is tested, `test_require_orca_worktree_agent_governed.py:31-56`); documented ceiling, not fixed |
| Accepted text-level leaks of the guard | `G=gi; ${G}t push origin main`, `GH=gh; $GH pr merge 5`, `$'\x67it' push origin main`, `/usr/bin/gi[t] push origin main`, `bash ./push.sh` pass at every level | Pinned by `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:3602-3622` (design T-GAP-1, R1, owner decision O-7); the real fix is named as the L2 layer |
| Tool coverage of the guard | `Monitor` is classified but the identity hook does not adapt it (`launch-job-identity.sh:426-428` per research 03); `SendMessage`, `Workflow`, `RemoteTrigger` and remote-isolation `Agent`/`Task` are ESCAPE | Local or background `Agent`/`Task` prompts are never scanned |
| Revision skew | HEAD, working tree, ORIGIN/MASTER and the host-installed set differ (L2 layer, FR-025 identity fields, ownership wording of the gate's message) | A statement about the L2 layer is true of ORIGIN/MASTER and the installed guard and identity hook on the research host, not of this CHECKOUT (`research/06` F6) |
| Twelve live observations | OB-H1 items 1-9, OB-H2, OB-H3, OB-CTX are skipped tests: "needs a real governed session and has not been run" | Hook order and subagent identity are unobserved, and so is every behavior of the launch-level guard in a live session (`tests/hooks/test_launch_level_observations.py:79-84`). Rewrite behavior was observed in two recorded runs (one governed, one ordinary session), for a main-session Bash call under the identity adapter only (HISTORICAL recorded runs, Section 5.3 evidence table); that record lists `/clear`, resume, auto mode, concurrent-hook rewrite composition, persistent cwd, background scheduling and nested-subagent payloads as unobserved (`identity-integration-evidence.md:19`) |

### 10.3 Graceful Degradation

**What the reference may and may not say about "fail closed".** Governed sessions fail closed only on the guard script's own failure paths. The qualifier that must travel with the phrase: *except when the hook is stopped at its timeout, identity is lost, or the session is not governed* (`gap-P3-G06.md` sections 5-6, correcting `research/03` C.3).

| Failure | Impact | Degraded Experience |
|---|---|---|
| Launch-level guard stopped at its 5 s timeout (both events) | The design records "call goes ahead (V8, R2)" as a failure-table row and residual risk (`docs/designs/sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:216,440`); V8 is a harness fact the design relied on, not observed in this repository. DOCUMENTED agreement: a timed-out `PreToolUse` command hook does not block unless `onFailure: "block"` (v2.1.295 or later) is set, "don't count on a stalled hook to act as a gate" (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, `web-01` Q1). Probe (private temp dir, fixed input, single runs): `kill -TERM` while reading stdin and mid-run of a governed 64 KB command both ended exit 143 with empty stderr; `kill -KILL` exit 137; a completed governed block gave exit 2 (`gap-P3-G06.md` section 3). The script does not turn a signal into a deny | A governed call that overruns the budget may proceed unguarded. The signal the host uses, whether the user sees a message, and the behavior of the installed Claude Code are [UNVERIFIED]. Observed reach (Section 11.1a; HISTORICAL records, not re-run): a governed 64 KB command takes 0.5 to 0.9 s at idle (`tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:1005`, CHECKOUT), and the slowest of 100 runs under 56 busy loops (2x CPU oversubscription, "not a CI condition") took 5536.1 ms, above the 5 s timeout (`limit-selection.md:11`). Recorded figures for a governed small command (T-SPD-4, reported and not asserted; the rows are in Section 11.1a): medians of about 14 ms on a 28-CPU host at load average 2.54 (`speed-results.md:7,19-20`, 2026-11-07), and, in the full suite under `-n auto` with 28 workers on 28 CPUs, a `git status` median of 40.4 ms and a p95 of 67.3 ms, above the reported p95 target of 60 ms (`d3-suite-raw.log:3943`, 2026-11-08). Whether a normal governed command can reach the 5 s timeout on a heavily loaded host is not established [UNVERIFIED]; that it cannot is an inference, not an observation |
| Worktree gate stopped at its 3 s timeout | Same documented behavior; no timing test exists for the gate (`gap-P3-G06.md` R3) | A bare create may proceed; the gate's budget is the shortest of the orchestration guards |
| Identity loss: no readable top-level `session_id` | Declared fail-open for a tool call: the guard treats the call as ungoverned even if the store holds a bound session (`launch-level-guard.sh:390-394`; `test_identity_loss_allows_silently`, `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:1606-1611` per G06). On UserPromptSubmit it is fail-open only for a prompt with no launch marker; a prompt that carries a launch marker and has no valid `session_id` is denied with `LL-NOBIND` (`:366-370`, and `:233-236` for an envelope `jq` cannot parse; CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12) | A governed session behaves as ungoverned for that tool call; a marked first prompt with no valid `session_id` is blocked, not allowed |
| Identity hook, raw event over 1 MiB | The hook reads at most 1,048,576 bytes plus one and returns 0 with no output above that, so a governed Bash call proceeds unwrapped; the 65,536-byte refusal applies only below it (`launch-job-identity.sh:415-419`, `:446-447`, CHECKOUT, re-read 2026-11-12). The launch-level guard is a separate registration (`hooks.json:115-124`) and is not changed by this path; how it treats an event of that size was not traced | Only the identity wrapping is skipped for that call; the identity hook is fail-open here, unlike its refusal of an envelope of 65,537 to 1,048,576 bytes |
| Broken JSON or no `jq` | Governed (bound id visible in the text) or marker prompt: exit 2; otherwise allow. Any bound id anywhere in the broken text counts, even a nested one (`:208-238`, `:212-216` per research 03; probes G47-G49 and the no-jq PATH runs) | Fail closed for governed, open for ungoverned |
| Guard or gate not registered, or hooks disabled | No control at all. The claim preflight refuses a governed launch if the guard or identity registration is missing or `disableAllHooks` is true; it does not check the worktree gate's registration | Documented routes that skip hooks (`--bare`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SIMPLE`, `--safe-mode`, `allowManagedHooksOnly`) are outside the preflight; not observed |
| Installed hook older than the checkout | Without `--force` an older installed script or matcher is kept silently; `orca_worktree.py:925-1015` tells the operator to run `superclaude install --force` for exactly that case (`stale` strings `:932-933` per research 03) | Host mix on the research host: gate equals HEAD/ORIGIN/MASTER, guard and identity equal ORIGIN/MASTER |
| L2 pre-push layer absent | Claim warns `W-L2-ABSENT` and launches; limits are L1 only | The guard header calls L2 "the real fix" for text-level leaks (`launch-level-guard.sh:710-711` per research 03) |
| Orca not installed, not running or unreachable | Claim stops with `E-ORCA-REQUIRED` before any GitHub write; install skips its Orca step; the watcher stops with `orca_not_found` | Everything not needing Orca (hooks, installer, doctor) still works |
| Orca list unusable or create reply odd | No create on an unusable list; zero new rows after a create is `W-WORKTREE-CREATE-FAILED` and several new rows is `W-WORKTREE-AMBIGUOUS` (10.1c); record reuse is flagged unverified | A duplicate worktree is avoided at the cost of a warning |
| Shim check inconclusive | Advisory warning only; agent keeps running; `launch` entry still written so a re-run returns `already_launched` | Person closes the agent by hand if the remedy applies |
| First-tab close refused | `W-FIRST-TAB-LEFT`, cosmetic | One empty tab to close by hand |
| ccsession naming fails (name list directory not writable) | `--auto-name` prints nothing; the launch is untagged; the `[ccsession]` screen text can still match if read in time | Possible false `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION` |
| Heartbeats off or late | No stall signal from Orca; the coordinator treats absence as absence and never stops, retries or releases on it (WC-10) | Slower detection; read-only probes, a typed nudge and a direct question only |
| `ask` unanswered for 10 minutes | The worker's `ask --timeout-ms 600000` times out and resumes by message ID (preamble) | Worker waits again; no duplicate question |
| Worker gives no contract | `--outcome failed`; coordinator treats the Task as failed (WC-5) | New Task per WC-6 |
| Worker `blocked` | Contract carries `blocked_question`; brief policy decides report or ask once | At most one ask per Task (WC-3) |
| Shared installer budget exhausted | Orca step and plugin step report "(time limit)" or warnings; install exit code unaffected | Under 2 s left per plugin means the plugin is skipped (`install_user_setup.py:412-415` per G03) |

## 11. Performance Characteristics

> **Important:** This section states limits only: registered timeouts, speed pins in tests, documented cadences, and the few figures a file in the repository records from a run. No figure here was measured for this reference, and none is invented. The research found no performance target document for the system as a whole (an absence the research did not search the whole repository for [UNVERIFIED]; `research-notes.md:41` only says not to invent figures). The launch-level hook design does carry a speed budget for the guard (`docs/designs/sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md:219-228`, NORMATIVE design text, re-read 2026-11-12): acceptance targets, not observed results, of which only some are asserted (the pins are in 11.1b). The worktree gate has no timing test (`gap-P3-G06.md` R3), and the claim script, the watcher and Orca calls have no recorded timing (11.1a). Anything not listed is unmeasured.

### 11.1 Performance Profile

| Same, full suite under `-n auto` (28 workers on 28 CPUs) | 998.6 ms maximum | same | CI-shaped 1x load |

Targets "median 20 ms, p95 50 ms" are printed and reported, not asserted, because they are flaky on a loaded host (`tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:996-997`, re-read) | T-SPD-1 and T-SPD-2 (empty store, 1,050-file store) | A recorded 2026-11-07 run exists at `.dev/tasks/to-do/TASK-WF-implementTheLaun-20261107-092804/artifacts/speed-results.md`; its ungoverned-path figures are not restated here, and it predates the 3.5 s limit; its governed small-command rows are in the next row |

| Any Orca RPC, `worker-start` readiness, mailbox latency, hook order effects | **Not measured** | none | Live Orca was out of scope for every research file |

### 11.2 Performance-Critical Code

| Area | Optimization | Why It Matters | Location |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ungoverned sessions in the guard | Empty-store fast exit: if `$LL_STORE/sessions` is missing and the input holds no `[sc-launch:`, exit 0 before parsing | Every prompt and every guarded tool call of every ungoverned session pays this hook; the hook is registered on a wide tool matcher | `launch-level-guard.sh:168-173` (re-read) |
| Guard parse | One `jq` pass extracts event, tool, skill, path, isolation and the command; no `${var//...}` substitution is run over command text; globbing off (`set -f`) | Keeps the governed path near the sed/tr cost of normalisation | `launch-level-guard.sh:14-15,174-199` (re-read), `:420` per research 03 |
| Oversize commands | BIG class denies a command over 65,536 bytes before normalisation, so the 1 MB case is the cheapest of the four (median 436.0 ms in the 2026-11-07 run per G06) | Bounds worst-case cost of a hostile or accidental large input | `launch-level-guard.sh:422-423` per research 03 |
| Identity hook for an unbound state | Exit 0 before needing `uv` when `HOME` is unset or `~/.claude/state/launch-level` is absent; with no `uv`, `jq` alone decides whether to exit 0 or 2 | `uv run` start-up is the expensive step; ungoverned sessions never reach it | `launch-job-identity.sh:20-54` (re-read) |
| Worktree gate | One `jq` and one `LC_ALL=C awk` over the command; exits at `[ "$WORD" = "orca" ] \|\| exit 0` before building the message | Registered with the shortest timeout (3 s) | `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:343-358` (re-read) |
| Claim shim check | Title lookup uses the 15 s read timeout, not the 120 s default (pinned by `test_la5_title_lookup_uses_the_short_terminal_timeout`, `tests/skills/test_issue_orca_worktree.py:1591-1610`); first hit ends the check; no pause after the last try | Five stalled `terminal show` calls would otherwise cost 5 x 120 s | `orca_worktree.py:1129-1161` (re-read) |
| Claim helper subprocesses | No shell, captured output, a timeout on every call, `list --limit 500`; a list that cannot be trusted never triggers a create | Bounded wait per Orca call | `orca_worktree.py:124-147,296-332` (re-read) |
| Installer | One shared 30 s deadline, minimum request timeout 1.0 s | A 60 s workspace limit (Coder) must not be exceeded by the Orca and plugin steps | `install_user_setup.py:36-40,224-227` per G03 A.3 |
| Orca worker list | Paged (page max 100), pinned snapshots with cursors | Large Runs | `worker-list-method.ts:43-160` (ORCA-CHECKOUT, per G01 S5) |

### 11.3 Asset Management

N/A. The system holds no significant assets (images, videos, fonts, large data files). Its files are small state records: launch-level store entries, `worktree.json`, ccsession name lists, tab records and title marks (9.1). Sections 6 (State Management) and 7 (Component Inventory) of the template are also N/A for this system (no frontend); they are marked in Sections 6 and 7.
