## 12. Conventions & Patterns

The rules of the road for changing this subsystem. Most of them are conventions of text read by LLM sessions and of small shell hooks; very few are enforced by code (section 14.1 states which).

### 12.1 Code Conventions

| Convention | Description | Example |
|---|---|---|
| Hook decision contract | Allow is exit 0. Block is one message on stderr and exit 2. A hook never exits 1. DOCUMENTED: exit 2 blocks and stderr is the reason; any other code with plain-text, empty or invalid-JSON stdout is a non-blocking error, so exit 1 would let the call go ahead (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks, "Exit code output"; web-01 Q2). The "never exit 1" rule is the repository's own convention, not a documented requirement. [CODE-VERIFIED] | `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:10-11`; `launch-level-guard.sh:5` |
| Fail-open where the hook cannot judge | The worktree gate allows on missing `jq` or `awk`, bad JSON, a non-Bash tool, an empty command and any scanner error; it blocks only when the scanner positively reports a bare create. [CODE-VERIFIED] | `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:12-15`, `:65-72`; tests `FAIL_OPEN` `tests/hooks/test_require_orca_worktree_agent.py:144` |
| Fail-closed only for governed sessions | The launch-level guard denies (exit 2) on its own internal failure when the session is bound, and allows when it is not. This covers the script's own failure paths only (qualifier: Section 10.3). [CODE-VERIFIED] | `launch-level-guard.sh:147-163` (EXIT trap `ll_on_exit`, installed at `:163`) |
| No `onFailure` on registrations | Neither guard registration sets `onFailure`; a test pins its absence in both `hooks.json` trees. DOCUMENTED: `onFailure: "block"` (Claude Code v2.1.295 or later) is the way to make a failure or timeout block (web-01 Q1); the design rejects it because it would block ungoverned sessions whenever a hook breaks (`merged-requirements.md:98,694,701`, via gap-P3-G06 section 2). [CODE-VERIFIED] | `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard_registration.py:63-65` |
| Hooks are flat installed files with a managed registration | Scripts live in `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/`, are copied flat to `~/.claude/hooks/`, are listed in `_FRESHNESS_SCRIPTS` and are registered by command path `~/.claude/hooks/<name>.sh`. `make verify-sync` requires every script to be in the list. [CODE-VERIFIED] | `install_hooks.py:42-94` (entries `:88,90,93`, comment `:62-64`); `hooks.json:105-134` |
| Registration ownership is by command identity | The installer merges registrations by exact command, never by matcher alone; without `--force` an installed registration is kept as is; a missing one is inserted before the first registration owned by a later source entry. [CODE-VERIFIED] | `install_hooks.py:402-419`, `:457-471` |
| Command `## Unattended mode` shape | A converted command carries one section that points to `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md`, applies only to a standalone `--unattended` token, fixes exactly one report directory, states its `Contract.` bullet (`report_path`, `artifacts`, status mapping), refuses person-mode flags, lists its gates, nested calls and outward actions. The section names no `src/superclaude/` path, no Orca command (case-insensitive `\borca(-ide)?\s+(orchestration\|worktree\|terminal\|agent)\b`, `test_worker_contract.py:843-845`), and `nested/` only for its listed nested commands. [CODE-VERIFIED] | `src/superclaude/commands/graph.md:59-68`; checker `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:843-845`, `:869-886` |
| Flag parse rule | `--unattended` counts only as a standalone argument token outside any quoted argument. No code parses it; each command's text states it and the worker LLM applies it. [CODE-VERIFIED as text; enforcement is by the LLM] | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:13`; `graph.md:63` |
| One report directory per Task | The brief passes exactly one of the command's own directory-valued output flag or `--report-dir`; neither or both is refused before any work. The contract path is `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml`. [CODE-VERIFIED as text] | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:15`, `:32`, `:38` |
| Worker contract fields | Top-level keys `worker_contract_version` (`"1"`), `worker_status` (`succeeded`, `partial`, `failed`, `blocked`), `command`, `report_path`, `artifacts`, `blocked_question`, `reason`. `artifacts` leaves out git-ignored paths and paths outside the repository. [CODE-VERIFIED as text] | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:32-39` |
| Rule IDs | Rules are named WC-2 to WC-15 (no WC-1; WC-6 sits in section 4) and decisions D1 to D5. A reference to "WC-1" would dangle. Decisions D1-D5 are HISTORICAL design records (`.dev/orca-command-refactor/decisions/APPROVED.md`), not part of the distributed docs. | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:31-65`, `:71`; research/01 B.4, D.1; research/06 U3 |
| Brief fixed lines | The Guard line and the Chain line are copied word for word into every brief, the Guard line before the first `Command:` line. Pinned as text. | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:288-289`; `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:376-380` (Guard line), `:368-373` (Chain line, `brief-chain-line`), `:780-788` (guard before the first `Command:` line); the checks read the contract template, not any brief |
| Retry is a new Task | A coordinator never uses `worker-start --retry-of`; every retry is a new Task with its own report directory. This deliberately overrides Orca's own guide advice. [CODE-VERIFIED as text] | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:71-79`; research/02 section 2; gap-P3-G01 S5a |
| Contract-text tests are phrase pins | Each rule maps to phrases that must appear in the contract (`RULE_PHRASES`); mutation tests remove a phrase and expect the check to fail. A pin proves the text exists, not that a session follows it. [CODE-VERIFIED] | `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:25`; positive check `test_contract_states_rule` `:907-909`; mutation test `test_rule_check_fails_when_rule_removed` `:1044-1051` |
| Hook tests run the real script | Hook tests feed fixed JSON on stdin to the real script with `HOME` in `tmp_path`; the block message is asserted byte for byte. | `tests/hooks/test_require_orca_worktree_agent.py:31-48`, tables `:57`, `:101`, `:144` |
| Installer functions take `home` | Installer helpers take a `home` argument; tests fake `shutil.which` and `subprocess.run` and never run a real Orca. [CODE-VERIFIED via research gap-P3-G03 G.3; test lines not re-read] | `install_user_setup.py:230`; `tests/cli/test_install_user_setup.py:289,358` (the count 3 of `--skill`) |
| Orca binary names | The installer probes `orca-ide` then `orca`; each candidate must answer `skills list --json` with a topic named `orca-cli`. The issue watcher imports the same tuple. [CODE-VERIFIED] | `install_user_setup.py:35`, `:198-221` (not re-read in full); `issue_watch/launch.py:22` |
| Repository rules that bind a change | Source of truth is `src/superclaude/`; `.claude/` mirrors are never staged; `.dev/tasks/` is always staged; PRs only through `/sc:pr-submit --monitor 3` against `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`; no installed primitive names a `src/superclaude/` path. [NORMATIVE; project `CLAUDE.md`] | project `CLAUDE.md` sections "Never Stage or Commit `.claude/` Contents", "PR Target = Fork", "Never Hardcode `src/superclaude/` Paths" |
| Evidence labelling | Reference text states the evidence class of each non-trivial claim and never presents a contradicted or unverified claim as current. | `BUILD-REQUEST.md:49-54` |

### 12.2 Architectural Patterns

| Pattern | Where Used | Description |
|---|---|---|
| Normative contract with a thin enforcement layer | `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`, command sections, three hooks | Authority is stated as text read by LLM sessions (WC-2 to WC-15). The only executable IronClaude-side controls are the lexical `--agent` check and, inside bound `/sc:issue --claim` sessions, the launch-level guard. Orca adds machine-side controls on its side (worker-start scope, depth cap, narrow run-binding refusal). [CODE-VERIFIED] (research/01 C, C.1; research/03 F) |
| Two roles plus the person | contract sections 3 and 4 | A person-opened coordinator and a worker session identified only by a preamble carrying a Task ID and a Dispatch ID. Subagents are not roles. Worker fan-out is barred at any depth setting (WC-13, decision D5). Nothing in code reads a role. [CODE-VERIFIED as text] |
| Preamble as the authority for verb syntax | contract and Orca | The contract never copies verb syntax or heartbeat cadence; they come from the dispatch preamble built by `buildDispatchPreamble` (`IronOrca/src/main/runtime/orchestration/preamble.ts:52-152`; heartbeat default 5 minutes, range 1-60, may be off). Text identical in INSTALLED-FILES (gap-P3-G01 S1, S2, S8). `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:7` |
| Pull-based follow-ups | WC-12 | A worker pulls coordinator messages with `check` at checkpoints and acknowledges with `ack <message_id> applied\|queued\|refused`; nothing is pushed into a turn. |
| Absence is not death | WC-10, ladder L0-L5 | A late heartbeat or `unverifiable` verdict authorizes only read-only probes, a nudge and a question (L1-L3). Restart (L4) needs a positive stall proof or the person's stop. Orca gives no stall verdict; its `unverifiable` means no fresh positive evidence (gap-P3-G01 S5, S5a, S9). |
| File-based result channel with LLM writer and LLM reader | every converted command | A command's skill writes `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml`; the worker maps `worker_status` to `worker_done --outcome`; Orca carries `--report-path` without checking the file; the coordinator opens it. No executable reads the worker fields. Four on-disk layouts exist (gap-P3-G04 section 3.3). [CODE-VERIFIED negative, two searches] |
| Marker, pending record and single-use nonce binding | `orca_worktree.py launch` -> `launch-level-guard.sh` | The claim script writes `pending/<nonce>` and launches with `[sc-launch:v1:<nonce>]`; the guard consumes the nonce once (`mv`) and publishes the session record by hard link, so a binding cannot be replaced. A forged leading marker binds at the strictest level. [CODE-VERIFIED] (research/04 F1, research/03 C.2) |
| Text-level classification with pinned accepted leaks | `launch-level-guard.sh` | Commands are classified on normalised text, not parsed shell; variables, built names and `bash ./script` leak by design (design residual risk R1, owner decision O-7; `tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:3602-3622`). [CODE-VERIFIED] |
| Advisory check after launch | shim check in `orca_worktree.py` | `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION` never stops, closes or blocks the launched agent; status stays `launched`. [CODE-VERIFIED] (research/04 F1; gap-P3-G05 F4.3) |
| Non-fatal Orca step in the installer | `install_user_setup.py` | IronClaude only requests `orca skills install`; Orca produces and owns the three guides. Every path returns `True`, so Orca absence, failure or timeout never fails `install` (a malformed probe reply can still raise, 14.1 L32). [CODE-VERIFIED] (gap-P3-G03 A.2, A.3) |
| Source of truth plus three separate copy flows | `src/superclaude/` -> `.claude/`, `~/.claude/`, `plugins/` | Edit `src/`. (1) `make sync-dev` writes `.claude/{skills,agents,commands/sc,hooks,templates}` only (`Makefile:108-163`); `make verify-sync` checks that tree. (2) `superclaude install` writes `~/.claude/`. (3) The `plugins/superclaude/hooks/scripts/` mirror is a hand-maintained tracked copy: copy the edited script byte for byte (mode 0755); which mirrors have a byte-equality test and the rule for a new mirrored script: Section 13.2. No Makefile target, script or pre-commit hook writes `plugins/`. [CODE-VERIFIED] |
| Retired scheduler fenced in three layers | Orca (ORCA-CHECKOUT, INSTALLED-FILES textually) | The legacy automatic Task scheduler is retired by CLI handlers, an RPC retired-method set and an admission fence; Orca settles a Task on a valid `worker_done` and, after a `succeeded` outcome only, promotes dependents to `ready` but starts nothing. Not an IronClaude component. (gap-P3-G01 S7) |

### 12.3 Anti-Patterns (Things to Avoid)

| Anti-Pattern | Why It's Wrong | Do This Instead |
|---|---|---|
| Recognising the Orca CLI by one literal name (`b == "orca"`, case `orca)`) | Both scripts miss the other names the product uses (statement and reasons it is not exotic: Section 8.2). For the launch-level guard this is the recorded DEFECT (14.2 TD1); for the worktree gate it is a documented ceiling. [CODE-VERIFIED, probes re-run 2026-11-12, 15.2 step 5] | Match the set of names the product uses and state the remaining limit (obfuscated or built names) in the header and the design; follow the remediation plan. |
| Treating the `hooks.json` array order as an execution order | DOCUMENTED: matching hooks run in parallel, completion order is non-deterministic, and every matching hook runs to completion (https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks; hooks guide "Limitations"). | Make each hook independent; do not rely on one hook running first or on a sibling's `deny` stopping another. |
| Two hooks rewriting the same call's input | DOCUMENTED: rewrites are neither merged nor ordered (hooks guide, "Limitations"). Only `launch-job-identity.sh` rewrites Bash input here. | Keep one rewriting hook per tool; a new Bash hook returns decisions only. |
| Writing "fail-closed for governed sessions" without a qualifier | The phrase hides three cases that allow: a hook stopped at its timeout, identity loss on a tool call and an ungoverned session. The qualifier and its evidence are stated once, in Section 10.3 (L21 for the timeout hole). | Use the qualified wording of Section 10.3. |
| Blocking with exit 1 | DOCUMENTED: a non-blocking error unless stdout is schema-valid JSON that decides. | Block with exit 2 and a stderr message. |
| Quoting the ownership wording as installed or merged | The new wording is an uncommitted edit set; HEAD, `origin/master` and the research host's installed copies carry the old "coordinator under another orchestrator" text (research/06 F5, F6). | Say "uncommitted at the time of writing" and re-read `git status` first. |
| Treating the Orca checkout as the installed or running build | The depth default differs between `dc57b3d6e` and the installed files (Section 9.3); `worktree ps --agent-wait` exists only in the installed files; the running setting is UNVERIFIED. | Report both with provenance; never write "Orca refuses a worker's helper start" without scoping it. |
| Reading heartbeat age or `unverifiable` as an Orca stall verdict | Orca derives no verdict from `last_heartbeat_at` on the live surface; `unverifiable` means no fresh positive evidence; `finished_unverified` has no production writer (gap-P3-G01 S3, S4). | Apply the contract's S-table and ladder as IronClaude policy over raw evidence. |
| Using `worker-start --retry-of` in the coordinator flow | Orca's circuit-breaker refusal runs only on a `--retry-of` start, so WC-6's own flow is never guarded by it; only the coordinator's three-failed-starts comparison guards (research/02 section 2). | Create a new Task per retry (WC-6) and make the comparison. |
| Putting a `src/superclaude/` path, an Orca command name or a hand-written nested directory in an `## Unattended mode` section | The section checker fails on each (`tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:869-886`); installed primitives run where `src/` does not exist. | Name installed paths (`~/.claude/...`) and use the contract's wording. |
| Editing `.claude/` mirrors or using `git add -f` on them | `.claude/` is gitignored sync output; doubling diffs breaks `make verify-sync`. | Edit `src/superclaude/`, `make sync-dev`, stage the `src/` side only. |
| Relabelling a shipped-but-unwired capability as a gap, future phase or question | Rule C8 (`BUILD-REQUEST.md:54`) needs a cited scope decision. | Record a DEFECT and link a remediation task (14.2). |
| Calling the sub-orchestrator layer shipped, or "orchestrator" a role | No command, skill, flag or contract role exists; `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` has no orchestrator role; #444 is OPEN. | Say "deferred"; keep the future-role question open. |
| Citing a line number from research without re-reading it | Research lines age (the contract is 327 lines, not 273; the commits on `origin/master` that HEAD lacks changed between 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12 as the local ref moved; history in the Evidence Classes legend under Document Information). | Re-read the source line immediately before citing (15.2). |

## 13. Extension Guide

How to extend the subsystem. The guide records what the source and tests require today; it does not add behavior. Where a step depends on an unfinished plan (the name-recognition remediation), the row says so.

### 13.1 Common Extension Tasks

| Task | Steps | Files to Touch | Pitfalls |
|---|---|---|---|
| Convert a command to accept `--unattended` | 1 Add the `## Unattended mode` section (skeleton 13.1.1 a). 2 Make the command's skill write `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` on every outcome. 3 Add an `UNATTENDED_EXPECT` entry. 4 Put the name in a phase tuple and update the set checks. 5 Classify any git or `gh` write verbs the new text contains. 6 `make sync-dev`; then `superclaude install --force` or `update` to refresh installed copies (both overwrite the shipped files they copy with no backup, apart from the exceptions listed in Section 5.6 item 1). 7 Decide whether the brief flag table needs a row. | `src/superclaude/commands/<name>.md`; the command's `src/superclaude/skills/<protocol>/SKILL.md`; `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` (`UNATTENDED_EXPECT` `:438`, `P1_COMMANDS` `:395`, `P2_COMMANDS` `:416`, `_check_p1_set` `:1355`, `_check_p2_set` `:1556`, `WRITE_VERB_CLASSIFICATION` `:2016`); brief table `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:309-327` | Existing set checks fail for a 32nd command: `_check_p1_set` requires `UNATTENDED_EXPECT` to sit inside P1 plus P2 (`:1360`), and `_check_p2_set` requires exactly 15 P2 names, set equality with P1 plus P2 and `len(expect) == 31` (`:1556-1566`); a new phase tuple and checks are needed [CODE-VERIFIED]. `UNATTENDED_COMMANDS` is derived (`:746`); the developer guide's (`docs/developer-guide/SuperClaude-Developer-Guide-Commands-Skills-Agents.md`) "add to `UNATTENDED_COMMANDS`" is stale (research/06 F3 items 1-3). A command in `NEVER_AS_WORKER` (`:382-391`) or `NOT_CONVERTED_IN_P1` (`:1335`) must not get a section. `/sc:implement` has no producer by scope decision (P4). The section checker rejects `src/superclaude/` paths and Orca command names (`:869-886`). The brief table is only partly pinned: `_check_p2_brief` (`tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py:947-955`, run by `test_p2_brief_table_rows_and_bullet` `:972`) asserts a table row for each of five P2 commands (auggie-review, cli-eval, cli-portify, review-translation, spec-panel; rows 312, 315, 316, 323 and 324 of `WORKER-CONTRACT.md:309-327`), a single "From P2" bullet naming every P2 command (`:304`) and the absence of "until P2", and removing a row or dropping a command from the bullet fails a test (`:986-1001`). The other twelve rows, the slot rules and the flag cells were found unpinned by research/02 11.4, which marked that negative [UNVERIFIED] (searched, not proven), so drift there may fail no test. |

Matching hooks run in parallel (DOCUMENTED), so array position is not an order. No `onFailure` key. Without `--force` a user's older registration with an outdated matcher is not updated (`install_hooks.py:417-419`). Hook scripts must not name `src/superclaude/` paths. |

Neither configures Orca's Claude command (manual step). Line numbers other than `install_ccsession.py:41` come from gap-P3-G03 and were not re-read. |

No TIMING test exists for the gate (gap-P3-G06 R3). A plain `superclaude install` keeps an existing registration owned by the same command and so keeps its old timeout (`install_hooks.py:417-419`; only `--force` replaces it in place, `:421-455`), so a timeout change reaches an installed host only after step 4. A hook stopped at its timeout lets the call go ahead (L21); the 3.5 s asserted limit is below the 5 s timeout by design, and the design's 1 s figure is only reported. |

#### 13.1.1 Skeletons (shapes read from source; placeholders in angle brackets)

**a. `## Unattended mode` section** (shape of `src/superclaude/commands/graph.md:59-68`; each bullet is that file's own bullet name):

```markdown
## Unattended mode

The shared rules are in `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md`; read it first. This section lists only what `/sc:<name>` resolves itself.

- **Switch.** It applies only when `--unattended` is a standalone argument token outside any quoted argument; otherwise nothing here applies and no worker contract is written.
- **Report directory.** Exactly one of <the command's own directory flag> or `--report-dir <dir>` is required; both, or neither, is refused before any work. <path rules>
- **Contract.** <where `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` is written; `report_path`; `artifacts` (git-ignored paths left out); mapping of the command's own status to succeeded / partial / failed / blocked>
- **Person-mode flags.** <flags refused: `worker_status: failed`, the flag in `reason`>
- **Gates.** <each gate: default, caller flag, or blocked with `blocked_question`>. Nested invocations: <none | `/sc:<x>` with `<report-dir>/nested/<x>-<n>/`>.
- **Outward actions.** <what stays off: push, commit, stage, PR>
```

Its registration entry in `tests/skills/test_worker_contract.py` (shape of `:438-443`): `"<name>": {"flags": ["<whole-token flags the section must contain>"], "phrases": ["<text the section must hold>"], "nested": []}`. The checker additionally requires the three strings `return-contract.yaml`, `~/.claude/WORKER-CONTRACT.md` and `--report-dir` (`:869-875`), and `nested/<x>-` exactly for each name in `nested` (`:880-884`).

**b. Hook** (registration shape of `hooks.json:105-114`; list shape of `install_hooks.py:42`; contract of `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:10-15`):

```json
{ "matcher": "Bash", "hooks": [ { "type": "command", "command": "~/.claude/hooks/<name>.sh", "timeout": 3 } ] }
```
```text
install_hooks.py, list _FRESHNESS_SCRIPTS:  "<name>.sh",
script header:  allow -> exit 0; block -> one stderr message + exit 2; never exit 1; fail open when it cannot judge
```

**c. Name recognition** (ILLUSTRATIVE ONLY; not the remediation, not implemented; the plan leaves "explicit list or pattern" to Task 1): the guard's arm is the exact-match case `orca)` at `launch-level-guard.sh:458`, the gate's test is `b == "orca" || b ~ /^\$/` at `require-orca-worktree-agent.sh:145`. An explicit-list form would change those two predicates to accept the same set of names in both files; the allow-list at `launch-level-guard.sh:475-477` (read-only verbs such as `terminal list|show|read`) must still pass under every recognised name.

## 14. Known Limitations & Technical Debt

Source of every row: the register `gaps-and-questions.md` (A3 remaining gaps, B accepted evidence limits, C owner questions, D defect register) and the research and gap files it points to; this section does not restate the register, it gives each limitation one row with its evidence class, tag and the reference sentence to use. Rows keep the template columns; the evidence class and tag sit in the first cell and the sentence in the second. Limitation IDs (L01-L42, sub-tables A to E) are this section's own and are used for cross-reference.

## 16. Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Supervised orchestration | An Orca coordinator session, opened by a person, runs IronClaude `/sc:` commands as workers through `orca orchestration` Tasks and `worker-start`, under the rules of `WORKER-CONTRACT.md`. "Supervised" has no definition of its own in the contract; its operational meaning is dispatch through `worker-start` with a preamble (research/01 B.1). |
| Coordinator | The session that plans, creates Tasks, starts workers, reads reports and performs every outward action; "an Orca coordinator session the person opened" (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:69`). Never a session Orca started as a worker (`HOW-WE-WORK.md:400`). Nothing in code detects the role. |
| Worker run / worker session | A top-level session that received an Orca preamble carrying a Task ID and a Dispatch ID; only it sends lifecycle messages (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:11`). Identified by prompt text, not by code. |
| Ordinary session (contract sense) | Any session that is not a worker run, the person-opened coordinator included: the contract's complement of "worker run" (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:11`). Not the guard's notion; see "Ungoverned session". |
| Non-worker session | Shorthand in this reference for a session that is not a worker run, for example the agents the claim script and the issue watcher launch. |
| Governed session | A session bound by the launch-level guard through the claim script's launch marker; every other session is ungoverned. A different identity system from "worker run" (research/01 B.1). |
| Ungoverned session (guard sense) | A session with no launch-marker binding in the guard's store; the guard allows all its calls, and also allows a tool call whose `session_id` is missing (a prompt that carries a launch marker and has no valid `session_id` is denied `LL-NOBIND`; Section 5.3, Failure behavior). The person-opened coordinator, a supervised worker and a watcher-started session are ungoverned. |
| Person-opened | The coordinator eligibility rule: a procedural fact, not a runtime property. |
| Eligible session | Undefined in the distributed guidance; only the negative reading (a supervised worker is not eligible) is stated (L16). |
| Orchestrator | Used once in the ownership paragraph (`core/CLAUDE.md:58`); no orchestrator role exists in the contract or code (L16). |
| Sub-orchestrator / subordinate mode | A proposed layer between the coordinator and workers. Deferred, not implemented, not shipped; #444 is OPEN. Not the same as Orca's federated own-Run preamble text (research/05 5.3). |
| Subagent | A command's Agent-tool fan-out inside a session. Not a role; may not send lifecycle messages or call Orca (WC-7). Hook payloads inside a subagent carry `agent_id` and `agent_type` (DOCUMENTED). |
| Task, Dispatch, Run | Orca records: a Task is a unit of work; a Dispatch is one attempt to run a Task by a worker; a Run groups Tasks under a coordinator. A retry is a new Task under WC-6. |
| Preamble | The text Orca types into a dispatched worker: identity lines, `worker_done`, `heartbeat`, `ask`, `check` commands and, when depth allows, a SUB-DISPATCH section. Built by `buildDispatchPreamble`; the contract defers to it for verb syntax and cadence (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:7`). |
| `worker_done` | The worker's closing report, sent exactly once with `--outcome succeeded\|failed` and `--report-path`; Orca settles the Task on a valid one (WC-5). |
| Heartbeat | A periodic liveness message at the cadence the preamble sets (default 5 minutes, range 1-60, may be off); never a stall proof (WC-10). |
| Progress token | The worker-side liveness evidence the S0, S1, S3 and S6 triggers read (S2 uses a declared window or transcript activity): the current `Command:` line index plus a count that rises only when on-disk progress rises, in a heartbeat's subject or phase text or a `status` body line (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:65`, NORMATIVE). Not an Orca field and not requested by Orca's preamble; with heartbeats off the preamble has no heartbeat command, so no heartbeat carries one (the contract also lets it ride in a `status` body line) (defined once under Table B, Section 5.2). |
| `ask` / `check` / `--wait` / `--ack` | `ask` blocks until the coordinator replies (preamble timeout 600000 ms, resume by message ID); `check` pulls messages; `--wait --types ...` is the coordinator's blocking wait; `--ack` acknowledges a batch (an unacked batch replays). |
| Brief | The text of a Task spec: Guard line, Chain line, gate policy, local-commit line, report directory, worktree, outward actions, `Command:` lines (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:283-327`). |
| Brief guard | The worker-side check of each `Command:` line before running it; four refusal cases with a `guard refused:` body. Instruction only; no executable (L10). |
| Never-as-worker list | `/sc:agent`, `/sc:git`, `/sc:help`, `/sc:issue`, `/sc:pm`, `/sc:spawn`, `/sc:swarm-wizard`, and `/sc:pr-submit` while decision D3 keeps it off workers (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:23`). Advisory (L19). |
| Unattended mode | The behavior a command switches to when it receives a standalone `--unattended` token; selected by the flag only (decision D1); no command detects Orca. 31 commands carry the section (16 in P1, 15 in P2). |
| Report directory | The one directory that holds the worker contract; the command's own directory-valued output flag or `--report-dir`. |
| Worker contract / `return-contract.yaml` | The file `<report-dir>/return-contract.yaml` with `worker_contract_version`, `worker_status`, `command`, `report_path`, `artifacts`, `blocked_question`, `reason`. Distinct from the workflow package contract (`<pkg>/return-contract.yaml`, contract 1.1, `status: success\|partial\|failed`), which code reads (`archive_workspace.py:325-334`). |
| `worker_status` | `succeeded`, `partial`, `failed` or `blocked`; mapped from each command's own status. `blocked` means a person gate (WC-2), except that impact and graph map their flag STOP to `failed` (L30). |
| WC-n | Worker-contract rules WC-2 to WC-15 (no WC-1). WC-13 bars worker fan-out; WC-6 makes every retry a new Task. |
| D1 to D5 | Owner-approved decisions of 2026-11-02 (`--unattended` as the only switch; Ruling authorship; pr-submit off workers; no outward action by a worker; depth one and person-opened coordinator). HISTORICAL records under `.dev/orca-command-refactor/`. |
| P0 to P4 | Phases of the worker-contract rollout plan (`source.md` section 6). P1 and P2 converted 31 commands. The plan puts roadmap, validate-roadmap, validate-tests and save in P3 and implement and post-release in P4 (pr-submit only if D3 (b) is chosen), and says `/sc:load` never gains the flag; none of these seven carries the section in the checkout (Section 5.7, item 9). |
| Ruling | A person's acceptance of an `/sc:implement` halt (`E-HALT-QA`, `E-NO-RULING`), written as a `T<id>: Ruling:` ledger line; a coordinator-written one is "executor-invented approval". The ledger check verifies order, not authorship (L17). |
| S0-S11, L0-L5, deadline W | The contract's liveness states, escalation ladder and silence deadline (`max(10 min, 2 x median heartbeat interval)`, capped at 45 min); starting values, "not a measured one". IronClaude policy, not Orca verdicts. |
| Stall proof | Positive evidence allowing a restart (L4): Orca `exited`, a process exit, or a transcript whose final agent turn sent no `worker_done`; or the person's recorded stop. Nudges, asks, heartbeats and context exhaustion are not stall proofs. |
| Fleet liveness / PTY liveness | Two Orca words at `worker-show`: `projection.liveness.verdict` (`exited`, `unverifiable` with one of ten reasons, `live`; the one printed as "Agent liveness") and `observation.status` (PTY liveness). `unverifiable` is always absence, never proof of death. |
| `agentWait` | Orca's field naming a worker parked on a prompt only a human can answer; null means looked and found none. |
| Nested-worker depth | Orca setting capping delegation depth: the default differs between the development checkout and the installed files (Section 9.3); effective running value UNVERIFIED (L03, L27). |
| Retired scheduler | Orca's legacy automatic Task dispatcher; retired and fenced; Orca does not start ready Tasks itself (gap-P3-G01 S7). |
| Launch-level guard (L1) | `launch-level-guard.sh`: a UserPromptSubmit and PreToolUse hook that restricts a session bound by the claim script's marker; levels `worktree`, `full`, `full-submit` (internal W, WA = W after approval, F, FS; the approval rule is in 10.1b). L2 is a pre-push layer present only on `origin/master`. |
| Class (ESCAPE, TAMPER, ...) | A category the guard assigns to a tool call. The 16 classes, their triggers, the levels that block them and their message ids are the table in 10.1b. ESCAPE: starting or messaging another session, typing into a terminal, or an `orca` word outside the read-only allow-list. TAMPER: a command or edit that reaches the guard's own store, `.claude/settings`, `.claude/hooks`, git config or `disableAllHooks`. IMPL: a Skill call to implement, task, improve, cleanup, spawn or build. TURN: a Cron or Schedule tool call. SUBMIT: `gh pr create`, `edit`, `comment` or `ready`, `gh issue create`, `edit` or `comment`, or the pr-submit Skill. PROTECTED: a canonical `git push` to `master` or `main`. UNKNOWN: a git or gh form that the guard's tables do not list. |
| Launch marker, pending record, nonce | `[sc-launch:v1:<32 hex>]` at the start of a launch prompt; `pending/<nonce>` holds the level word; the guard consumes it once and binds the session. |
| Identity hook | `launch-job-identity.sh`: rewrites a governed session's Bash calls through a supervised runner; Linux only; its `identify` mode has no runtime consumer in the checkout. |
| Fail-open / fail-closed / ceiling | Fail-open: allow when the hook cannot judge. Fail-closed: deny on the script's own failure for a governed session. Ceiling: a documented class of calls the control does not catch. |
| IR-4 | The claim script's enforcement preflight (`enforcement_preflight`, `orca_worktree.py:925`): in the claim process it checks `jq`, `uv`, the Linux process interfaces, the installed guard and identity hook and their registrations, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` and `disableAllHooks`; a failure is warning `W-ENFORCEMENT-ABSENT` and no agent starts (5.4). |
| IR-7 | The design requirement (`docs/designs/sc-issue-launch-level-hook.md`) that the claim refuse to launch when the L2 layer is absent. The HEAD claim script only warns (`W-L2-ABSENT`; `orca_worktree.py:800-805`: "IR-7 ... says refuse; this plan builds no L2 layer"); `origin/master` adds an owned L2 hook with refusal still off. |
| L2 | The pre-push layer that the guard header calls "the real fix" for text-level leaks (`launch-level-guard.sh:710-711`); present on `origin/master` only (`launch-level-pre-push.sh`). L1 is the guard itself. |
| T-SPD-5 | The design's speed pin for the guard's large-command runs. The design says each run takes under 1 s; the test asserts `LARGE_LIMIT_S = 3.5` s on every runner and keeps 1 s as a reported figure (`tests/hooks/test_launch_level_guard.py:1021`; design `:228`). |
| SD-1 | State divergence 1: `worktree ps --agent-wait` is accepted by the Orca build files installed on this host and absent from the Orca development checkout. A divergence between two builds, not a contract defect (8.1b). |
| `LL-*` | Message ids of the launch-level guard: one per blocked class (for example `LL-ESCAPE`, `LL-COMMIT`), `LL-BROKEN` (internal failure on a governed tool call), `LL-NOBIND` (binding failure on the prompt branch), and the notes `LL-STATE` and `LL-TEXT`; the full list is in 10.1b. |
| V8, R2, U1, OB-H1 | Design references: harness fact V8 (a timed-out hook does not block), residual risk R2, ship gate U1 (subagent `session_id`), and the live observation set OB-H1 to OB-H3 and OB-CTX (skipped tests). |
| Claim flow / issue watcher | `/sc:issue --claim` (a Claude session runs `orca_worktree.py`: agent-less worktree create, one launch with a marked prompt) and `superclaude issue-watch once` (one fixed `orca agent launch` in a configured checkout). Both start non-worker sessions, never supervised workers. |
| ccsession / `ccsession-orca` | The session-naming wrapper and the wrapper Orca is told to use as its Claude command; produces the tab title the shim check reads. |
| Shim check | The claim script's advisory check (screen text or tab title, five tries) that the launched agent is behind ccsession; failure is `W-LAUNCH-NOT-CCSESSION`, never blocking. |
| DEFECT (rule C8) | An in-scope capability declared shipped or enabled but unwired at its runtime entrypoint, recorded with a linked remediation task; cannot be relabelled without a cited scope decision (`BUILD-REQUEST.md:54`). |
| Evidence classes and tags | NORMATIVE, CHECKOUT, ORIGIN/MASTER, ORCA-CHECKOUT, INSTALLED-FILES (Orca build files), HOST-INSTALLED (`~/.claude/` copies and host settings), DOCUMENTED, HISTORICAL, DEPLOYED-RUNNING; [CODE-VERIFIED], [CODE-CONTRADICTED], [UNVERIFIED] (see the legend under Document Information). |
| Lineage | The original Task plus every replacement Task; the ladder's caps (L2, L4) count per lineage (`WORKER-CONTRACT.md:146`, restart budget `:159`, resume-note field `:171`). |
| Quarantine | The contract's handling of a `worker_done`, `question`, `escalation`, `handoff` or `merge_ready` message whose Task ID or Dispatch ID differs from the sender's current ones: it is logged and shown to the person and never applied (table A of Section 5.2). |
| Plugin mirror | The tracked tree `plugins/superclaude/` (hooks, commands, core). It is hand-maintained, not written by `make sync-dev`, pinned by a byte-equality test for only five of its 13 mirrored hook scripts, and not equivalent to the source: it carries the `## Unattended mode` section for 2 of the 31 converted commands, and its `core/` has no `WORKER-CONTRACT.md` and no `CLAUDE.md` (Sections 5.3, 5.7, 12.2 and 13.2). |
